## PR M5.4 (1 of 2) — Shift end runs the engine on the phone (Phone track)

- Done: ending a shift now works with no connection. `ShiftController.end` stops the tracker, runs the shared engine over every stored row of the shift (`runLocal` in `shift/endPayload.ts`, replacing `buildEndExtras`) and hands the shift to the outbox (`handOff`): it queues `shift.create` (if the live create was never acknowledged), the start `odometer.put` (if unsent), the end `odometer.put`, then `shift.end` with `ended_at`, `manifest` and `phone_result` (same body shape as before, but built at end time, offline, before any point is uploaded; points the server later refuses show up as `points_missing`, which is what that reason is for). `store/shiftStore.ts` (`ShiftStore` on `app.db`): `begin` at shift start, `finish` writes status `ended`, the phone totals ("Calculated on phone", `calc_source = phone`), trips (auto), stops (only with the `stops` segmenter), simplified route as GeoJSON, flags, manifest and `calc_version` in one transaction; `applyEnd` stores the server's numbers from the `shift.end` answer (`calc_source = server`, `server_id`, `recon_status/reasons`, `on_server`) and, when the server differs, writes the phone's numbers to `conflict_log` (`recon_diff`); `list/get/trips/stops/odometer`; `localDate(ms, instance zone)`. The hand-off is re-runnable after a kill (queue written first, `shift.end` last; `load()` finishes an ended-but-not-handed-off shift without a second `shift.end`). The runner's `canSend` is the end barrier (no PENDING point of the shift); the controller still calls the upload worker's `drainShift` for each queued end before running the outbox. The upload worker now treats `shiftReady` as async and parks a shift whose queued create the server refused (`shiftParked`), so it cannot block other shifts. `createLazyAppDb` opens and migrates `app.db` on first use; `Parts.openAppDb` is required (tests pass Node's SQLite). `Flags` gains `timezone`.
- Tests: 9 new in `localshift.test.ts` (offline end vs the committed fixture result: totals, split, trips, stops; queue order and body; whole_shift stores no stops; engine failure; server answer replaces the label; differing total; parked create; kill before the hand-off; time zones), 1 rewritten in `shift.test.tsx` (queued end survives a restart on the same `app.db`).
- Deviations: over the 400 line cap (about 800 lines with tests): store, controller and the queue hand-off could not be shipped apart. The running shift's create and start odometer still go straight to the server (with the 409 prompt) and enter the outbox only at shift end if unacknowledged. `interruptions` are still not sent. A shift that has zero stored points gets no phone result (totals stay empty until the server answers).
- Not verified: `expo-sqlite` transactions, `Intl` time zones on Hermes, and the whole flow on a device.

Home has a "Shifts" button.

## PR M5.5 — Backlog drain (Phone track)

- Done: `sync/drain.ts` (`SyncDrain`, `services.sync`). One `drain()` serves the running shift first (create, start odometer, its points in batches of at most 100), then every ended shift with work left, oldest `started_at` first: the shift's queued requests (create, odometer readings), then all of its telemetry in batches of 500 (`BACKLOG_BATCH`, one batch per call so the running shift can be looked at in between), then its `shift.end` (the runner's barrier holds the end until no PENDING point of the shift is left). After every backlog batch, if the running shift has points older than the server's upload hint (`uploader.intervalMs`) they go next. A retryable failure returns `offline` with the backoff delay and leaves everything pending; 401/403 return `halted`; a shift whose queued create the server refused is parked (`needs_attention`) and does not hold back later shifts. `progress` / `subscribe` give `pointsSent`, `pointsTotal` (exact below 10,000 pending points, otherwise the sum of the shifts' manifests), `shiftsDone`, `shiftsTotal`, phase. `cancel()` stops between requests (nothing to clean up: state is in the databases); the next `drain()` resumes. `start()/stop()` run it on a timer (the server's hint, or the retry delay); Home now uses `sync.start()` instead of `uploader.start()` and shows "Sending N of M points, shift i of n" with a "Stop sending" button. `UploadWorker.drain` takes `{shift, size, maxBatches, shouldStop}` and a new `cancelled` state.
- Tests: 5 new in `drain.test.ts`: C8 shape with 24 offline shifts of 600 points each (order, create then 500+100 points then end after 600/600, zero duplicates, all `on_server` with the server's numbers, queue empty, progress), cancel then resume without duplicates, a failed batch holds that shift's end and the later shifts until the server is back, a refused create is parked without blocking, running-shift priority between backlog batches.
- Not measured: wall time and bytes per shift (C8 numbers need a device and the VM); the 24 shifts here are synthetic (600 fixes). The drain does not do the settings handshake or the pull (M5.7, M5.8). The Home progress text has no screen test.

## PR S1 — Core additions: vehicle details and profile settings (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `core/0008_profile.sql` rebuilds `vehicles` (the kind CHECK is widened to add `ebike`, `scooter`, `walking`) and adds `make`, `model`, `year`, `plate`, `power`; `settings` gets `display_name`, `region`, `week_start` (default Monday), `time_format` (default 24h) and `default_vehicle_id`. The migration runner understands a first line `-- migrate: foreign_keys=off` (SQLite ignores that PRAGMA inside a transaction, so it is set around the run; `foreign_key_check` must be empty before the commit; the pragma is turned back on in a `finally`). `POST/PATCH /vehicles` take the new optional fields (null clears); `PATCH /settings` takes the five profile fields; `region` is checked against the new `regions` list of the country pack and a country change clears a region the new country lacks; `default_vehicle_id` must be an active vehicle and archiving the default clears it. Eligibility: `bicycle`, `ebike`, `scooter` and `walking` have no deduction value in any pack (`notAutomobile` in `countries.ts`). OpenAPI and generated types updated.
- Tests: 7 new in `profile.test.ts` (details and null, new kinds and bad values, profile defaults and bounds, region rules, default vehicle rules, no deduction for non-automobiles, the table rebuild keeps shifts and readings and re-enables foreign keys); 1 new in `countries.test.ts`; the vehicle shape expectation in `vehicles.test.ts` updated.
- Assumptions: none beyond `18` (no owner question touches S1). Not done: the web screens (S1w), the phone reading the new fields through `sync` (the settings and vehicle entities already carry them).

## PR M5.7 — Phone pull, tombstones, conflict log, driver-visible diff (Phone track)

- Done: `sync/pull.ts` (`ChangePuller`, `services.puller`). `GET /sync/changes` pages of 200 from the stored cursor (`sync_state.changes_cursor`); each page and its cursor are written in one transaction. Applies: `vehicle` (table), `shift` (insert if unknown; a shift whose `shift.create` or `shift.end` is still queued is not touched, only `server_id` is noted; otherwise the server's fields, totals and `recon_*` replace the phone's, `calc_source = server`, and a changed total is logged as `recon_diff`), `trip` (upsert by `trip_key`, shift found from the key or `server_id`), `odometer` (shift readings, `server_id` added to the table by migration 2), `override` (an `orphaned_override` is logged as "One of your edits could not be applied", never dropped), `settings` (stored through `Pairing.storeSettings`, so the flags follow). Deletes: shift purge, trip and odometer tombstones remove the local copy; unsent local edits for them go to `conflict_log` (`deleted_on_server`) and are removed from the outbox, never re-sent. Last-writer-wins against a queued edit: strictly newer phone edit time keeps the phone value (still going up); older or equal loses, is logged (`superseded_by_server`) and the queued item is dropped. `410 cursor_expired` restarts from cursor 0 and deletes nothing by itself. With `modules.sync` off (`Flags.sync`) nothing is pulled. The drain calls the pull when it finishes (forced if it sent something); otherwise at most every 5 minutes. Shift detail shows unseen changes ("Server confirmed 4.9 km. Your phone showed 5.0 km (0.1 km difference). Reason: ...") and a tap dismisses (`seen = 1`).
- Tests: 8 new in `pull.test.ts` (insert/paging/cursor/settings, recompute diff text and dismissal, LWW older/tie/newer, queued end not overwritten, purge with unsent edit, trip tombstone and orphan, 410, off/offline/revoked/interval).
- Limits: the pull trusts the feed's current-state design; a local trip with a key the server never logged is not removed when only the shift changed (the server's tombstones cover the label-recompute case). The queued-edit kinds `shift.patch`, `trip.override`, `trip.manual` are read by the pull but nothing on the phone creates them until M5.9. No separate "all changes" list screen (only the per-shift notices). Not run against the real server or on a device.

## PR M5.8 — Status markers, global banner, reminders, low storage, wrong server, restore generation (Phone track)

- Done: `sync/status.ts` (pure): `markerFor` (hollow "On phone only", spinner, check, check with a differs badge, warning), `reminderFor` (nothing before 3 days, then once a day, stronger wording from 7 days, constants), `storageState` (200 MB / 50 MB thresholds, unreadable = unknown), `buildStatus` (green / yellow / red and the sentence for revoked, bad credential, wrong server, restored server, low or critical storage, parked items, offline). `sync/connection.ts` (`ConnectionMonitor`): the API client now reports every answer's headers and can be blocked; a different `X-MileWage-Instance` than at pairing blocks every request (retryable error, so queued data stays and nothing is sent) until the driver chooses "Check the address"; an answer without the headers is not a wrong server; the drain starts with `GET /settings` so a wrong server shows before any data is sent. A higher `X-MileWage-Restore-Gen` (stored in `sync_state.restore_gen`) sets every `on_server` shift back to `local`, shows the restored-server sentence and blocks eviction of acknowledged points (`evictBlocked`). Home has `SyncBanner` ("Not yet on server: N shifts · P points · oldest D days ago", the red sentences, the reminder text, Sync now, Check the address / Re-pair for a wrong server); the shift list shows the marker label per row. `OutboxRunner.haltedState` feeds the banner.
- Tests: 8 new in `status.test.tsx` (markers, reminder schedule incl. daily repeat and strong wording, storage, every colour/sentence, wrong server stops all requests and clears, no-header answer, restore generation, banner with revoked device).
- NOT done / unverified: (1) re-sending after a restore: the points the phone still holds are not set back to PENDING because the tracker interface has no such method (native change, not in this track); shifts are only marked "on phone only" and eviction is blocked. (2) The reminder is shown in the banner only; no operating-system notification adapter exists (no notifications package installed, no new permission added). (3) Free-storage reading: `Parts.freeBytes` is an injected port, NOT wired in production (no file-system package installed), so the low-storage banner never shows on a device yet; the `SQLITE_FULL` loud stop is native work. (4) The sync marker is text only (no icons). (5) Unpair blocking while data is unsynced (15 section 9.1) is not built. (6) The banner uses the device clock for "oldest D days ago". Nothing ran on a device.

## PR S3 — Module `earnings` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `earnings/0001_shift_earnings.sql` (one row per shift and platform, `ON DELETE CASCADE`); `GET /shifts/{ref}/earnings` (typed rows, the label breakdown per platform and the effective figure per platform), `PUT .../earnings/{platform}` (idempotent, whole-row replace, shift must be ended or finalized else `409 shift_not_ended`, platform checked against the platforms module when it is on) and `DELETE` (idempotent, returns the listing). **Effective earnings** (`createEarningsRepository().effective`): a typed row is the figure of its (shift, platform); otherwise that platform's label sum; labels with no platform count under `other`; none means `null`. `GET /summary` earnings, tips and hourly figures now use it (new optional `platform` query that filters earnings only and adds `platform_filter: "earnings_only"`); turning `stops` off no longer nulls earnings when `earnings` is on. The shift-level CSV takes Gross_Pay, Tips, Platform and Deliveries from the effective rows and gets a trailing `Bonus` column. Event `earnings.changed`.

It is a setting: `comma.earnings_override` = `"replace"` or `"sum"` in `config/settings.json` (or `MILEWAGE_EARNINGS_OVERRIDE`), read once into `config.comma` (`comma-options.ts`, which also holds the tax-countries and challenge-target settings that later PRs use). Recorded in `18` section 17.
- Tests: 8 in `earnings.test.ts` (replace and restore, another platform adds, platform filter, stops off and nothing-entered null, rules and validation, CSV, `sum` mode, config parsing, module off); the module matrix covers the off side; the shift CSV header in `exports.test.ts` gains `Bonus`.

## PR M5.9 — Phone trip overrides, manual trips, offline odometer and notes edits (Phone track)

- Done: `store/edits.ts` (`PhoneEdits`, `services.edits`). `editTrip` (kind and purpose of an auto trip: stored at once with `edited_at`, queued as one coalesced `trip.override`; a manual trip is queued as `trip.manual`), `addManualTrip` (trip key = lower-case UUID, queued as `PUT /shifts/{ref}/trips/{uuid}`), `setOdometer` (shift start/end reading, queued `odometer.put`, coalesced), `setNotes` (`shift.patch` with `updated_at`). Edit times are `now + gnssOffsetMs` when the tracker has a fresh fix (15 section 10). The shift's active/deadhead/personal split is moved by the edited distance only (the engine's own total is not recomputed; a new manual trip adds to the total); the server's numbers replace them on the next pull. Refused: unknown trip, over-long text, bad manual trip, a finalized shift; an auto trip's distance is never written. Last-writer-wins and the audit trail on the phone side are the pull rules of M5.7 (older or tied edit loses and is logged, strictly newer keeps going up); the server side is the existing M5.9 server PR. Shift detail: "change to X" per trip (cycles active, deadhead, personal), "Add trip" (distance, kind active), "Save end odometer"; each flushes the queue.
- Tests: 6 in `edits.test.tsx` (reclassify + coalesce + one PUT with the GNSS-corrected time, split moves, refusals incl. finalized and distance untouched, manual trip total/key/PUT, odometer and notes coalescing and send, an older queued edit losing to the server on the pull with a log row, one tap on the detail screen).
- Limits: no delete of a manual trip on the phone (web only, 15 section 8.4); the add-trip form has no kind or time choice (kind active, times = shift end); the override surviving a recompute is the server's job (tested there), the phone only shows the server's rows after the pull; editing a running shift is not offered (only ended shifts). Not run on a device.

## PR M6.5 — Read-only stops and the estimated split honour stops_enabled (Phone track)

- Done: shift detail shows the active/deadhead split (marked "(estimated)"), the detected stops (time, minutes, confidence, "looks like a pickup/dropoff", all read only, headed "Stops (estimated, labelled on the web)") and the "(estimated)" trip marker only when the cached settings have `stops_enabled` on AND the `stops` module on AND the shift itself was recorded with the `stops` segmenter. With the setting or module off, or for a shift recorded while it was off, only the single total is shown (trips list without active/deadhead words, except personal and manual trips). The stored data of an earlier shift is not touched by the setting.
- Tests: 4 in `stops.screen.test.tsx` (flag matrix: on; setting off after recording; module off; shift recorded with the setting off stays one total after it is turned on).
- Limits: the stops shown are the phone's own run of the engine; the phone has no stop labels (web only) and the change feed carries no stops, so after a label changes the server's trips the phone's stop list is not refreshed. No stop map markers on the route. Not run on a device.


## PR (roadmap M5/M6.5 extra) — Phone home summary, week and month from local data (Phone track)

- Done: `store/summary.ts` (`summarize`, pure) and `HomeSummary` on the home screen: this week (Monday start, instance time zone) and this month: distance in the instance unit and the deduction value, with "(includes N shifts not yet on the server)". Same meaning as the web dashboard and `GET /summary`: only ended or finalized shifts count, the value is each shift's country-pack value (`countryPacks` from `@milewage/shared`, vehicle kind from the cached `vehicles` table, default car) with the business metres already driven in the same rate period, summed; "not available" when the country has no rate for the dates (never zero). It reads only `app.db` and the cached settings, so it works offline.
- Tests: 4 in `summary.test.tsx` (week start, week/month sums with active/empty shifts excluded and unsynced counted, pack values, Canada's tier across shifts, unknown country, home screen with a shift not yet sent).
- Limits: the phone holds only the history it has seen, so a tiered rate (Canada 5,000 km, GB 10,000 miles) can be off when older shifts of the year are missing; earnings and net hourly figures are not shown (stop labels are web only); the vehicle kind is `car` until a vehicle has been pulled (`vehicles` table filled by the change feed only). The pack rates are the unverified ones of M9.2. Not run on a device.

## PR S4 — Module `expenses` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `expenses/0001_expenses.sql` (18 section 5.2 verbatim); the 20 Comma categories as data in `packages/shared/src/expense-categories.ts` (50 % defaults for phone, data plan and meals; `out_of_pocket` not deductible; fuel, maintenance and insurance `covered_by_standard_rate`); routes `GET/POST /expenses` (filters, cursor pages, `templates=true` lists reminders), `GET/PATCH/DELETE /expenses/{id}`, `GET /expenses/totals?year=`, `/expenses/merchants?q=`, `/expenses/due`, `/expenses/categories`, `POST /expenses/{id}/occurrences`, `PUT /expenses/{id}/snooze`. Deductible amount and `double_counts` are computed at read time (round half up; the flag needs a vehicle, a covered category, an automobile kind and the standard-mileage method). The method comes from a new core seam `deductionMethodFor(db, user, vehicle, year)` (`deduction-method.ts`, default standard mileage); the `tax` module installs its resolver in S6 so `expenses` never imports `tax`. Recurring is a reminder: nothing is created by a job; confirming creates one occurrence and moves `next_due` one step (month ends stay month ends); deleting a template keeps its occurrences. Event `expense.changed`. `has_receipt` is already in the shape (false until S5).
- Tests: 12 in `expenses.test.ts` (defaults and rounding, validation, patch/delete, filters and cursors, totals, merchants, due/confirm/advance, snooze and delete-keeps-occurrences, interval maths, double counting and the seam, module off); the module matrix covers the 404 side of all 11 operations.
- Deviations: the `platform=` filter on `listExpenses` named in 18 section 4.2 is **not** built: an expense has no platform, so there is nothing to filter. Totals treat double-counted rows as spend but not as deductible (reported separately as `double_counts_cents`).

## PR S5 — Module `receipts`; backup and restore carry the receipt files (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `receipts/0001_expense_receipts.sql`; `PUT/GET/DELETE /expenses/{id}/receipt` (web session only; JPEG, PNG, WebP or PDF; 10 MB per file, 2 GiB in total; the declared type must match the file's first bytes; a JPEG loses its GPS data: the GPS directory of the Exif block is emptied and its values zeroed, orientation and image data are kept, an unreadable Exif block or any XMP block is dropped whole; stored under a server-made name in `<data>/receipts/` through a `.part` file and rename). Writes get the existing `507` while the disk state blocks writes (`hooks.ts`). Deleting an expense deletes its file. Module `receipts` depends on `expenses`.
- **Backup and restore (10):** the backup engine copies every receipt file into the verified backup copy as a `receipt_blobs` table (so the single encrypted file, the offsite download and the restore upload stay one file). Restore checks each embedded file against the sha256 of its `expense_receipts` row before anything is touched (a mismatch is a 422 and nothing changes), then writes the files to `<data>/receipts/` after the swap and removes files no restored row refers to. A row whose file was missing at backup time is restored without a file and logged.
- Tests: 8 in `receipts.test.ts` (GPS removal on a hand-built Exif JPEG, unreadable Exif, upload and download with matching hash, replace and delete leave one file, expense delete removes the file, refusals incl. 415/422/413/404/401, 507 on a nearly full disk, backup then restore on a fresh host with identical bytes and the scratch table gone, module off with expenses on); the matrix covers the off side.
- Not verified: JPEGs from real phones (only a hand-built big-endian Exif block was used; little-endian files go through the same code but are untested); a receipt directory on a full disk during restore; a backup of thousands of receipts (memory: one file at a time, but the database copy grows by their size, so the 2x-database-size disk rule of 10 section 11.3 now includes the receipts).
- Not done: the web upload control (S5w), a phone camera upload (device scope has no access, by design), the text of `10` itself (the amendment is recorded here and in `18` section 17).

With the module off there is no column and no call. The footer says photos lose their location data.

## PR S6 — Module `tax`: CA estimate, tax years, Tax Jar, vehicle tax years (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `tax/0001_tax.sql` (`tax_years`, `tax_jar_entries`, `vehicle_tax_years`); tax pack in `packages/shared/src/tax.ts` (`caTaxPack`, `estimateTax`, registry) and `tax-report.ts`; routes `GET/PUT /tax/{year}`, `GET/POST /tax/{year}/jar`, `DELETE /tax/jar/{id}`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /vehicles/{id}/tax-years/{year}`. The estimate follows 18 section 7.2: taxable income = effective earnings (typed totals, else labels) less deductible expenses (not the ones a standard rate covers, business % applied) less the mileage deduction of standard-mileage vehicles; self-employed CPP and CPP2 (two bands, exemption); income tax = the driver's set-aside rate; sales tax only when registered, from a typed `collected_cents` less the input tax credits of the deductible expenses; quarterly instalment = a quarter of income tax plus CPP; deadlines (instalments 15 Mar/Jun/Sep/Dec, balance 30 Apr and filing 15 Jun of the next year); T4A alert above CAD 500 per platform; jar balance against a target of earnings times the set-aside rate. No earnings entered means no estimate (`income` and `estimate` are null, never 0). The mileage deduction stays a deduction line, never cash.
- **Core seam:** the module installs its resolver for `deductionMethodFor` (added in S4), so `actual_expenses` for a vehicle and year makes `deduction_cents` null in shifts, the summary and the exports, and makes its fuel, maintenance and insurance count as expenses (no `double_counts`). Without the module the default stays standard mileage, exactly today's behaviour. `expenses` and the summary never import `tax`.
- **Assumptions (owner questions unanswered), recorded in `18` section 17:** only CA ships (`comma.tax_countries` defaults to `["CA"]`; US and GB have no pack, so they answer `supported: false`, and so does CA if the setting removes it); `caTaxPack.verified = false`, `unverified: true` in every response and a notice "Unverified rates" for the UI.
- **UNVERIFIED (important):** every CPP/CPP2 figure for 2025 and 2026 (ceilings 71 300/81 200 and 74 600/85 000 dollars, exemption 3 500, rates 11.90 % and 8.00 % self-employed), the sales-tax rates by province, the CAD 500 slip threshold, the 30 April balance date and the instalment rule were written from memory, not compared with the CRA pages. QPP (Quebec) is not in the pack: for region QC the contribution is left out with a note.
- Deviations: the rate override fields of `vehicle_tax_years` (`rate1_milli`, `rate2_milli`, `threshold_m`) are stored and returned but **not applied** to the deduction (the pack rate rules, as in `07` D7); the instalment is a flat quarter of the estimate (the CRA's own instalment options are not modelled).
- Tests: 10 in `tax.test.ts` (every estimate line with hand-checked figures, inputs and sales tax, no-earnings null, unsupported country and the config switch, method switch through shifts, summary and expenses, jar, module off, pack arithmetic incl. QC and missing year); the matrix covers the 404 side of the 8 operations. Exporters `expenses` and `tax_summary` follow in S6b.


## PR S6b — Exporters `expenses` and `tax_summary` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: two exporters registered like the others (`exporters/reports.ts`), each with its own switch (`modules.export_expenses`, `modules.export_tax_summary`, default on; via `config/settings.json` or `MILEWAGE_MODULE_EXPORT_<FORMAT>`) and each requiring its module (`expenses`, `tax`), so they are listed in `modules.exporters` only when they exist. They are "standalone" exporters: `GET /exports/{format}` selects no shifts for them and accepts `year`, `category` and `format` besides `from`/`to`. **`expenses`** (CSV): `Date, Category, Merchant, Amount, Deductible, Business_Use_Pct, Deductible_Amount, Tax_Credit, Vehicle, Shift_Date, Receipt, Recurring, Notes` plus a trailing `Covered_By_Mileage_Rate`; a covered cost shows 0.00 deductible, as in the tax estimate; default range is the current year; text cells get the formula guard. **`tax_summary`** (CSV or `format=json`): the section 7.2 lines with their inputs (earnings by platform, deductible expenses by category, mileage deduction per vehicle with its method, pension contribution, set-aside, instalment, sales tax, jar balance, deadlines), the "estimate, not tax advice" line and, while the pack is unverified, the "Unverified rates" line; for an unsupported country only the notice.
- Web: the Exports screen lists the two formats (labels and hints) and runs them with the chosen period; `tax_summary` takes the year of the period's start.
- Tests: 6 in `exports-reports.test.ts` (columns, rounding, quoting and formula guard, default year, category filter, bad ranges, tax CSV lines hand-checked, JSON, unsupported country, per-format switch and module requirement); `exports.test.ts` and `settings.test.ts` expectations of the format list updated.
- Not done: a "Year-in-Review" image (not carried, 18 section 3.3); receipt files in the export (not wanted by the spec); the print view is the browser's print of the PDF page, which still does not exist (M9.4).

## PR S7b — Module `analytics` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: `GET /analytics?from&to&group&bucket&platform` (web session). No table. One group per call: `trends` (day, week or month buckets with earnings, hours, distance, $/h; earnings null where none were entered; totals of the previous period of the same length), `rhythm` (shifts and hours by weekday, shifts by start hour in the owner's zone, average shift length), `sources` (earnings, tips and share per platform), `outlook` (straight-line projection from the days elapsed; none under 3 shifts or without earnings, with the reason), `efficiency` (active and deadhead share, flagged `estimated`, only from stop-based shifts; the spread of $/h across shifts), `order_economics` (pay per order, per km and per mile, orders per hour; orders are the typed count or labelled drop-offs; `null` when no order count exists), `cards` (gross, expenses and burn ratio, net take-home = gross less deductible expenses less the income-tax set-aside, set-aside, $/h by shift time and by typed online minutes, total hours). Cards for a module that is off (expenses, tax) are left out, not zero; the `platform` filter is earnings-only and says so. Defaults: the last 30 days, at most two years.
- Deviation: "tax set-aside" on the cards is the income-tax part (set-aside rate times gross) only; the pension contribution is not subtracted in net take-home (the full estimate is the Tax screen's).
- Tests: 8 in `analytics.test.ts` (every card value hand-checked; trends buckets and previous period; rhythm, sources, outlook, efficiency, order economics; null honesty and the platform filter; validation; cards without expenses and tax; money lines null without earnings; module off); the matrix covers the 404 side.

## PR S8 — Module `maintenance` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `maintenance/0001_maintenance.sql`; `GET/POST /vehicles/{id}/maintenance`, `PATCH/DELETE /maintenance/{id}` (web session). Kinds: oil change, tires, brakes, other (fuel and washes are expense categories, as in 18 section 6). With `expenses` on and a cost, `record_expense` (default true) creates a `maintenance` expense for the vehicle on the service date and links it; the entry then stores no cost and reads it from the expense (a change of cost or date edits the expense), so a cost is one fact in one place. The response carries `double_counts` (the linked expense is covered by the vehicle's standard mileage rate). Deleting an entry keeps its expense (a tax record). Without `expenses` the cost is stored on the entry.
- Tests: 6 in `maintenance.test.ts` (expense creation and link, cost read from the expense, edits flow to it, no cost, `record_expense: false`, actual-expense vehicle not flagged, ordering and delete-keeps-expense, validation and ownership, expenses off, module off); the matrix covers the 404 side.
- Not done: the Maintenance tab on the vehicle screen (S8w); reminders from odometer or date (not carried, 18 section 3.3).

## PR S8b — Module `import` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: `POST /imports/{kind}/preview` and `POST /imports/{kind}` for `kind` = `expenses` or `shifts` (web session; the body is the raw CSV, `text/csv` or `text/plain`, gzip allowed, 5 MB; the confirmed column mapping is the `mapping` query parameter, a JSON object of field to column name, otherwise guessed from the header). No vendor format is read (decision 1.8 stands). The preview reports the columns, the mapping, counts and each of the first 200 rows as `ok`, `duplicate` or `error` with a reason, and writes nothing. The commit writes the ok rows in one transaction and is idempotent: a repeat of the same file imports zero rows. Duplicates: expenses by (date, amount, merchant), shifts by (vehicle, start instant, distance). **Expenses:** date, category (slug or label, unknown becomes `other`), amount, merchant, deductible, business %, notes. **Shifts:** date, start time, end time or duration, distance in the owner's unit, vehicle (by name, else the default vehicle, else the only active one), platform, gross, tips, bonus, notes. An imported shift is `finalized`, `whole_shift`, has one manual trip ("Imported") carrying the typed distance, an `imported` info flag, and (earnings module on, amounts given) a `shift_earnings` row with `source = 'import'`. Local times are converted with the owner's zone (daylight saving handled). **Every shift-level CSV export gets a trailing `Imported` column** (the trip-level file already says `manual` and "Imported"), so a tax office can tell typed from tracked distance.
- Tests: 10 in `imports.test.ts` (CSV reader, amounts and zone arithmetic; expenses preview and its zero writes; commit once and again; confirmed mapping and refusals; gzip and text/plain; shift preview reasons; commit shapes: shift, trip, flag, earnings, an overnight row; the Imported column and repeat; expenses module off; import off); the matrix covers the 404 side; the shift CSV header test gains `Imported`.
- Deviations: dates must be `YYYY-MM-DD` and times 24-hour `HH:MM` (ambiguous `MM/DD/YYYY` is refused rather than guessed); expense rows cannot carry a vehicle or tax credit; one error row does not stop the others.
- Not done: the wizard (S8bw), reading files with another delimiter or encoding.

The text under it says dates are YYYY-MM-DD and imported shifts are marked in every export. Not a route, so `screens.tsx` and the Caddyfile are unchanged.

## PR S9 — Module `notifications` (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `notifications/0001_notifications.sql` (`notification_prefs`, `notifications` with a unique `(user_id, dedupe_key)`); `GET /notifications` (web or device read; `unread`, `limit`, `cursor`; returns `unread_count`), `PATCH /notifications/{id}` (read or unread), `DELETE /notifications/{id}`, `GET/PATCH /notifications/prefs` (the six toggles of 18 section 11; the phone reads `shift_reminders` and `sync_reminder` and fires those itself). Rows carry a kind, a message id (`body_key`) and a `ref` (a date or an id from the dedupe key): no free text and no amounts. A switched-off toggle stops its kinds being added (goals toggle: goal met, badge, challenge, level up; streak: streak freeze and risk; tax deadlines; backup). Triggers: **tax deadline** (a pack deadline within 10 days; tax module on and the country enabled), **recurring expense due**, **goal met** (once per goal and period), **backup overdue** (no good backup for 36 hours after setup was confirmed), all evaluated when the inbox is read; **backup failed** from the existing `backup.finished` event, once per local day. A module that is off emits nothing. Event `notification.created`.
- **Deviations:** (1) no timer at all: the spec says the existing daily housekeeping job, but that job belongs to diagnostics (and is off with it); evaluating on read needs no timer and the inbox only matters while someone looks at it (the phone keeps its own local reminders). A consequence: nothing is added while nobody opens the web. (2) `DELETE` is a soft dismiss (`dismissed_at`, a column the spec table lacks) so that the same condition does not bring a dismissed row back on the next read. (3) Weekly digest, maintenance and insurance notices are not built (18 section 3.3).
- Tests: 7 in `notifications.test.ts` (deadline once and only within 10 days; recurring and goal triggers; backup failure once a day and overdue; modules off emit nothing; toggles gate rows, validation and device scope; read, unread filter and count, dismissal staying dismissed, cursor pages; module off); the test harness now exposes the event bus.
- Not done: the bell and list on the web (S9w); badge, challenge, level-up and streak rows come from `progress` (S10).

With the module off there is no screen, no count, no card and no call.

## PR S10 — Module `schedule`, default OFF (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `schedule/0001_schedule.sql` (templates, entries, off days; unique `(user, date, start_time)`); `GET /schedule?from&to` (web or device read; window up to a year), `POST/PATCH/DELETE /schedule/entries`, `POST/PATCH/DELETE /schedule/templates`, `POST /schedule/expand`, `PUT/DELETE /schedule/off-days/{date}`. Templates use the weekday bitmask of 18 section 9 (bit 0 Monday). **Nothing is generated by itself:** `expand` creates entries for a window from the active templates, skipping off days and entries that already exist, and a second run creates none. A planned day that has a worked shift is linked to it by date when the window is read (never required). Platform (checked against `platforms` when on) and vehicle are optional. The module is `defaultEnabled: false` (**assumption 1** in `18` section 17, as in Comma): `modules.schedule` in `config/settings.json` or `MILEWAGE_MODULE_SCHEDULE=1` turns it on.
- Tests: 8 in `schedule.test.ts` (default is off and how to turn it on; module off; entry CRUD and the duplicate start; validation of times, dates, references and windows; device read but no write; shift link by date; expand with off days and existing entries, twice, inactive; template delete keeps entries; weekday bits); the matrix covers the 404 side of all 10 operations.

## PR S10w — Web: Schedule screen (Comma-parity track)

The page says a plan is not a record.

## PR S10b — Module `progress`, default OFF (Comma-parity track, server)

- Done: migration `progress/0001_progress.sql` (`badge_unlocks`, `progress_state`, as in 18 section 8.2); 24 badges as data in `packages/shared/src/badges.ts`; `GET /progress` (web) evaluates and returns XP, level, streak with freezes and "at risk", this week's challenges and the badge grid. It records only stable facts (new badge unlocks, freezes granted and used, completed challenges as `challenge:<id>:<week start>` rows) and derives the rest every time; a second call records nothing new. Depends on `goals` and `earnings`; `defaultEnabled: false` (**assumption 1**; switch on with `modules.progress` or `MILEWAGE_MODULE_PROGRESS=1`). When `notifications` is on, unlocks, level-ups, freezes used, completed challenges and a streak at risk reach the inbox (once each by dedupe key; the toggles apply).
- **Rules (mine where 18 is silent, all recorded here):** XP = 10 per ended shift, +15 for a shift of 4 hours or more (the "duration target" is **assumed to be 4 hours**), +30 for a shift longer in distance than every earlier one (the "record"), +40 per badge, +60 per completed weekly challenge; 100 XP a level; each level gained grants one freeze (at most 3). **Goals-met XP (+20) is not included:** it needs the history of past periods, which nothing stores. A freeze bridges one missed day in the streak and is used once (its date is remembered). Earnings, tips and deliveries badges, and the earn challenge, are evaluated only from entered or labelled figures and stay locked otherwise (`value: null`). "Deliveries" are labelled drop-off stops; the challenge is absent while nothing is labelled. Weekly challenges: earn (target `comma.challenge_target_cents`, default 50 000 minor units = CAD 500, **assumption 6**), 20 labelled drop-offs, work 5 different days. Badge thresholds are this project's own, not Comma's. The earnings badges count the settings currency's major units whatever the currency.
- Tests: 9 in `progress.test.ts` (default off and how to enable; zero state with 24 locked badges and null challenge; XP by the table for three growing shifts with level, freezes, badges and "at risk"; idempotence; typed earnings unlock a badge and complete a challenge with the target from configuration; a freeze bridges one gap once; no freeze, the streak stops; the drop-offs challenge appears only when labelled; inbox rows; dependency refusal and module off); the matrix covers the 404 side.
- Not done: the web screen (S10bw); the celebration on unlock; the weekly XP history.

- Tests: 2 in `progress.test.tsx`; navigation list and Caddyfile test updated.

## PR J1a — Countries: places, dated parameters, slots and resolver (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J1a-countries-core` (pushed, not merged). J1 is split in two (J1a core, J1b CA, US and GB moved in) to stay under 400 lines.
- Done: `packages/shared/src/countries/` with the contract (`types.ts`: `CountryModule`, `Place`, `ResolvedPlace`, `Dated`), dated parameters in the OpenFisca shape (`dated.ts`: `valueAt`, `datedProblems`; a final `null` ends a parameter), data slots (`slots/`: `salesTax` as a list where combinable components add to the parent and others replace it, with `compound` components charged on top; `pension` where the nearest level wins and `null` means "not estimated here"; each slot has its own check), the resolver (`resolve.ts`: country → region → city; a region stored as `ON` is read as `CA-ON`; an unknown place gets national data only; verified only if every level that supplied data is) and `countryName()` from `Intl.DisplayNames`. `VehicleKind`, `notAutomobile` and `DeductionInput` move into the folder; `countries.ts` re-exports them, so no consumer changes.
- Standards checked (new, beyond plan 21 section 2.4):
  - OpenFisca ends a parameter with `value: null` at a date (it replaced the old XML `<END>` tag); `None` is an allowed value. We use the same: a final `null`. [OpenFisca parameters](https://openfisca.org/doc/coding-the-legislation/legislation_parameters.html), [openfisca-core parameter types](https://openfisca.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/openfisca_core/parameters.html).
  - libaddressinput level words, read from Google's address data: `state_name_type` (`state` for US, none for CA so the default `province`, `area` HK, `county` IE, `prefecture` JP, `do_si` KR) and `locality_name_type` (`post_town` GB, `district` HK). GB has no `state_name_type`, so `nation` is our own word for its four nations. [Address metadata](https://github.com/google/libaddressinput/wiki/AddressValidationMetadata), [data/US](https://chromium-i18n.appspot.com/ssl-address/data/US).
  - `Intl.DisplayNames` is listed by FormatJS as not built into Hermes, so `countryName()` falls back to the ISO code instead of crashing on the phone. [FormatJS on Hermes](https://formatjs.github.io/docs/guides/react-native-hermes).
- Tests: 8 in `packages/shared/test/country-core.test.ts` (value at a date and the end; parameter checks; Medusa add or replace through region and city; compound total; pension null; unknown place, loop and verified; slot checks; names and the fallback). Shared package 42 passed.
- Not done: the registry and the CA, US and GB modules (J1b).

## PR J1b — Countries: CA, US and GB as country modules (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J1b-countries-ca-us-gb` (pushed, not merged), on J1a.
- Done: `countries/ca`, `countries/us`, `countries/gb` as `CountryModule`s with the same figures as the old packs: mileage rates as dated parameters (CA 2024–2026 ending `2027-01-01`; US eras ending `2027-01-01`; GB cars and motorcycles ending `2027-04-06`), rates by vehicle class (car, van, motorcycle; no rate for bicycles, e-bikes, scooters or walking), the rate period as `yearStarts` (`01-01`, GB `04-06`). Region names: 13 provinces and territories, 50 states and DC, 4 nations. CA data slots: GST 5 % nationally, HST replacing it in ON, NB, NL, NS, PE, CPP nationally and `pension: null` in QC, all copied from the tax pack. `mileage.ts` (`mileageDeduction`, `periodStart`, `regionsOf`) and `registry.ts` (`createCountryModules`, `countryModules`). The old packs and every consumer are untouched; J1c points them at the modules.
- **Rounding kept:** tiered rates (CA, GB cars) round twice, to 1/100 cent and then to a cent; flat rates (US, GB motorcycles) round once. This is today's behaviour, now a property of the rate shape (plan 21 section 8). A test shows a distance where rounding once would give 7 cents and today gives 8.
- **Found, not changed:** the CRA GST/HST calculator page says Nova Scotia's HST is 14 % from 1 April 2025; the pack has 15 %. Kept at 15 % with a note on `CA-NS` (figures change only when a person checks them). [CRA GST/HST calculator](https://www.canada.ca/en/revenue-agency/services/tax/businesses/topics/gst-hst-businesses/charge-collect-which-rate/calculator.html).
- Standards checked: region names as in Google's address data (libaddressinput `sub_names` for CA and US; CC-BY 4.0; US list without territories and military codes, as before). [data/CA](https://chromium-i18n.appspot.com/ssl-address/data/CA). Units of a dated rate are written like OpenFisca's `unit` metadata (`cents/km`, `pence/mi`).
- Tests: 6 in `packages/shared/test/country-modules.test.ts`: each module gives exactly the old pack's deduction and period start over 12 dates (each year edge, 6 April, 1 July), 7 vehicle kinds, 10 distances and 5 earlier distances (12,600 cases), plus the same regions, unit, sources and `verified`; the double rounding; CA sales tax and pension match the tax pack for all 13 regions; the registry. Shared package 48 passed.
- Not done: the old packs as aliases, deleting `countries.ts`, conformance tests (J1c).

- Branch `pr-J2-country-platforms` (pushed, not merged), on J1c.
- Done: a `platforms` data slot (`countries/slots/platforms.ts`): each level adds to the list above it, and an offer with `available: false` takes one away (a region where an app does not run). Slugs must exist in the shared list (slot check). CA, US and GB list the same platforms as before, in the same order (`other` in each). Shared `platforms.ts` keeps only slug, label and vocabulary; the `countries` tags are gone. `GET /platforms` reads the list for the driver's country and region from the registry, then the driver's own rows (the duplicate filter is now just the existing de-duplication).
- Standards checked: Shopify Markets assigns catalogs to markets and a child market inherits the parent's products, with restriction done on the parent: the same "inherit and add" model; our `available: false` is the gap (a region can take one away without editing the country). [Shopify Markets catalogs](https://help.shopify.com/manual/markets-new/catalogs), [overview](https://help.shopify.com/en/manual/markets/getting-started/overview). Medusa's `combinable` (plan 21 section 2.4) does not fit here, because platforms always combine.
- Tests: 3 in `packages/shared/test/country-platforms.test.ts` (each country's list and order; a region adds or removes; every shared platform is offered somewhere and has no country tag) and 1 in `apps/server/test/platforms.test.ts` (GB with a region, then own platforms; US). The Canadian listing test passes unchanged.
- Not done: nothing for this step. Region-specific apps are not added (no checked data).

## PR J3a — Countries: tax pack as a per-country strategy with lines (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J3a-tax-strategy` (pushed, not merged), on J2. J3 is split in two (J3a the shared strategy, J3b the API, server and web switch) to stay under 400 lines.
- Done: `countries/tax.ts` with the generic contract (`TaxPack` with `estimate(input, place)` and `deadlines(year, place)`, `slip`, `checkedAgainst`; `TaxEstimate` with `lines` of `{ key, label, cents, note }`; `estimateTax(country, input)` resolves the driver's region first). `countries/ca/tax.ts`: the CA strategy reads the resolved place: the `pension` slot (now carrying the CPP figures as dated parameters for 2025 and 2026, ended `2027-01-01`; QC sets it to `null`) and the `salesTax` slot (GST, or HST where it replaces it), instead of `ownPensionPlan` and `salesTaxBp`. `caCountry.tax` is set. The old `tax.ts` still serves every consumer until J3b; its names are not re-exported from the countries folder yet, to avoid a clash.
- Before switching, the old pack wrote out its estimate for 1,680 inputs (5 years, 7 regions including none and an unknown one, 6 incomes, with and without expenses, 2 set-aside rates, registered or not) to `test/fixtures/ca-tax-grid.json`; the new strategy matches every one.
- Standards checked: Stripe Tax returns a `tax_breakdown` list per calculation: an amount in minor units, a display name, and null rate details with a `taxability_reason` when a tax is not imposed. Our line is the same idea (`cents: null` with a `note`). [Stripe Tax Calculation object](https://docs.stripe.com/api/tax/calculations/object). PolicyEngine and OpenFisca parameters (plan 21 section 2.4) for the CPP figures.
- Tests: 3 in `packages/shared/test/country-tax.test.ts` (the frozen 1,680 estimates; lines add up to the total for every country with a pack and every region; CA deadlines, slip, line keys and labels); pension slot checks its dated figures (`country-core.test.ts`). Shared package 64 passed.
- Not done: API, server, exports and web still read the old fields (J3b).

## PR J3b — Countries: the tax estimate as lines in the API, exports and web (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J3b-tax-lines-api` (pushed, not merged), on J3a.
- Done: **API change** to `GET /tax/{year}`: `estimate.cpp_cents`, `cpp2_cents`, `pension_note` and `income_tax_cents` are replaced by `estimate.lines` (`key`, `label`, `cents`, `note`; they add up to `total_set_aside_cents`); the estimate's fields are now `required` in OpenAPI; generated types regenerated. The `tax_summary` export writes one row per line, named from its key, so the rows are unchanged (`Pension_Contribution`, `Pension_Contribution_Second_Band`, `Income_Tax_Set_Aside`). The tax-deadline notification reads the country module. The web Tax screen renders the lines as they come, with a line's note when it is not estimated (QC). The old `packages/shared/src/tax.ts` (`caTaxPack`, `taxPacks`, `createTaxRegistry`, `CppYear`, old `TaxPack`) is deleted; the new names are exported from the countries folder.

- Tests: `apps/server/test/tax.test.ts` (expected values unchanged, read as lines; new: lines add up to the total and the instalment is a quarter; QC and 2023 notes as full text; 2027 has no figures), `apps/server/test/exports-reports.test.ts` (the two other row names), `apps/web/test/tax.test.tsx` (new: lines rendered as given, a null line shows its note or a dash, a line the screen has never heard of is shown).

## PR J4a — Countries: every consumer reads the country modules; aliases removed (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J4a-country-consumers` (pushed, not merged), on J3b. J4 is split in two (J4a consumers and the tax switch, J4b the odometer note) to stay under 400 lines.
- Done: the shift deduction (`processing/presenter.ts`) and the phone's home summary (`store/summary.ts`) use `countryModules`, `mileageDeduction` and `periodStart`; the settings region check reads the country's regions from its places (same codes, same order, same message); `DEFAULT_COUNTRIES` is gone and the countries a driver may choose default to the registry (`countryModules.codes()`). The registry now lists US, CA, GB in that order, the order of the old list, so `GET /settings` `modules.countries` and the web country drop-down are unchanged. `countries/legacy.ts` (`caPack`, `usPack`, `gbPack`, `CountryPack`, `createCountryRegistry`, `countryPacks`, `toCountryPack`) is deleted; `countries.test.ts` and the phone summary test keep every expected value on the new names.
- **`comma.tax_countries` is a hide-only switch** (owner decision Q3): one helper, `shownTax(country, region, shown)`, used by the tax report and the deadline notifications, gives a country's tax pack only if the country has one and the switch lists it. Listing US or GB shows nothing; an empty list hides CA. Default unchanged (`['CA']`), so behaviour is unchanged.
- `expense-math.ts` is unchanged: it only asks whether a vehicle is an automobile (`notAutomobile`), which is not a country rule. Assumption (owner to confirm): an expense of an automobile counts as covered by the standard rate even where the driver's country has no rate (as today).
- Standards checked: the switch is an "ops toggle" / kill switch in Martin Fowler's feature-toggle taxonomy: an operator control that turns a feature off without a deploy; it never turns on what the code does not provide. [Feature toggles summary](https://openpracticelibrary-cn.pages.dev/practice/feature-toggles), [kill switches](https://medium.com/cinch-technology/are-you-sure-thats-a-feature-toggle-37d58229bbbc).
- Tests: `country-modules.test.ts` (registry order; the old alias names are no longer exported), `apps/server/test/tax.test.ts` (new: listing US and GB in `tax_countries` gives no estimate and no deadlines), `countries.test.ts` and `apps/phone/test/summary.test.tsx` ported with the same values.
- Not done: the odometer note (J4b). `GET /countries` (J5).

## PR J4b — Countries: the odometer note comes from a `logbook` slot (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J4b-country-logbook` (pushed, not merged), on J4a.

CA carries the CRA text that was written into `Odometer.tsx`, word for word. **API change (additive):** `GET /settings` (and the settings a phone gets when it pairs) has a new required field `logbook`, the resolved note for the driver's country and region, or `null`; OpenAPI and generated types updated. The web Odometer screen shows the note and hides the "Readings are optional" line from that field; both `country === 'CA'` branches are gone. The web has no other way to get country data until `GET /countries` (J5), so this is the minimal field the task allowed.

[libaddressinput data service](https://chromium-i18n.appspot.com/ssl-address/data/CA). The CRA logbook rules themselves (12-month base year, 3-month sample within 10 %) were looked up and are described the same way by secondary sources; the CRA page was not read, so the CA note says a person must check it. [taxtips.ca trip log](https://taxtips.ca/smallbusiness/trip-log-for-business-use-of-vehicle.htm).

## PR J5 — Countries: public `GET /countries`; names and a region drop-down on the web (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J5-countries-api` (pushed, not merged), on J4b.
- Done: **API change (additive):** `GET /countries`, public and read-only (no login; it also answers while the recovery key waits for confirmation, so setup can use it). It returns the countries a driver may choose, in the registry order (US, CA, GB): `code`, `name` (CLDR through `countryName()`), `currency`, `unit`, `labels` (libaddressinput level words, `null` when none), `supports` (`mileage`: the country has rates; `tax`: it has a tax pack and `comma.tax_countries` does not hide it), `verified`, `regional_rules` (some region or city has its own data) and `regions`, each with `code` (as `settings.region` stores it: `ON`), `name`, `own_rules`, `verified` (resolved place) and `cities` (none yet). The list follows the server's allowed countries, as `modules.countries` does. OpenAPI (`listCountries` in module `core`, schemas `Country`, `CountryPlace`) and generated types updated. Web: Setup lists country names from the server and takes currency and unit from it (its hard-coded list is gone); Settings shows country names, a region drop-down named with the country's word (Province, State, Nation) with "Not set", and, when the country has regional rules and no region is set, the note "Set your province to include its own rules. Until then, only the national rules apply." (owner decision Q4). A new country clears the region in the draft. If `GET /countries` cannot be read, Settings falls back to codes and the old free-text region field.
- **`logbook` stays in the settings response.** It is the resolved note of the driver's own place (country and region combined), which is what the Odometer screen needs; the countries tree describes what places support, not slot contents. Moving it would make the web combine levels itself. The phone does not read it (checked `apps/phone/src`), so nothing changes there.
- Standards checked: Stripe's Country Specs API (`GET /v1/country_specs`) is a read-only list keyed by ISO code with each country's default currency and what it supports. [Stripe country specs](https://docs.stripe.com/api/country_specs/list). Shopify's Storefront `localization.availableCountries` gives `isoCode`, `name`, `currency` and `unitSystem` per country, the same fields as ours. [Shopify international pricing](https://shopify.dev/docs/storefronts/headless/building-with-the-storefront-api/markets/international-pricing.md). Form practice: country before state or province, and the subdivision choices follow the chosen country. [FormSmarts on subdivisions](https://formsmarts.com/weblog/form-builder/how-to-capture-a-users-state-province-or-other-country-subdivision-on-a-form), [Smashing / Baymard country selector](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2011/11/redesigning-the-country-selector/).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/countries.test.ts` (2: public before and during setup, names, CA's 13 provinces with ON's own rules, US labels and no tax; `tax_countries: []` hides CA's tax; unknown codes are skipped). `apps/web/test/settings.test.tsx` (new: names, province drop-down, the prompt and its absence for US and once a region is set, a new country clears the region; the profile test now picks the province from the drop-down). `apps/web/test/auth.test.tsx` (setup waits for the country names and still sends USD and mi for the United States), `apps/web/test/shell.test.tsx` (setup calls only public routes: its state and the country list). Full suite 887 passed.
- Not done: the prompt is on Settings only; the Tax and Dashboard screens do not show it yet. Names are English only (no translation standard yet, plan 21 Q7). Setup still starts on CA and does not ask for a region (the onboarding wizard is a separate plan, section 3.11).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): "regional rules" means any region with its own data, including data a hidden tax estimate would use (CA's HST and QPP), so the prompt shows for Canada even when `comma.tax_countries` hides tax.

## PR J6 — Countries: the no-country-code lint rule, the full conformance suite and a README (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J6-country-contract-rules` (pushed, not merged), on J5.
- Done: **lint rule** (plan 21 section 3.4 rule 1) in `eslint.config.js`: in `apps/*/src` and `packages/*/src`, outside `packages/shared/src/countries/`, a country or region code may not be compared (`===`, `!==`, `==`, `!=`), used as a `case`, or tested with `includes`, `indexOf` or `has`. The codes are every ISO 3166-1 code CLDR names (read through `Intl.DisplayNames` when the config loads), with or without a subdivision part (`CA-QC`). Tests are not under `src`, so they are free. Modelled on the track-engine rule (`no-restricted-*`, test with `ESLint.lintText`). **Hits fixed or justified:** the web Tax screen's "Registered for GST/HST" now reads "Registered for {name}" from a new `sales_tax_name` field of `GET /tax/{year}` (the `salesTax` slot labels of the driver's place: HST in Ontario, GST in Alberta; `null` without a pack; API change, additive; OpenAPI and generated types updated). The TIFF byte-order check `'MM'` in `receipts/exif.ts` matches Myanmar's code; it is not a country and carries a disable comment saying so. Not flagged and kept: `comma.tax_countries` defaults to `['CA']` (owner decision Q3, an operator default, not logic) and Setup starts on CA (a form default; the onboarding wizard will choose).
- **Conformance suite** (plan 21 section 3.8), in `country-conformance.test.ts` for every registered country: as before (codes, CLDR name, currency, unit, sources, `verified`, places, slot data, dated rates ended by `null`, no rate for non-automobiles, period start) plus: every region code is in the ISO 3166-2 list; a city code is its region plus a three-character UN/LOCODE part; every dated parameter lists its dates in order (`datedProblems` now reports "dates are not in order"; `valueAt` still reads any order); a tax pack has sources, `verified`, the "checked against" text, a valid slip, the year's deadlines in order within the year and the next, and lines that add up to the total for the country and every place (moved here from `country-tax.test.ts`). Platform slugs in the shared list were already checked by the `platforms` slot.
- **ISO 3166-2 list:** `packages/shared/test/fixtures/iso-3166-2.json` (5,027 codes, status regular), made by `scripts/iso-subdivisions.mjs` from Unicode CLDR release 49 `common/validity/subdivision.xml`. Licence Unicode-3.0 (permissive); used by tests only, never shipped. CLDR bases its subdivision codes on ISO 3166-2 and may differ from it in rare cases (plan 21 section 2.4). Debian's `iso-codes` (`iso_3166-2.json`) was the other maintained source; not used because it is LGPL-2.1 and MileWage has no licence yet.
- `packages/shared/src/countries/README.md` for contributors: the data shape, slots, how to add a country, a region and a city, sources and `verified`, how to run the tests and refresh the ISO list.
- Standards checked: ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` with AST selectors is ESLint's built-in way to forbid a pattern without a plugin. [ESLint no-restricted-syntax](https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-restricted-syntax). CLDR validity data for subdivisions. [CLDR subdivision.xml](https://github.com/unicode-org/cldr/blob/release-49/common/validity/subdivision.xml), [Unicode licence](https://www.unicode.org/license.txt). Debian iso-codes as the alternative. [iso-codes](https://salsa.debian.org/iso-codes-team/iso-codes). date-holidays per-country tests (plan 21 section 2.4).
- Tests: `packages/shared/test/country-lint-rule.test.ts` (8: six code-in-logic shapes rejected in server, web and phone; allowed in the countries folder and in tests; HTTP methods, byte marks, lower case and codes as data pass). Conformance: 12 (a new tax test per country, skipped for US and GB, which have no pack). `country-core.test.ts` (unsorted dates reported, still read). `apps/server/test/tax.test.ts` (`sales_tax_name` HST in Ontario, `null` for the US), `apps/web/test/tax.test.tsx` ("Registered for HST" from the data). Full suite 896 passed, 3 skipped.
- Not done: three-letter subdivision codes on their own (`ENG`, `SCT`) are not caught by the lint rule (too many false matches with other three-letter words); no such code is used in logic today.

## PR J7 — Countries: the gig-work dimensions in the contract, as closed lists (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J7-country-dimensions` (pushed, not merged), on J6.
- Done: `countries/dimensions.ts` with the closed lists of plan 21 section 3.6a (owner decision Q8): work kinds (`delivery`, `ride_hailing`, `moto_taxi`, `courier`), worker statuses (`contractor`, `employee`, `micro_business`, each with an optional country label, required for `micro_business`), income-tax regimes (`net_profit`, `presumptive`, `flat_fee`, `withheld_final`), mileage methods (`per_distance`, `actual_costs`, `none`), payers (`platform`, `cash`, `wallet`) and payout rhythms in the `IncomeModel` (section 3.6), the OECD model fields a platform statement can carry, and the `ExpenseOverrides` hook (relabel or add categories, plan 18 section 5.1) with `expenseCategoriesFor(country)`. The contract gains `work`, `statuses`, `income?`, `expenseOverrides?` on the country, `methods` on its mileage and `regimes` on a tax pack. A new `filing` slot (nearest level wins, as a whole) holds the sales-tax registration rule (`threshold` with a dated amount, `from_first_dollar`, `platform_collects`) and the platform statement (name, due `MM-DD`, OECD fields, copy to the driver). `dimensionProblems(country)` checks every answer against the lists.
- **Filled only with today's use:** CA, US and GB: work `delivery`, status `contractor` (no label), mileage methods `per_distance` and `actual_costs` (every country already lets the driver choose per vehicle and year, `deduction-method.ts`); the CA tax pack: regime `net_profit`. `income`, `expenseOverrides` and `filing` stay empty for all three: no checked source yet (plan 21 section 4). Nothing reads the new fields, so no behaviour changes; `GET /countries` does not show them yet.
- Not filled on purpose: the Canadian small-supplier threshold and the Part XX platform statement (plan 21 section 2.5 finding) are known from secondary pages only; a person must check the CRA pages before they go into `filing`.
- Standards checked: the OECD Model Rules for Reporting by Platform Operators (2020) report, per seller, identity, tax ID, financial account, consideration paid or credited each quarter, the number of activities, and fees, commissions and taxes withheld, with a copy to the seller. [HMRC IEIM901741 fees, commissions and taxes withheld](https://www.gov.uk/hmrc-internal-manuals/international-exchange-of-information/ieim901741), [Matheson summary](https://matheson.com/insights/detail/oecd-model-rules-for-reporting-by-platform-operators-with-respect-to-sellers-in-the-sharing-and-gig), [HMRC reporting rules](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/reporting-rules-for-digital-platforms/reporting-rules-for-digital-platforms), [CRA guidance](https://www.canada.ca/en/revenue-agency/programs/about-canada-revenue-agency-cra/compliance/reporting-rules-digital-platforms/guidance-on-reporting-rules.html). The ILO's location-based platform work for the work kinds and the other sources of plan 21 section 2.5 for the statuses, regimes and registration rules.
- Tests: `packages/shared/test/country-dimensions.test.ts` (5: the lists; today's answers for CA, US and GB; every kind of wrong answer reported; expense overrides relabel and add, shared list unchanged without them; the `filing` slot combines and is checked). Conformance: every registered country has no dimension problems. Shared package 78 passed; full suite 901 passed, 3 skipped. The contributor README has a short section on the dimensions.
- Not done: a region cannot yet override `work` or `statuses` (no country needs it); the driver's own choices (status, regime) are not stored (plan 21 section 3.6a, later).

## PR J8 — Countries: the driver's own mileage rate where the country has none (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J8-custom-mileage-rate` (pushed, not merged), on J7.
- Done (owner decision Q5): **one rate per driver, on the settings**: `own_mileage_rate` `{ cents_per_unit, unit }` (minor units per km or mile, hundredths kept: 30.5), stored in two new `settings` columns (core migration `0009_own_mileage_rate.sql`, hundredths of a cent and the unit). **API change (additive):** `GET /settings` gains `own_mileage_rate` and `mileage_rate` (`official`, `yours` or `none`); `PATCH /settings` takes `own_mileage_rate` (or `null` to clear; above 0 and at most 100,000). OpenAPI (`OwnMileageRate`) and generated types updated. Shared helpers `mileageRateSource(country, own)` and `ownRateDeduction(rate, trip)`. The shift deduction (`presenter.ts`, so shifts, summary, exports and the tax report) uses the own rate only when the driver's country has no mileage rates at all; like an official rate it gives nothing for bicycles, e-bikes, scooters and walking, and nothing for a vehicle deducted through actual expenses. **Labels:** the web Shifts list, week totals and shift detail say "Deduction (your rate)", the Dashboard says "Deduction value (your rate)" with "Distance times your rate. It is not an official rate."; the shift-level CSV names the column `Deduction_Value_Your_Rate`. Settings › General shows "Your mileage rate (NPR per km)" only for a country whose `GET /countries` entry has no mileage support.
- **Decision: the own rate never overrides an official one** (conservative default). It is kept when the driver moves to a country with rates, but unused there (`mileage_rate: official`). A country with rates whose dates have ended (for example 2027 before a new rate is announced) also gets no own-rate value.
- **Where it is stored, and why:** MileIQ (individual accounts) has one custom rate for the whole account, used instead of the IRS rate; Driversnote sets a custom rate per workplace (not per vehicle; a second vehicle needs a second workplace) with a date range. MileWage has one driver per server, so the settings are the account-wide place. Assumption (owner to confirm): one rate without dates, applied at read time to every shift, like MileIQ's individual setting; a rate change therefore changes past values (as every deduction here is computed at read time). Rates by date, as Driversnote does, can be added later.
- **Effect today:** every country a driver may choose (US, CA, GB) has official rates, so nothing changes for current drivers; the rate becomes usable once a country without a mileage pack is registered. Assumption (owner to confirm): drivers still choose only registered countries.
- Standards checked: [Driversnote custom rates](https://driversnote.helpscoutdocs.com/article/102-manage-reimbursement-rates), [MileIQ business rate](https://support.mileiq.com/hc/en-us/articles/203801459-How-to-edit-the-Business-Rate-and-Distance-Unit), [MileIQ for Teams custom rates](https://support.mileiq.com/hc/en-us/articles/28953180884244-MileIQ-for-Teams-How-do-I-set-Custom-Mileage-Rates).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/own-rate.test.ts` (3: a country without rates has `none` and null deductions, then the own rate values a car and not a bicycle, `yours`, and the CSV column says so, then clearing it; the US keeps the IRS rate and the plain column although an own rate is stored; bad rates are refused). `apps/web/test/settings.test.tsx` (the rate field for a country without official rates, hundredths sent, hidden for Canada), `apps/web/test/shifts.test.tsx` ("Deduction (your rate)"). Full suite 906 passed, 3 skipped.
- Not done: the phone still computes its Home deduction from the country registry and does not know the own rate; J9 makes it follow the server.

## PR J9 — Countries: the phone's Home figures follow the server (Track J, plan 21)

- Branch `pr-J9-phone-country-consistency` (pushed, not merged), on J8.
- Done: **the server's deduction wins on the phone.** Phone database migration 3 adds `shifts.server_deduction_cents` and `server_deduction_known`; the pull step (`sync/pull.ts`, insert and update of a shift) stores the `deduction_cents` the server sends with each shift (it already came with the shift; nothing was kept). `summarize` (`store/summary.ts`) uses that value for every shift the server has sent, including `null` (no rate, or a vehicle deducted through actual expenses, which fixes the old mismatch where the phone still showed a number), and estimates only the shifts the server has not sent: from the country's rates, or from the driver's own rate (J8) where the country has none, never for bicycles, e-bikes, scooters or walking. Prior distance for tiered rates still counts every shift on the phone, as before. After a server restore (higher restore generation) the stored values are forgotten with the `on_server` state, so the phone estimates until the shifts are resent. **Settings:** the phone keeps the server's settings as the whole JSON in its key-value store (`pairing.settings`, written at pairing, by `GET /settings` and by the pull feed; the `settings_cache` table of migration 1 is not used), so `region`, `own_mileage_rate` and `mileage_rate` were already stored; `CachedSettings` now names them (optional, for older servers). The Home card says "Deduction value (your rate)" when `mileage_rate` is `yours`. **Money:** `money()` on the Home screen places the decimal point with the currency's own digits (Intl's `maximumFractionDigits`; without Intl, a table of the ISO 4217 currencies whose minor unit is 0, 3 or 4 digits), so JPY 1234 is ¥1,234 and KWD 1234 is 1.234, instead of always dividing by 100.
- **Region:** mileage has no regional data (no `mileage` slot is used), so the driver's region does not change the phone's estimate; it is stored with the settings for later use.
- Plan 21 section 8 gains a watch-out: keep heavy dependencies (a holiday library) out of `packages/shared`, because the phone bundles it.
- Standards checked: the server is the source of truth for computed figures, the phone shows its own figure only for what the server has not processed (MileWage README principle; the same offline-first rule the shift distances already follow). ISO 4217 minor units (exponent per currency). [ISO 4217 list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_4217), [Intl.NumberFormat resolvedOptions](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/NumberFormat/resolvedOptions).
- Tests (phone, jest): `summary.test.tsx` (new: the server's value wins, a server `null` counts as none, an unsent shift is estimated; the own rate for a country without rates and not for a bicycle, ignored for Canada; money digits with and without Intl; the "your rate" label on the Home screen; the US and CA cases keep their numbers), `pull.test.ts` (the shift's server deduction is stored, a `null` is stored as known; settings from the feed keep `region` and `mileage_rate`), `status.test.tsx` (a restore forgets the server values). Phone suite 239 passed, 2 skipped; full suite 906 passed, 3 skipped.
- Not done: the server's value is the one sent with the shift's latest change. A later change that alters it without touching the shift (the driver's own rate or deduction method, or an earlier shift arriving late under a tiered rate) is not resent by the change feed, so the phone can lag until the shift changes again. Assumption (owner to confirm): acceptable for the Home summary; a fix is for the server to log affected shifts on those events. The `POST /shifts/{id}/end` answer is not used for the value (only the feed). An unsent shift of a vehicle deducted through actual expenses is still estimated until it is sent (the phone does not know the method). The web's `formatMoney` still assumes 2 digits.

## PR L0 — Plan: earnings as entries (plan 22, owner decision Q7a)

- Branch `pr-L0-earnings-entries-plan` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-J0-country-plan`.
- Done: [`22-earnings-entries-plan.md`](./22-earnings-entries-plan.md). Per shift and platform, entries of ten types (`fare`, `tip`, `bonus`, `cash_collected`, `commission`, `platform_fee`, `tax_withheld`, `top_up`, `payout`, `adjustment`) with a signed integer amount whose sign is its effect on what the platform owes the driver, enforced by the database. Totals are sums at read time; the balance can go below zero. Today's `gross/tips/bonus` become `fare/tip/bonus` entries in migration `earnings/0002` (non-zero amounts only; a row of zeros stays a row), then the three columns are dropped. The existing `PUT` keeps working and never removes the other seven types; two new operations add and remove one entry. Every consumer already reads `effective()` / `totalOf()`, so the change sits in one repository. Plan 21 §5 points to it.
- Standards checked: OECD Model Reporting Rules for Digital Platforms via [HMRC IEIM901741](https://www.gov.uk/hmrc-internal-manuals/international-exchange-of-information/ieim901741) (consideration after fees, commissions and taxes withheld; those reported apart; number of activities); [Stripe balance transactions](https://docs.stripe.com/api/balance_transactions/object) (closed type list, signed minor units); [Fowler's accounting patterns](https://www.martinfowler.com/eaaDev/AccountingNarrative.html) (balance = sum of entries, no edits); Uber and Grab driver statements (cash trips, commission from the wallet, negative balance); Mexico and India platform withholding; Gridwise and Stride (tips kept apart). Links in plan 22 §2.
- Assumptions (owner to confirm): A1 earnings are after commission and fees, before tax withheld (unchanged for every existing row); A2 entries belong to a shift, so a weekly payout is entered on a shift of that week; A3 signs are fixed per type and corrections are adjustments; A4 a commission entered here and as an expense is deducted twice (not detected).
- No code.

## PR L1 — Earnings: entries table and migration of today's rows (Track L)

- Branch `pr-L1-earnings-entries-db` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-L0-earnings-entries-plan`.
- Done (plan 22 §3, §4): migration `earnings/0002_earning_entries.sql` creates `earning_entries` (ten types, signed integer minor units, the sign rule as a CHECK, cascade from the row), turns each existing `gross/tips/bonus` amount above zero into a `fare/tip/bonus` entry dated with the row's `edited_at`, and drops the three columns. A row of zeros stays a row with no entries, so its total is still 0 and not `null`. The migration runs after the verified pre-migration backup `upgrade.ts` already takes. `earnings-repository.ts` reads every figure as a sum of entries (gross, tips, bonus, fees, adjustments, tax withheld, cash collected, balance); `totalOf` (earnings) is gross + tips + bonus + fees + adjustments, the same number as before for every existing row. Today's `PUT` replaces the fare, tip or bonus entries only when their sum changes and never touches the other seven types. `addEntry`, `deleteEntry` and `entriesOf` are ready for L2. API, OpenAPI and screens unchanged.
- Standards checked: as in L0 (OECD MRDP via HMRC, Stripe balance transactions, Fowler's accounting patterns); [SQLite `ALTER TABLE DROP COLUMN`](https://www.sqlite.org/lang_altertable.html#altertabdropcol) (3.35+, a column's own CHECK does not block it; the server has 3.53).
- Tests: 4 in `earnings-entries.test.ts` (migration on a database with four rows over two shifts: every amount, count, note, source and edit time reads back, zeros stay a row, only non-zero amounts become entries, the effective total is unchanged, the columns are gone, a deleted shift takes its entries; wrong signs and zeros refused by the database; a cash trip with commission, withheld tax and a top-up gives earnings 8 000 and balance −2 100 then 2 900, and `GET /summary` shows 8 000; the old `PUT` keeps a commission and an unchanged two-fare split and replaces a changed amount). The existing earnings, import, tax, analytics, goals, progress and export tests pass unchanged.
- Assumptions (owner to confirm): A1–A4 of plan 22 §8.
- Not done: the API for entries (L2), the web (L3), the tax report lines (L4).

## PR L2 — Earnings: API for entries (Track L)

- Branch `pr-L2-earnings-entries-api` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-L1-earnings-entries-db`.
- Done (plan 22 §5): `POST /shifts/{ref}/earnings/{platform}/entries` (`addEarningEntry`: type from the closed list, signed `amount_cents`, optional note; creates the platform's row if missing; shift must be ended; a wrong sign is a 422 that says which sign the type needs, e.g. "a commission takes money off, so it must be below 0") and `DELETE /shifts/{ref}/earnings/{platform}/entries/{id}` (`deleteEarningEntry`, idempotent; only an entry of that row; the row stays). No edit operation: delete and add again. Both emit `earnings.changed`. `GET /shifts/{ref}/earnings` rows gain `fees_cents`, `adjustments_cents`, `total_cents`, `tax_withheld_cents`, `cash_collected_cents`, `balance_cents` and `ledger` (the entries); effective rows gain `fees_cents`, `adjustments_cents`, `tax_withheld_cents`. Existing fields and operations unchanged. OpenAPI (`EarningEntryType`, `EarningEntryCreate`, `EarningEntry`, both paths in `x-modules.earnings`) and generated types updated.
- Standards checked: as in L0; Stripe's signed `amount` per balance transaction is the model for the request body.
- Tests: 2 in `earnings.test.ts` (a cash trip through the API: sums, ledger order and note, effective total and `GET /summary` 8 000, removing the commission, removing twice and through another platform's row changes nothing; rules: active shift 409, wrong signs and zero 422 with the message, unknown type, fraction, missing amount, unknown platform 422, a negative adjustment accepted, unknown shift 404, no session 401). The module matrix now covers the 404 side of both new operations with `earnings` off.
- Assumptions (owner to confirm): A1–A4 of plan 22 §8.
- Not done: the web (L3), the tax report lines (L4).
