## PR L4 — Earnings: fees and withheld tax in the tax report (Track L)

- Branch `pr-L4-earnings-tax-income` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-L3-earnings-entries-web`.
- Done (plan 22 §5.3, §6): `GET /tax/{year}` `income` gains `fees_cents`, `adjustments_cents` and `tax_withheld_cents`, so gross + tips + bonus + fees + adjustments = `total_cents`. The estimate itself is unchanged in code: it already reads `total_cents`, which since L1 is after commission and fees. Withheld tax is shown, not subtracted (crediting it is a country tax pack's rule, Track J). The `tax_summary` export adds `Income,Platform_Fees_Taken`, `Income,Adjustments` and `Income,Tax_Withheld_By_Platforms` rows, also only when not zero; fees and withheld tax are written above zero, as on the OECD statement, because the export's formula guard would turn a leading minus into text. A negative correction is still written with that guard (rare). The Tax screen shows "Platform fees (already taken off)" and "Tax withheld by platforms (not counted below)" only when they are not zero, so nothing changes for today's drivers. OpenAPI `TaxYear.income` and generated types updated.

Existing tax tests pass unchanged.

## PR V1 — Vehicles: who owns the vehicle (Track V, owner decision Q7b)

- Branch `pr-V1-vehicle-ownership` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-J0-country-plan`.
- Done: spec in [`21`](./21-jurisdictions-plan.md) section 3.6c. Each vehicle has `ownership`: `owned`, `financed`, `leased` or `rented`. Migration `core/0009_vehicle_ownership` adds the column in place (`ADD COLUMN ... NOT NULL DEFAULT 'owned' CHECK (...)`; no table rebuild), so every vehicle, id and reference is kept. `POST` and `PATCH /vehicles` accept it, every vehicle response and the phone sync feed carry it; OpenAPI (`VehicleOwnership`) and generated types updated. A record only: no tax rule reads it. The phone is unchanged (it keeps name, kind and archived state only).
- Standards checked: [STAR VehicleOwnershipType](https://schemas.liquid-technologies.com/STAR/5.3.4/vehicleownershiptypeenumeratedtype.html) (Owned, Leased, Rented; `financed` added for hire purchase and drive-to-own: [Bolt and MAX](https://techcabal.com/2021/11/24/bolt-partners-max-ng/), [Moove in Kenya](https://africa.com/headlines/nigerian-fintech-company-moove-comes-to-kenya)); leases not split into finance and operating ([IFRS 16 vs ASC 842](https://dart.deloitte.com/USDART/home/publications/deloitte/additional-deloitte-guidance/roadmap-ifrs-us-gaap-comparison/chapter-5-broad-transactions/5-7-leases)); India weekly rentals ([Yulu](https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/11/indias-yulu-raises-93m-as-quick-commerce-boom-fuels-e-bike-demand/)). Full list in `21` section 3.6c.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): existing vehicles, and new ones sent without the field, are `owned` (rather than "not said").
- Tests: `apps/server/test/ownership.test.ts` (4: default and each value, 422 for unknown and null, sync feed, migration on a database with vehicles, shifts and readings); `vehicles.test.ts` and the `core/0008` rebuild test in `profile.test.ts` updated for the new column; phone `pull.test.ts` (a vehicle with `ownership` and an unknown future value caches as before).

## PR O1 — Onboarding: the server keeps whether setup is finished and the work kinds (plan 23)

- Branch `pr-O1-onboarding-server` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-X1-tracks-merged`.
- Done (plan 23 section 4.3): core migration `0011_onboarding.sql` adds `settings.onboarded_at` (epoch ms) and `settings.work_kinds` (JSON list) and marks every owner who already has a vehicle as onboarded (at the migration's time), so they do not see the wizard. **API change (additive):** `GET /settings` gains `onboarded_at` (null until the wizard is finished) and `work_kinds`; `PATCH /settings` takes `work_kinds` (closed list `WorkKind` from J7; only kinds the driver's country offers, otherwise 422 "must be among delivery") and `onboarded` (`true` sets `onboarded_at` to the server's time and keeps an earlier one; `false` clears it, for "Run setup again"). A new country drops the kinds it does not offer, as it drops a region. **Rule 1 on the server:** when the driver has chosen nothing and the country offers one kind, `work_kinds` answers that kind, so CA, US and GB drivers have `['delivery']` without being asked. `GET /countries` gains `work` (the kinds each country's data covers), which the wizard needs to know whether to ask. OpenAPI (`WorkKind`, `Country.work`, `Settings`, `SettingsPatch`) and generated types updated; web test fixtures carry the new fields (settings fixture onboarded, so existing screens behave as before).
- **`onboarded` instead of writing `onboarded_at`:** the time comes from the server's clock, like every other `*_at`, so the client sends only the fact.
- Standards checked: the plan 23 list (GOV.UK, NN/g, W3C WAI) covers the screens; for the server side, GitLab keeps a user's onboarding state in its database (`user_details.onboarding_status`) and drives the sign-up navigation from that stored value rather than the browser, which is what `onboarded_at` does. [GitLab MR 141932](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/merge_requests/141932), [GitLab issue 454680](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/454680), [GitLab issue 547922](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/547922).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/onboarding.test.ts` (4: a new owner is not onboarded and has `['delivery']`; finishing sets the time once, running again clears it; kinds outside the country's list are refused, a country with two kinds (test code `YY`) starts empty and keeps the order sent, a move back to Canada drops `moto_taxi`; `GET /countries` lists `work`; the migration marks the owner with a vehicle and leaves the one without). All 4 fail without the change. Full suite 929 passed, 3 skipped; phone 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): owners with a vehicle are onboarded (plan 23); an archived vehicle counts too.
- Not done: the wizard (O2–O4).

## PR O2 — Onboarding: the wizard shell, region and work steps (plan 23)

- Branch `pr-O2-onboarding-shell` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-O1-onboarding-server`.
- Done (plan 23 sections 4.1, 4.2): **routing:** when the session is ready and `settings.onboarded_at` is null (a new owner right after the recovery key, or after "Run setup again"), `App.tsx` shows the wizard instead of the app; an onboarded driver can still open it at `/onboarding/<step>` ("Continue setup", O4). **Steps in one file** (`onboarding/steps.tsx`): `id`, `title`, `visible(ctx)`, `done(ctx)`, `Component`; `ctx` is the settings plus the driver's country from `GET /countries`. **Shell** (`onboarding/Wizard.tsx`): the URL names the step (`/onboarding/region`), so the browser's Back and a reload work; without a step (or with one that does not apply) it resumes at the first visible step not done, read from the server (rule 3); heading and document title "Where you drive — Step 1 of 2" counting only visible steps (rule 4); the heading takes focus on each step; a "Back" link above the card to the previous visible step; after the last step it sends `PATCH /settings { onboarded: true }` and opens the dashboard (O4 puts "Check your answers" and Finish there). **Step form** (`StepForm.tsx`): Continue saves, then goes on (rule 5); "Skip this step" goes on without saving; a server error keeps the driver on the step. **Where you drive** (shown when the country has regions): the region drop-down named with the country's level word (Province, State, Nation) and "Not set"; when the country has regional rules: "Until you choose your province, only the national rules apply." (Q4); optional. **What work you do** (shown only when the country's data covers more than one kind, rule 1; never for CA, US or GB today): checkboxes, "Choose all that apply.", saved in the country's order.
- Standards checked (beyond plan 23 section 3): GOV.UK back link at the top of a question page. [Back link](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/back-link/). GOV.UK checkboxes: "Select all that apply" as the hint, one fieldset. [Checkboxes](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/checkboxes/). Moving focus to the new page's heading after a client-side route change, so screen readers announce it. [Gatsby accessible client routing research](https://www.gatsbyjs.com/blog/2019-07-11-user-testing-accessible-client-routing/).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/onboarding.test.tsx` (4: a driver not onboarded gets the wizard, Canada shows only the province step as "Step 1 of 1" in heading and title, Continue sends the region then `onboarded`, and the dashboard opens; a country with two kinds: skip sends nothing, Back returns, the kinds are sent in the country's order; resume at the first step not done even from `/dashboard`; an onboarded driver gets the app and can still open `/onboarding/region`). All fail without the change. Full suite 933 passed, 3 skipped; phone 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Not done: apps and vehicle (O3); goal, phone, check answers and Finish (O4). No visual check yet (done once in O4 for the whole wizard).

## PR O3 — Onboarding: apps and vehicle steps (plan 23)

- Branch `pr-O3-onboarding-apps-vehicle` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-O2-onboarding-shell`.
- Done (plan 23 section 4.2 steps 3 and 4), in `onboarding/SetupSteps.tsx`, two entries in `steps.tsx`: **Your apps** (module `platforms`): the apps `GET /platforms` lists for the driver's country and region (switched-off ones too) as checkboxes, plus "Another app" which reveals "Name of the app". Continue switches off the listed apps not ticked and switches on the ticked ones (`PUT /platforms/{slug}` with `active`, only for those that change) and adds the other app as the driver's own (`label`; slug made as on the Platforms card, now a shared `slugOf`). Nothing ticked is the same as skipping, so every app stays on. Done when an app is switched off or one of the driver's own exists. **Your vehicle** (always shown): name, type and ownership (Track V words, shared with the Vehicles page), `POST /vehicles`. A line says which rate applies and follows the type: "Gets the official mileage rate of Canada.", "No official rate here: you can set your own rate." with J8's optional "Your mileage rate (NPR per km)" field (`PATCH /settings own_mileage_rate`), or "Bicycles, e-bikes, scooters and walking have no mileage rate.". Without a vehicle there is no skip and Continue without a name says "Enter a name for your vehicle."; with one, the step lists the vehicles and can be skipped or used to add another. The wizard now reads countries, platforms and vehicles together and again after each saved answer (a region can change the apps). Errors in the driver's answer are shown as written (`StepError`).
- Standards checked (beyond plan 23 section 3): GOV.UK checkboxes with a conditionally revealed question ("Another app" reveals the name). [Conditionally revealing a related question](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/checkboxes/#conditionally-revealing-a-related-question). GOV.UK error messages start with what to do ("Enter …"). [Error message](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/error-message/). The rate line uses the J8 sources (MileIQ, Driversnote) and the 18 section 4.1 rule that non-automobiles get no allowance rate.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/onboarding.test.tsx` 3 new (apps: nothing ticked at first, the other app needs a name, then only the unticked app is switched off and the other app is added; vehicle: no skip without a vehicle, the rate line for Canada and for a bicycle, the name error, ownership `rented` sent; a country without official rates: the own rate 0.305 is sent as 30.5 per km, an existing vehicle allows going on without adding one). The O2 tests now count the apps and vehicle steps. Full suite 936 passed, 3 skipped; phone 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): before the driver answers, no app is ticked (rather than every listed app, which is how the server starts); a driver who keeps every listed app and adds none is asked again by "Continue setup", which is harmless.
- Not done: goal, phone, check answers, Finish (O4).

## PR O4a — Onboarding: weekly goal and pair-your-phone steps (plan 23)

- Branch `pr-O4a-onboarding-goal-phone` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-O3-onboarding-apps-vehicle`. O4 is split in two (O4a steps 5 and 6, O4b check answers, Finish, "Continue setup", "Run setup again" and the dashboard region prompt) to stay under 400 lines.
- Done (plan 23 section 4.2 steps 5 and 6), in `onboarding/GoalPhoneSteps.tsx`, two entries in `steps.tsx`: **Weekly goal** (module `goals`): "How much do you want to earn each week? (CAD)"; Continue adds a weekly earnings goal "Weekly earnings" (`POST /goals`, amount through the Goals page's `targetOf`); empty is a skip; an existing weekly goal is named. Done when an active weekly goal exists. **Pair your phone** (module `devices`): asks for a pairing token, shows the Devices page's pairing code (QR, link, expiry), and checks `GET /devices` every 1.5 s until a phone that is not revoked appears, then says "Paired: Pixel 8." and the skip ("Pair later") goes away. Done when such a phone exists. **The pairing code is now one component** (`PairingCode`, exported from `pages/Devices.tsx`), used by the Devices page and by the step, so later changes to the pairing UI (PO4: "Get the app", "Open in the MileWage app") land in one place. The wizard also reads goals and devices.
- Standards checked (beyond plan 23 section 3): GOV.UK text input for numbers (`inputmode="decimal"` for money, no `type=number`). [Text input, numbers](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/text-input/#numbers). Polling for the other device's result, as the OAuth device grant does (the waiting side asks at an interval until the other side has finished). [RFC 8628 section 3.4–3.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628#section-3.4). The waiting and paired messages are a `status` live region, so a screen reader hears the change. [WAI-ARIA status role](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#status).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/onboarding.test.tsx` 2 new (goal: a wrong amount is refused, 800 becomes a weekly earnings goal of 80 000 cents, then the phone step; phone: the QR shows, "Pair later" is offered, the phone appears on the step's first check, "Paired: Pixel 8." and Continue finishes with `onboarded: true`); step counts updated (Canada: 5 steps). `devices.test.tsx` passes unchanged after the extraction. Full suite 938 passed, 3 skipped; phone 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Expected conflict with PO4** (`pr-PO4-web-get-the-app`): PO4 changes the pairing block of `pages/Devices.tsx`, which this PR moved into `PairingCode` in the same file. Resolve by putting PO4's "Get the app" and "Open in the MileWage app" inside `PairingCode`; the wizard step then shows them too, as plan 24 section 4.3 asks.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the goal step asks only for weekly earnings (Comma's "What's your weekly target?"); hours, shifts and distance goals stay on the Goals page.
- Not done: check answers, Finish, "Continue setup", "Run setup again", dashboard region prompt (O4b).

## PR O4b — Onboarding: check your answers, Finish, Continue setup, Run setup again (plan 23)

- Branch `pr-O4b-onboarding-finish` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-O4a-onboarding-goal-phone`.
- Done (plan 23 sections 4.2 step 7 and 4.4): **Check your answers** (`onboarding/CheckStep.tsx`, the last entry in `steps.tsx`): every visible step with its answer in a few words (each step now has a `summary`) and a "Change" link whose hidden text names the step; after a change, Continue comes back to the answers instead of walking on. **Finish** (the step's button) sends `PATCH /settings { onboarded: true }` and opens the dashboard; it refuses with "Add your vehicle first: a shift needs one." when there is no vehicle (the vehicle step cannot be skipped, but Back and Change can pass it). The step form now waits for Finish, so a server error shows on the step. **Dashboard:** the "Finish setting up" card has **Continue setup**, which opens `/onboarding`, and the wizard resumes at the first step not done; while the country has regional rules and no region is set, a note "Set your province to include its own rules. Until then, only the national rules apply." with a link to Settings (owner decision Q4, the same words as Settings). **Settings › General** has a "Setup" card with **Run setup again**, which opens the first step (`/onboarding/start`). The apps step leaves the built-in "Other" alone (it is the earnings fallback; "Another app" names the driver's own), in the step and in the answers. The pairing code's text column wraps under the QR at phone width (it was squeezed beside it, also on the Devices page).
- **Run setup again does not clear `onboarded_at`:** the driver stays onboarded, so leaving the wizard half-way returns to the app, and Finish keeps the first time. `PATCH /settings { onboarded: false }` (O1) stays available. Conservative choice: the driver is never locked into the wizard by a button in Settings.
- Standards checked (beyond plan 23 section 3): GOV.UK summary list for the answers, with visually hidden text on each "Change" link so a screen reader hears what it changes, and returning to the check page after a change. [Summary list](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/summary-list/), [Check answers](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/check-answers/). The region note is a `note`, not an `alert` (nothing urgent; an alert interrupts screen readers). [WAI-ARIA note role](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#note).
- **Visual check** (headless Chromium from the Playwright cache, driven with `playwright-core` borrowed from another project's `node_modules`; script and screenshots in the session scratchpad, nothing added to the repo): the built web client behind a stand-in static and `/api` proxy, against the real server bundle on a fresh data directory; owner created through `POST /setup` and confirmed. At 390 px: region (title "Where you drive — Step 1 of 6 — MileWage"), apps, vehicle, goal, phone and check answers, then Finish to the dashboard; at 1280 px: vehicle, check answers and dashboard. Found and fixed here: the pairing code's text squeezed beside the QR at 390 px; the built-in "Other" listed next to "Another app". No horizontal scroll at 390 px; no console errors.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/onboarding.test.tsx` 3 new (the answers as rows with their Change links; Change goes to the region step and Continue comes back with Quebec; Finish sends `onboarded: true` and the app opens; Finish without a vehicle is refused with no request; Settings › Run setup again opens "Where you drive — Step 1 of 6" with no request); the phone test now ends on "Check your answers — Step 6 of 6"; the apps test checks the built-in "Other" is not listed. `apps/web/test/dashboard-extras.test.tsx` 1 new (the province note and its link, Continue setup opens the wizard at the first step not done, no note once a province is set). All fail without the change. Full suite 942 passed, 3 skipped; phone 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Expected conflict with PO4** (`pr-PO4-web-get-the-app`): as in O4a, `PairingCode` in `pages/Devices.tsx` (this PR also changes its text column class); PO4's additions go inside it.
- Not done: translations (plan 21 Q7d). Account creation (`Setup.tsx`) is unchanged (plan 23 section 4.5).

## PR W2a — Web: shift detail header, key figures and checks (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2a-shift-detail-header` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X1-tracks-merged`.
- Why: the owner (2026-11-07) found the shift page "messed up, not organized". W2a–W2d rework it; this PR is the top of the page and the order of the blocks.
- Done: the title is the date in words ("Thursday, Nov 5, 2026"); one line under it gives the time range, the length and the vehicle's name. A "n to review" badge links to the checks. Four key figures: Distance (with personal distance under it), Business (active and deadhead under it, each marked estimated, only for a stop-segmented shift), Time, and the deduction with its label (J8). The Details card is gone: start and end are in the header, personal distance is in the figures, the phone check is in Checks. **Flags become "Checks"** in plain words from `14` §8 (an unknown code is shown as it is), review ones first with a "Review" badge, then the phone check in words. Order below the figures: route, trips, then earnings and odometer beside checks, notes and export (side by side when the page is wide enough, by container query), then "Label stops" (optional) across the full width.
- Standards checked: Uber's driver trip details (a breakdown of each trip's parts, then earnings: [Uber, earnings basics](https://www.uber.com/us/en/drive/basics/earnings/)); Strava activity page (key figures above the map, details below: [Ride activity pages](https://support.strava.com/hc/en-us/articles/216919577-Ride-Activity-Pages)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/shifts.test.tsx` 2 new or rewritten (header line and the four figures with personal distance and the marked split; checks in words, review first, unknown code, phone check, the review link, "Nothing to check."). Headings in `exports`, `moduleflags`, `routeview`, `shiftearnings`, `stops` tests now match the date in words; `moduleflags` and `stops` read the split from the Business figure. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 117 files, 925 passed, 3 skipped (web 33 files, 216); phone jest 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium (Playwright cache) on the Vite dev server with the API answered by a fixture (a Toronto shift with 6 trips and 5 stops), 1280 and 390 px light; screenshots in the session scratchpad.
- Not done: the Earned figure and the platforms in the header (W2b); the timeline of trips and stops and the map links (W2c, W2d).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): "Business" is active plus deadhead; personal distance is shown under Distance.

## PR W2c — Web: the shift as a timeline beside the map (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2c-shift-timeline` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W2b-shift-detail-earned`.
- Done: the trips table is replaced by a **Timeline** card beside the route map (side by side when the page is at least 48rem wide, by container query; stacked, map first, below that). It lists, in time order: "Shift started", each trip (start time, distance, kind with the estimate marker for the engine's active and deadhead, then the time range, minutes, purpose and "edited" for a hand-made trip), and "Shift ended" (or "in progress"). The card says how many trips there are; on wide screens the list scrolls inside the card so it stays beside the map. Each trip is a toggle button (`aria-pressed`): choosing it picks it, choosing it again lets go; W2e links the pick to the map. A trip's coloured bar is the route yellow (paler for deadhead, grey for personal). With the route module off, one line says "The route map is off on this server, so the shift is shown as a list only." `Trips.tsx` keeps only `kindOf` and the `useShiftTrips` hook (the page reads trips once and rereads them after a recompute, as before).
- Standards checked: Strava (a list under or beside the map whose entries highlight their part of the route: [Ride activity pages](https://support.strava.com/hc/en-us/articles/216919577-Ride-Activity-Pages)); Google Maps Timeline (a day as a time-ordered list of places and moves next to its map: [Google Maps blog](https://maps.googleblog.com/2015/07/your-timeline-revisiting-world-that.html)); WCAG 1.1.1 non-text content (the list is the map's text equivalent, so the map is never the only way to read the shift: [Understanding 1.1.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-content.html)); WAI-ARIA button pattern for toggles (`aria-pressed`: [APG button](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/button/)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/trips.test.tsx` rewritten for the timeline (6: order and text with markers between start and end; manual and personal trips unmarked with purpose and "edited"; one pick at a time and let go; stops off reads business or personal; no trips and an error; the one-line reason with the route module off). `stops.test.tsx` waits for the Timeline region instead of Trips. All 6 fail without the change. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 117 files, 928 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium, 1280 and 390 px light; screenshots in the session scratchpad.
- Not done: stops in the timeline (W2d); the map side of the pick (W2e). Sorting trips by distance (the old table's column sort) is gone; the timeline is always in time order.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the time order is the useful one on this page; sorting stays in the exports.

## PR W2e — Web: the timeline and the map point at the same trip or stop (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2e-shift-map-links` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W2d-shift-timeline-stops`.
- Done: the route map marks **Start** and **End** (small labels, one above and one below the point, so a shift that ends where it began shows both) and every stop as a numbered dot, the same number and look as in the timeline; a low-confidence stop is paler (05 §3.1a). **Picking a trip** in the timeline draws its part of the route bold over the faded rest and frames it; **picking a stop** rings its dot and centres it (zoom 15 or closer); letting go frames the whole route again. **Clicking a stop on the map** picks it in the timeline, and the list scrolls inside its own box to show it (never the page). Movement is instant when the browser asks for reduced motion. The markers are hidden from screen readers: the timeline is the text equivalent and holds the same entries. The replay pin appears once the slider moves (at the start it hid the Start label). The Route card says "Numbers are the stops in the timeline. Pick a trip or a stop there to find it here." When there is no route (404, nothing recorded) the one line now ends "The timeline lists the trips and stops."; when the map cannot start, it says the timeline lists the same trips and stops. With the route module off the timeline takes the full width. The map is 18rem tall on phones, 24rem from 640 px. No API change: a trip's part of the route is cut from `GET /shifts/{ref}/route` by its start and end times (`segmentOf` in `map/route.ts`), stops carry their own position.
- Standards checked: Strava (hovering a segment highlights it on the map: [Ride activity pages](https://support.strava.com/hc/en-us/articles/216919577-Ride-Activity-Pages)); Driversnote trip view with start and end points ([review and edit trips](https://driversnote.helpscoutdocs.com/article/106-how-to-edit-a-trip)); WCAG 1.1.1 text equivalent ([Understanding 1.1.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-content.html)); WCAG 2.3.3 animation from interactions (`prefers-reduced-motion`: [Understanding 2.3.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/animation-from-interactions.html)); MapLibre markers with custom elements and `fitBounds`/`easeTo` ([MapLibre GL JS API](https://maplibre.org/maplibre-gl-js/docs/API/)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/routeview.test.tsx` 3 new (Start, End and stops at their positions, numbered in time order though sent out of order, the low one paler, markers hidden from screen readers; a picked trip fades the route, fills the picked line with its part and frames it, and a second press clears it; a stop clicked on the map is pressed in the timeline, ringed and eased to) and the scrub test checks the replay pin is off at the start; the stand-in for MapLibre records sources, paint, fits, eases and marker elements. `route.test.ts` 1 new (`segmentOf`). All fail without the change. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 117 files, 933 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 240 passed, 2 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium (Playwright cache, SwiftShader WebGL) on the Vite dev server with the API answered by a fixture (Toronto shift, 6 trips, 5 stops), OpenFreeMap tiles live: 390, 820 and 1280 px in light and dark, a picked stop at 390 and 1280 dark, a picked trip at 1280 light (bold between stops 1 and 2, the rest faded), route module off at 1280, no route at 390. At 820 px the sidebar is open and the map and timeline stack; from about 1100 px they sit side by side. Screenshots in the session scratchpad (`/tmp/claude-1000/-home-coder-Production-milewage/36d23177-8421-4bf2-8eda-bc483d8cb560/scratchpad/shots/final`).
- Not done: road snapping (plan 20, planned only); on phones MapLibre's compact attribution opens expanded over the bottom of the map until tapped (unchanged from D111); hovering a trip does not highlight it (a press does, which also works by keyboard and touch).

## PR W3a — Web: one scroll region at every width (owner request 2026-11-07)

- Branch `pr-W3a-scroll-fix` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X1-tracks-merged`.
- Found (headless Chromium, classic scroll bars on): at 820 and 1024 px wide the Expenses page scrolled sideways as a whole (page 1040 px in an 805 px window): the header, the cards and the description were cut off on the right and a page-wide scroll bar sat over the bottom of the sidebar. The shadcn `SidebarInset` is a flex item without `min-width: 0`, so a wide table's minimum width pushed the whole page area past the window and the table's own scroll box never engaged. At 390 px (no desktop sidebar) it was fine. Settings, below 1024 px: the eight section links were one row with `overflow-x-auto`, so a native scroll bar showed under them and Import, Alerts and Modules were off screen. In dark mode every native scroll bar was light grey on black (no `color-scheme`). With the window under about 600 px high the sidebar menu scrolled with its scroll bar hidden (`no-scrollbar`), cutting "Exports" in half with no sign there was more; folded to icons it could not scroll at all (`overflow-hidden`).
- Done: `SidebarInset` gets `min-w-0`, so a wide table scrolls inside its own box and the page never scrolls sideways. `color-scheme: light` / `dark` on the theme roots: scroll bars and native controls follow the theme. The sidebar menu shows a thin scroll bar when it is taller than the window, also when folded to icons (sideways overflow stays hidden). Settings: the section list is chosen by the settings area's own width (a Tailwind container query, so it also follows the sidebar being open or folded): at 672 px and wider a list beside the form that stays in view (and scrolls itself if the window is very short); narrower, one "Section: <name>" menu button whose items are links. Nothing in Settings scrolls sideways. The page itself stays the one scroll region for content, as shadcn's sidebar expects (the sidebar is fixed at the window height; the document scrolls; the header is sticky).
- Standards checked: shadcn's sidebar blocks let the document scroll beside a fixed `h-svh` sidebar ([sidebar-16](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/blob/main/apps/v4/registry/bases/radix/blocks/sidebar-16/page.tsx)); the same missing `min-w-0` reported and fixed in the shadcn Vue port ([shadcn-vue #1747](https://github.com/unovue/shadcn-vue/pull/1747)) and double scroll bars from the sidebar ([shadcn-ui #7365](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/issues/7365)); flex items' automatic minimum size ([MDN min-width](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/min-width)); [MDN color-scheme](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme) (scroll bars and form controls); settings section navigation: shadcn's own forms example is a row below `lg` that overflows the same way ([sidebar-nav](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/blob/79c054ac7a7b2a13acc09d72106fd40663cc9bee/apps/www/app/(app)/examples/forms/components/sidebar-nav.tsx)), shadcn-admin switches to a drop-down on small screens ([sidebar-nav](https://github.com/satnaing/shadcn-admin/blob/main/src/features/settings/components/sidebar-nav.tsx)); [Tailwind container queries](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/responsive-design#container-queries).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/layout.test.tsx` 4 (the page area can shrink; the sidebar scroll bar shows, also folded; `color-scheme` per theme; the settings nav never scrolls sideways, the menu names the open section, its items are links and open the section). All 4 fail without the change. `settings.test.tsx` passes unchanged.
- Visual check: headless Chromium (Playwright cache) against the real server bundle and the web dev server, seeded owner (Canada, km) and 25 expenses with long merchant names; 390×800, 820×700, 1024×700, 1440×900, light and dark, plus 900×520 with the sidebar open and folded. After: no page scrolls sideways at any size (Expenses 805/805 at 820, the table scrolls in its box); Settings shows the menu at 390 and 820 and the side list at 1024 and 1440; scroll bars are dark in dark mode; the sidebar shows its scroll bar at 520 px high. Screenshots in the session scratchpad, not in the repo.
- Not done: the settings structure itself (W3b).
- Expected conflicts: `SettingsPage.tsx` with O4 ("Run setup again" entry); none in `App.tsx` (not touched).

## PR W3b — Web: Settings in groups, one topic per section

- Branch `pr-W3b-settings-structure` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W3a-scroll-fix`.
- Done: the settings sections are in three groups, in the side list (with group headings) and in the narrow-screen menu (with group labels): **Preferences** (Profile and region, Units and format, Alerts), **Work and tracking** (Platforms, Tracking and privacy), **Data and account** (Import, Account, Server modules). Each section opens with its name as a heading and one line on what it is for; the cards no longer repeat the title, and the cards that came from other pages (password, import, platforms, alerts) now sit under it as level-3 headings ("Your platforms", "Tell me about" instead of repeating the section name). "General" is now "Profile and region" at `/settings/profile`; `/settings/general` still opens it (the account menu links there). "Tracking" is "Tracking and privacy" (stops, how long raw GPS points are kept, diagnostics); "Modules" is "Server modules". A section whose module is off is absent from the list and its address opens the first section; a group left empty is dropped. Same fields, same saving, no API change.
- Standards checked: grouped settings navigation with headings so the page has a proper heading outline ([Primer NavList](https://primer.style/product/components/nav-list/), used for GitHub settings); a settings area as one page per topic with a side list ([Vercel project settings](https://vercel.com/docs/project-configuration/project-settings), [shadcn forms example](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/blob/79c054ac7a7b2a13acc09d72106fd40663cc9bee/apps/www/app/(app)/examples/forms/components/sidebar-nav.tsx)); links between sibling pages of one section ([X-GOV.UK sub navigation](https://x-govuk.github.io/govuk-prototype-components/sub-navigation/), "Pages in this section"); plain section names that say what is inside.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/settings-structure.test.tsx` 3 (groups and order, the old `/settings/general` address; each section's heading and description; switched-off modules absent from the list and by address). All 3 fail without the change. `settings.test.tsx` and `layout.test.tsx` follow the new names; the platforms, alerts, account, import and module-flag tests pass unchanged.

The Save bar names the sections that hold unsaved changes ("Unsaved changes: Units and format, Tracking and privacy.") instead of "You have unsaved changes", since one Save sends them all.

Home Assistant's companion app and Nextcloud (plan 24 section 3) for "find your server, then connect".

## PR PO2a — Phone: QR scanner on ZXing, without Google ML Kit (plan 24 Q1)

- Branch `pr-PO2a-phone-zxing-scanner` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-PO1-phone-first-run-gate`.
- Done (owner decision Q1, plan 24 section 6): `expo-camera` is gone, and with it Google ML Kit's barcode scanner and Google's `datatransport` usage logging. A small Expo module, `native/qr-scanner`, shows the camera preview of [zxing-android-embedded](https://github.com/journeyapps/zxing-android-embedded) 4.3.0 (Apache-2.0, Journey Mobile and the ZXing authors) decoding QR codes only with [ZXing core](https://github.com/zxing/zxing) 3.5.4 (Apache-2.0; the `com.google.zxing` package name is the open-source ZXing project, not a Google service). It needs no Google Play services. The camera runs only while the scanner is open; the scanner closes when the app leaves the screen; a code is passed on once, and again only after 2 s (so a "wrong code" can be re-aimed). The library's own full-screen scan activity is removed from the manifest. Camera permission is asked with React Native's `PermissionsAndroid` (refused: "Ask again"; refused for good: "Open app settings"). The screen, viewfinder, torch and messages are unchanged. Plan 09 section 5.3 amended.
- Library choice: zxing-cpp's Android wrapper (`io.github.zxing-cpp:android`, Apache-2.0) decodes faster but needs CameraX, a camera preview written by us and native libraries per ABI; zxing-android-embedded brings its own preview in plain Java. It is in maintenance mode (last release 4.3.0), which is acceptable for reading one QR code; zxing-cpp is the fallback if it misbehaves on the phone.
- Standards checked: the library's README (Apache-2.0; SDK 24+ with ZXing core 3.4+, which is the app's minimum; `BarcodeView` needs the camera permission before `resume()` and hardware acceleration, on by default); the zxing-cpp wrapper page; scanbot's [vision-camera vs expo-camera](https://scanbot.io/blog/react-native-vision-camera-vs-expo-camera/) (every other Android option uses ML Kit or Play services).
- Proof there is no ML Kit: `test/manifest.test.ts` now fails when a dependency of the app is `expo-camera`, vision-camera or an ML Kit package, when a native module's Gradle file names ML Kit or datatransport, and (after a release build) when the merged manifest mentions them. The release APK's only Play-services files are `play-services-base`, `-basement`, `-location` and `-tasks` (fused location, plan 09); there is no `mlkit` or `transport` entry.
- Tests: `test/qrscanner.test.tsx` (camera asked first; a stray code says so and is not passed on; a pairing code is passed on once; refused offers Ask again; refused for good offers app settings); `manifest.test.ts` (above); JUnit `ScanGateTest` (once, again after the pause, never blank; a pairing QR made with ZXing reads back exactly). `npm run verify` green (vitest 848 passed, 1 skipped; phone 245 passed, with the release build present so the merged-manifest checks ran).
- Build: `scripts/phone-release-build.sh` (now also runs `:qr-scanner:testDebugUnitTest`) BUILD SUCCESSFUL; `apps/phone/android/app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk`, 108,231,115 bytes (four ABIs, debug key); JUnit qr-scanner 3 of 3, comma-tracker passed.
- Size: 302 + 28 changed lines (source and tests, without `package-lock.json`).
- Not checked: scanning on the phone (the owner tests); the preview in the full-screen modal on the S24.

## PR PO2c — Phone: connection checks and messages (plan 19 section 3.5, T4)

- Branch `pr-PO2c-phone-connect-checks` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-PO2b-phone-connect-screen`.
- Done (plan 19 section 3.5, which PO2 takes over as T4): **before connecting**, with no network call, the Connect screen refuses a code whose address is `localhost`, `127.x` or `[::1]` ("This code points at the computer that made it …") and, in a release build, an `http://` address ("This code uses an unsecured address (http) …"). **When connecting fails**: a `.ts.net` address with no VPN on the phone says "Tailscale is off on this phone. Open Tailscale, connect, then try again." with an **Open Tailscale** button; with a VPN on, "Your server is not answering through Tailscale …"; when the phone cannot tell, "Can't reach your server. Is Tailscale connected on this phone?" with the button; an answer without the `X-MileWage-Api` header that every MileWage answer carries says "That address answered, but it is not a MileWage server."; anything else keeps today's message. **Open Tailscale** opens the app, or its Play Store page (web page if there is no store) when it is not installed.
- `expo-network` is **not** used: its Android code checks cellular and Wi-Fi before VPN, and Android gives a VPN network the transports of the network under it, so Tailscale over Wi-Fi reads as "Wi-Fi". A small module, `native/net-check`, reads `TRANSPORT_VPN` of the active network (null with no network), whether Tailscale is installed, and opens it. Its manifest adds `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` (no prompt; already on the harmless list) and `<queries>` for `com.tailscale.ipn` only, so Android 11+ lets the app see that one app.
- Standards checked: [expo-network's Android source](https://github.com/expo/expo/blob/main/packages/expo-network/android/src/main/java/expo/modules/network/NetworkModule.kt) (type order cellular, Wi-Fi, …, VPN last); AOSP connectivity tests (a VPN over cellular has both `TRANSPORT_VPN` and `TRANSPORT_CELLULAR`); Android [package visibility](https://developer.android.com/training/package-visibility/declaring) (`<queries>` per package); [`NetworkCapabilities.hasTransport`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/net/NetworkCapabilities#hasTransport(int)).
- Tests: `test/connect.test.tsx` (tailnet address; localhost, 127.x, [::1] always and http only in release refused; each failure message; an answer without the MileWage header is `not_milewage`; on the screen, Tailscale off shows the message and opens Tailscale; a localhost code sends nothing); `manifest.test.ts` (the module asks to see Tailscale and nothing else; the merged release manifest can see it); JUnit `VpnStateTest` (a VPN over Wi-Fi is a VPN, Wi-Fi alone is not, no network cannot tell). Mock answers now carry `X-MileWage-Api` like the server's. `npm run verify` green (vitest 848 passed, 1 skipped; phone 253 passed).
- Build: `scripts/phone-release-build.sh` (now also runs `:net-check:testDebugUnitTest`) BUILD SUCCESSFUL; `apps/phone/android/app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk`, 108,254,227 bytes; JUnit net-check 3 of 3, qr-scanner 3 of 3, comma-tracker 18 of 18; the merged manifest has the `com.tailscale.ipn` query.
- Size: 349 + 23 changed lines (source and tests).
- Not checked on a phone: whether Android reports the VPN while on Wi-Fi with Tailscale (handled either way: null and both answers have a message).

## PR PO3b — Phone: permission steps after connecting (plan 24 screens 6 to 9)

- Branch `pr-PO3b-phone-permission-steps` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-PO3a-phone-connected`.
- Done: between Connected and Ready, one screen per step of the permission ladder (`permissions/ladder.ts` decides which steps are needed and in which order; a step already allowed is never shown): the location disclosure (Play's prominent disclosure, the same text as the Permissions screen), location while using, location all the time (explanation first, then **Open settings**, where Android 11+ shows "Allow all the time"; what happens without it), movement (activity recognition, which turns GPS off while standing still), notifications ("See your shift's time and distance in the notification bar."), and **Keep MileWage running** (Samsung's two settings first on a Samsung, otherwise the general setting and dontkillmyapp.com; **Open battery settings** opens the system page, no exemption permission, plan 09 section 5.3). Every step has **Not now**: the ladder gains `skip()`, and a skipped step stays undone. Coming back from system settings re-reads the permissions. Home's "Finish setting up" card now lists location, location all the time, notifications and Keep MileWage running (opening the battery page is remembered in the plain store, from either screen); the "Pair this phone" row is gone because Home exists only when connected.
- Standards checked: Android [background location](https://developer.android.com/develop/sensors-and-location/location/permissions/background) (ask for "while using" first, background separately after an explanation, in settings on Android 11+); Android [notification permission](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/notification-permission) (ask in context with the reason; a foreground service runs without it, but its notification then shows only in the task manager); [dontkillmyapp.com Samsung](https://dontkillmyapp.com/samsung) (battery Unrestricted / background restriction, Never sleeping apps); Google Play prominent disclosure (plan 09 section 5.6).
- Tests: `test/connected.test.tsx` (all steps in ladder order with the right OS requests and the battery page, then Ready; allowed steps are not shown; skipping everything leaves the items on Home's checklist); `permissions.test.tsx` (`skip` moves on and a later step stays reachable); `support/firstRun.ts` walks through setup for the other screen tests. `npm run verify` green (vitest 848 passed, 1 skipped; phone 262 passed).
- Size: 276 + 28 changed lines (source and tests).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): every step, including location, may be skipped with "Not now" (no dead end if Android's dialog is refused); Home's checklist and the Start shift check still require location.
- Not checked on a phone: the exact Samsung menu names on One UI 6/7 (taken from dontkillmyapp.com).

## PR PO4 — Server and web: Get the app, and a pairing QR any camera can open (plan 24 Q1 fallback, Q2)

- Branch `pr-PO4-web-get-the-app` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-PO3b-phone-permission-steps`.
- Done (owner decisions Q1 and Q2, plan 24 section 6):
  - **Which form the QR carries:** the https form, `<server>/pair#v=1&t=<token>` (new `pair_url` in `POST /pairing-tokens`). The MileWage app's scanner reads it (the phone now parses both forms; the base is the page's own address, so a `u=` added to the fragment is ignored), and any other camera app opens the server's **/pair** page. The token is in the fragment, so no server or proxy log sees it. `qr_payload` (`milewage://pair?...`) stays: it is the "Open in the MileWage app" link and the code text, and pasting either form works.
  - **/pair page** (web, public, no sign-in, outside the session; Caddy serves it as a web page): "Connect your phone to MileWage", **Open in the MileWage app** (the `milewage://` link, on Android), **Get the app** when the server has it, and what to do when the link is broken.
  - **APK from your own server:** `GET /api/v1/app/android.apk` (no sign-in; `HEAD` for "is it there") serves the file at `MILEWAGE_APK_PATH`, by default `milewage.apk` in the `config` volume, as `application/vnd.android.package-archive`; a 404 when there is no file. `infra/docs/install.md` section 3a: build with `scripts/phone-release-build.sh`, then `docker compose cp` it into place; replace it after an upgrade. No build pipeline.
  - **Devices page** (and a reusable `pairing/GetTheApp.tsx` for plan 23's "Pair your phone" step, O4): the QR shows `pair_url`; "Open MileWage on your phone and tap Scan the code"; **Get the app** only when the server has the file; **Open in the MileWage app** when the page is opened on an Android phone.
  - OpenAPI: `pair_url`, `downloadAndroidApp`, `checkAndroidApp` (module `devices`); `npm run openapi:generate`.
- Standards checked: [RFC 3986 section 3.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.5) and [MDN `URL.hash`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/URL/hash) (the fragment is not sent to the server); IANA media type `application/vnd.android.package-archive`; Android [unsafe use of deep links](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/risks/unsafe-use-of-deeplinks) (the app still validates the link and asks before connecting); P1g's note that Google Lens shows `milewage://` QR codes as text, which the https form avoids.
- Tests: server `devices.test.ts` (`pair_url` with the forwarded https host; the APK is a 404 for GET and HEAD until the file exists, then served with its type and file name, without sign-in); web `pairpage.test.tsx` (on a phone the link carries this server and the code; Get the app only when HEAD says so; a broken link says what to do; Caddy serves /pair) and `devices.test.tsx` (new wording, no buttons without the file or a phone, both with them), `caddy.test.ts`; phone `pairing.test.tsx` (the https form, a smuggled `u` ignored, a path prefix kept, `?` instead of `#` and another version refused). `npm run verify` green (vitest 853 passed, 1 skipped; phone 265 passed).
- Size: 265 + 20 changed lines of source and tests, plus 24 + 3 in `openapi.yaml`; generated types and docs not counted.
- Not done: plan 19's `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL` and Change address (T1–T3, T5); until then the QR address comes from the browser's address, as before.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the APK is served to anyone who can reach the server (no sign-in), because a phone downloads it before it is paired; it is the same file anyone can build from the repository.


## PR P1h — Phone: running-shift notification with live distance (Phone track)

- Branch `pr-P1h-phone-shift-notification` (on P1g). Owner decision 2026-11-07: a notification instead of Comma's floating pill (no `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`); 09 §5.10 added, §5.2 / §5.3 amended.
- Done: the foreground service's ongoing notification is the driver's live view while another app is on screen. Title "12.3 km this shift"; text with today's total (the shifts already ended today plus this one); a live clock in the header from the shift start; expanded, the start time and vehicle. Tap opens the app. On Android 16+ it asks to be a promoted ongoing notification (`setRequestPromotedOngoing`, `setShortCriticalText`), so the distance shows as a status-bar chip where the system allows it (`POST_PROMOTED_NOTIFICATIONS`, a normal permission older Android ignores; app.json and the manifest policy). Own silent channel `milewage_shift` (default importance, no sound or vibration; the old Comma channel is deleted); MileWage small icon in brand yellow. `androidx.core:core-ktx` 1.10.1 to 1.17.0 for the promoted-notification calls.
- The distance is counted **natively** (`LiveDistance.kt`), because the app's JS timers stop in the background: the same stages 1-3 as the engine's live accumulator on the same stored integers, seeded from the buffer after a service restart; approximate and never stored, like the Home figure. JS sends only the parts that do not change during a shift (`setNotice`: start time, unit, today's earlier shifts, footer; `src/shift/notice.ts`), on start, resume and restart; `stop()` clears them. Best effort: tracking does not depend on it.
- The Permissions screen's notifications-off warning now says what is lost: "Without notifications you will not see the time and distance of your shift while you use other apps."
- Tests: `ShiftNoticeTest.kt` (JUnit; counts like the shared engine's `createLiveAccumulator`, 1009 m on the same fixture with a noisy fix, a short step and a duplicate seq; texts in km and mi; safe defaults from JS), `test/notice.test.ts` (only today's ended shifts count, never the running one; set on start, cleared on end, set again after a restart; not set when the server's tracking module is off). `npm run verify` green (vitest 848 passed, 1 skipped; phone 239 passed, 2 skipped); release build (`scripts/phone-release-build.sh`) succeeds in about 6 minutes, APK 130 MB, JUnit 23 passed (ShiftNoticeTest 3, LiveDistanceTest 2); the merged release manifest test passes with `POST_PROMOTED_NOTIFICATIONS`.
- Size: about 520 added / 32 removed lines, over the 400-line rule because of the native code (about 340 lines in Kotlin and the module); the notification's parts are one feature and were not split.

## PR W4a — Web: dashboard greeting and earnings hero with Today / Week tabs (owner request 2026-11-07)

- Branch `pr-W4a-dashboard-hero` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X2-all-merged`.
- Owner request: "make it something similar to the Comma phone app dashboard". Structure and order are taken from the Comma phone app's Home screen (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`); the look is MileWage's shadcn/ui and tokens. Nothing from the Comma web app.
- Done: the page opens with a greeting as its heading, "Good morning / afternoon / evening, <display name>" (no name when none is set), and the date, both in the driver's time zone. Under it the **earnings hero** (18 §13): one tab row **Today · Week · Month · Year · 30 days** (Radix tabs: arrow keys, Home, End; the period words beside it, e.g. "Nov 2 – 7, 2026"), then a card "Earned, after app fees" with the big figure from `GET /summary` (`earnings_cents`, the effective earnings of track L). The line under it is Comma's: on Week "$119.60 today · 6 shifts this week", on Today "$722.50 this week · 6 shifts this week", on the longer periods the shift count. With shifts but nothing entered or labelled the figure reads "Not entered" with how to fix it ("Enter what the apps paid on a shift to see it here."; "Label your stops …" with the earnings module off; "Unavailable" with both off), never $0; with no shift at all it is $0.00. A failed request says why and offers **Try again**. The period picker of the page header is gone; the three metric cards stay below the hero for the chosen tab until W4b.
- Standards checked: NN/g, dashboards are for a glance and lead with the most important number ([Dashboards: Making Charts and Graphs Easier to Understand](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/dashboards-preattentive/)); Stephen Few, the first pitfall is going past one screen, and every number needs context ([Common Pitfalls in Dashboard Design](https://www.perceptualedge.com/articles/Whitepapers/Common_Pitfalls.pdf)); WAI-ARIA tabs pattern for the period switch ([APG Tabs](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/tabs/)), implemented by the shadcn/Radix `Tabs` already in the app; the dataviz rule "the one number a dashboard leads with is a hero figure" (no chart for a single value).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/dashboard.test.tsx` 4 new (greeting by time zone with and without a name; Week by default with today on the line, Today with the week on the line and the ranges asked; Month/Year/30 days as tabs; "Not entered" in the hero, $0.00 with no shift) and 2 changed (four regions now; the refusal test also presses Try again). `dashboard-extras.test.tsx`: the Today/Week test uses the tabs. Eight other tests found the page by the heading "Dashboard"; they now use `DASHBOARD` (the greeting) from `test/support/kit.tsx`, and recorded calls carry their query string. 8 of these tests fail on the old source. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 123 files, 967 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium (Playwright cache) on the Vite dev server with the API answered from a fixture (a week of six shifts in Toronto, earnings on five, a weekly goal, clock set to Sat 7 Nov 2026 15:30): 390, 820 and 1280 px, light and dark. Screenshots in the session scratchpad (`…/scratchpad/dash/before`, `…/dash/w4a`).
- Not done: tiles, deduction tip (W4b); layout, notices and goal card (W4c); the setup checklist redesign (W4d); recent shifts with route sketches (W4e); Add expense (W4f).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the dashboard opens on **Week** (Comma opens on Today). On the web the driver looks back after driving, and today is often empty.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the earlier periods (This month, This year, Last 30 days) stay as tabs after Today and Week, so nothing the dashboard showed is lost; Analytics still has its own periods.

## PR W4b — Web: dashboard tiles and the deduction tip (Comma's stats row)

- Branch `pr-W4b-dashboard-tiles` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4a-dashboard-hero`.
- Done: under the hero, Comma's three tiles for the chosen tab: **Shift time** ("22.8 h"), **Business distance** (active plus deadhead, in the driver's unit) and **Per hour** (earnings after app fees divided by shift hours). A tile that cannot be worked out shows "—" and why: "No shift time yet", "No earnings entered", or "Earnings are off". With stops on, one line under the tiles keeps the split: "Of that, 209.6 km active* and 128.4 km deadhead*. * Estimated from detected stops." Then Comma's write-off tip as MileWage's deduction: "Estimated deduction value: $243.36 on 338.0 km of business driving. It lowers your taxable income; it is not money paid to you. Open Tax" (the link only with the tax module). With the driver's own rate it says "Estimated deduction value (your rate)" (J8). No tip when the country has no rate or the value is 0. The three old metric cards, the total line and the page's own `/summary` request are gone: the hero, the tiles and the tip carry the same three metrics of 05 §4 (hourly earnings, the split marked as an estimate, the deduction value).
- Standards checked: as W4a; stat tiles as label and value with a short reason instead of a fake zero (dataviz "stat tile" contract; Few: numbers without context mislead, [Common Pitfalls in Dashboard Design](https://www.perceptualedge.com/articles/Whitepapers/Common_Pitfalls.pdf)); the estimate marker rule of 05 §3.1a kept.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/dashboard.test.tsx`: the three-metrics test now checks the tiles, the split line and the tip with its Tax link, and that the old cards are gone; the "not entered" test checks the Per hour reasons; "not available" (no rate pack) is now "no tip, and the own rate is named so"; the stops-off test also turns earnings off ("Unavailable", "Earnings are off", no marker). All four fail on W4a (5 of 9 in the file). `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 123 files, 967 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: as W4a, 390, 820 and 1280 px, light and dark (`…/scratchpad/dash/w4b`). At 390 px the tiles stay three across; values are one size smaller there so "1234.5 km" fits.
- Not done: layout, notices and goal card (W4c); setup checklist (W4d); recent shifts (W4e); Add expense (W4f).

## PR W4c — Web: dashboard layout, notices and the goal card

- Branch `pr-W4c-dashboard-layout` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4b-dashboard-tiles`.
- Done: **Notices** under the greeting, one line each with an icon and a link (Comma's banners, in MileWage's yellow-tinted alert): the region prompt (moved here unchanged, owner decision Q4); "2 shifts from the last 30 days have checks to review. Review shifts" (shifts with a `review` flag from `GET /shifts` for the last 30 days, as the "n to review" badge of W2a); a failed or overdue backup from unread notifications (`backup_failed`, `backup_overdue`, the Notifications page's own wording) with "Open Backups", only with the notifications module. A notice whose data cannot be read is not shown. **Goal card** (Comma's weekly goal card): "Weekly goal" (or daily, monthly, yearly), the goal's name, the share done as a large "72%", a meter on a pale track of the same yellow, "$722.50 of $1,000.00", the streak as "6-day streak" when the progress module runs and the streak is above 0, and "Open Goals". **Layout**: when the page area is at least 896 px wide (1280 px window with the sidebar open) the setup and goal cards form a 20rem side column beside the hero and the recent shifts; without either card the main column takes the width. Narrower (phones, 820 px with the sidebar open) it is one column: setup, hero, goal, shifts. The width is measured on the page area (ResizeObserver), so the document order always matches what is seen (each layout renders its own order) and the sidebar being open or folded is followed.
- Standards checked: NN/g dashboards (most important first, everything on one screen where it can be: [preattentive dashboards](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/dashboards-preattentive/)); shadcn dashboard blocks (cards in a grid beside the main content: [shadcn blocks](https://ui.shadcn.com/blocks)); a meter rather than a ring for one ratio to a target (dataviz "meter: the unfilled track is a lighter step of the same ramp"; NN/g on donuts in the same article); the progress bar keeps `role="progressbar"` with its value; WCAG 1.3.2 and 2.4.3 (reading and focus order follow the visual order: [Understanding 2.4.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-order.html)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/dashboard-extras.test.tsx` 3 new (streak with progress on, no request with it off; review and backup notices with their links and the 30-day request, none when there is nothing or notifications are off; side column when wide, setup first when narrow) and the goal test checks the period word, 50%, the meter value and the Goals link. 4 fail on W4b. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 123 files, 970 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: as W4a, 390, 820 and 1280 px, light and dark (`…/scratchpad/dash/w4c`).
- Not done: the checklist redesign (W4d); recent shifts (W4e); Add expense (W4f). Comma's backup banner is dismissible; here it goes away when the backup runs or the notification is read on the Notifications page (no new dismiss state).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): a ring (Comma) became a meter bar with the percentage beside it: the same information, easier to read, and the same bar as the Goals page.

## PR W4d — Web: "Finish setting up" redesigned (owner feedback 2026-11-07)

- Branch `pr-W4d-dashboard-setup` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4c-dashboard-layout`.
- Owner feedback on today's card ("there is no proper design or placement of this thing"): five plain rows, done ones struck through, floating in the middle of the page.
- Done: **Placement** (W4c): first card under the notices on phones and at 820 px; top of the side column beside the hero when wide; still gone once every visible step is done. **Design**: "Finish setting up", "2 of 5 done" and a progress bar (`role="progressbar"`, value 2 of 5). Each open step is a link to where it is done, with one line on why: Add your other apps → Settings › Platforms ("So each app's pay is kept apart."), Add your real vehicle → Vehicles, Set a weekly goal → Goals, Pair your phone → Devices, Run a backup → Backups. The first open step is highlighted and marked **Next**. Finished steps fold into "2 done" (a button with `aria-expanded`; open, they show with a check and "(done)" for screen readers) instead of struck-through rows. **Continue setup** (O4b, opens the wizard at the first unfinished step) stays the main button. **Hide for now** replaces the card with one line, "Setup steps hidden (3 left). Show them", in the same place; it is kept per browser in `localStorage` (`mw-setup-hidden`, every read and write in try/catch: with storage blocked the card shows, and hiding lasts until the page is opened again). Same server checks and module rules as before. The layout switch (W4c) gets a little slack going back to one column, so a scroll bar appearing cannot flip it back and forth.
- Standards checked: onboarding checklists: few steps, visible progress, the next step obvious, each step actionable, skippable or dismissible and resumable ([Appcues checklist best practices](https://docs.appcues.com/checklist-best-practices); [Shopify app onboarding](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/design/user-experience/onboarding): progress indicator, let merchants finish later); the Zeigarnik reason for keeping a visible bar ([Flook on onboarding progress bars](https://flook.co/blog/onboarding-progress-bar)); disclosure pattern for the folded list ([APG Disclosure](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/disclosure/)); plan 23 §4.4 (checklist for anything skipped, Continue setup).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/dashboard-extras.test.tsx`: the first test now checks "1 of 5 done", the bar value, every link with its why and address in order with Next on the first, the done step folded and shown by the button; new "Hide for now is kept in this browser and can be undone" (hide, reopen still hidden, Show them, storage cleared; storage that throws still shows the card). The module and disappear tests pass unchanged. Both fail on W4c. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 123 files, 971 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: as W4a, 390, 820 and 1280 px, light and dark (`…/scratchpad/dash/w4d`), hidden at 390 and 1280 in light and dark and folded list opened at 390 (`…/dash/w4d-hidden`), all steps done at 1280 (`…/dash/w4d/done-light-1280.png`: the goal card moves to the top of the side column).
- Not done: recent shifts (W4e); Add expense (W4f).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): "Hide for now" is per browser, not stored on the server (there is no setting for it); the steps link to the app's own pages rather than to single wizard steps, and Continue setup still opens the wizard.

## PR W4e — Web: recent shifts as Comma's rows

- Branch `pr-W4e-dashboard-recent` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4d-dashboard-setup`.
- Done: "Recent shifts" (the last five, newest first, "View all" to Shifts) now lists Comma's compact rows (`DashboardShifts.tsx`): the platforms of the shift (labels from `GET /platforms`, else the slug; "Shift" when nothing is entered), "1 to review" and "In progress" badges, then "Sat, Nov 7 · 10:00–13:24 · 52.0 km" (the three parts never break inside), and on the right what it earned after app fees (`GET /shifts/{ref}/earnings`, the sum of `effective`) with "$35.18 / h", or "—" and "not entered". Each row is one link to the shift. Earnings are read only when the row comes near the screen (IntersectionObserver, 200 px ahead) and only with the earnings module; a failure leaves the figure out. The empty list says "No shifts yet. Shifts are recorded on the phone." with "Pair your phone" (devices module). A failed list says why with **Try again** (before, the card disappeared). The deduction per shift and the active split per shift are no longer on the dashboard rows (the hero has the period's, the Shifts page has each shift's).
- Standards checked: Comma's "Recent sessions" row (platform, time, distance, earnings, per hour); lazy loading below the fold with IntersectionObserver ([MDN Intersection Observer API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Intersection_Observer_API)); one link per row, no nested controls ([WCAG 2.4.4 link purpose](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/link-purpose-in-context.html)); no dead ends (retry on failure).
- Tests: new `apps/web/test/dashboard-shifts.test.tsx` 3 (a row's platforms, review badge, date, time, distance, earnings, per hour, link and View all; "not entered", and no earnings request with the module off; the empty list with the pairing link, and a failed list retried). All 3 fail on W4d. `dashboard.test.tsx`: the hero's Try again is found inside the tab panel (the shifts card can show its own). `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 124 files, 974 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: as W4a, 390, 820 and 1280 px, light and dark (`…/scratchpad/dash/w4e`), and a new driver (no shifts, no vehicle, no name, no region) at 390 light.

## PR W4f — Web: route sketches on recent shifts, and Add expense

- Branch `pr-W4f-dashboard-sketch` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4e-dashboard-recent`.
- Done: each recent shift row ends with a small **sketch of the route** (Comma's route minimap; `map/RouteSketch.tsx`): `GET /shifts/{ref}/route` read when the row comes into view and only with the replay module, drawn as an SVG line, no basemap and no MapLibre map per row. The points are projected flat (longitude scaled by the cosine of the latitude), fitted into 64 × 48 keeping their shape (a straight east-west drive stays a line across, not a dot), and thinned to at most 150. Colours are the shift map's: route yellow over the Asphalt edge, the same in light and dark. The sketch is decorative (`aria-hidden`): the row's text says the date, time, distance and earnings, and the shift page has the map. No route (404) leaves an empty tile so the rows stay aligned; the tile is dropped on screens under 360 px. **Add expense** in the page header (Comma's bottom bar "Log expense"), only with the expenses module: it opens Expenses with the new-expense form already open (`/expenses?new`).
- Standards checked: WCAG 1.1.1, a decorative image is hidden from assistive technology when the text around it carries the information ([Understanding 1.1.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-content.html), [W3C decorative images](https://www.w3.org/WAI/tutorials/images/decorative/)); dataviz mark spec (2 px line, an edge under it so it reads on both surfaces, no legend for one series); brand route colours from `map/style.ts` (D111).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/dashboard-shifts.test.tsx` 2 new (the sketch is drawn with the route's points in the map's colours and hidden from screen readers, none for a 404, no request with replay off; `sketchPath` keeps a flat line flat and a square square, null for one point). `dashboard.test.tsx` 1 new (Add expense opens the form; absent with expenses off). Both page tests fail on W4e (the unit test needs the new file). `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 124 files, 977 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: final set in `…/scratchpad/dash/after`: 390, 820 and 1280 px in light and dark with a week of shifts; a new driver at 390 and 1280; setup finished at 1280 light and dark; goals off at 1280. Before: `…/dash/before`.
- Not done: "Add a past shift": the web has no way to create a shift (only the phone starts shifts; `POST /shifts` is the phone's start call), so it is left out rather than invented.

## PR PL4a — Platforms: the driver's own logo, server side (plan 25 section 7)

- Branch `pr-PL4a-platform-logo-server` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-PL3-no-bundled-logos`.
- Done: table `platform_logos` (migration `platforms/0002`: driver, slug, type, bytes as a BLOB of 256 KB at most, SHA-256, time). Module `platforms`: `PUT /platforms/{slug}/logo` (web session; PNG, JPEG or WebP as the raw body; first bytes must match the type; 413 over 256 KB; 415 for SVG or anything else; JPEG Exif removed with receipts' `stripGps`; built-in or own slug, else 404), `GET /platforms/{slug}/logo` (session or phone; `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, `no-store` as every API answer), `DELETE /platforms/{slug}/logo` (idempotent). Deleting a custom platform or resetting a built-in also removes its logo. `GET /platforms` gains `logo_version`. The routes sit in their own Fastify plugin so their 256 KB image parser does not touch receipts' 10 MB one. OpenAPI (three operations under `x-modules.platforms`, `PlatformLogo`, `logo_version`); `npm run openapi:generate`.
- Backups: the logos are database rows, so every backup carries them and a restore brings them back with no extra step.
- Standards checked: [OWASP File Upload Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/File_Upload_Cheat_Sheet.html), [MDN SVG as an image](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/SVG/Guides/SVG_as_an_image), [MDN nosniff](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/X-Content-Type-Options), [MDN CSP sandbox](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/sandbox), [SQLite internal versus external BLOBs](https://www.sqlite.org/intern-v-extern-blob.html); MileWage's receipts module (validation and serving reused).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/platform-logos.test.ts` (PNG stored and served with its headers, listed with a version; replace changes the version; JPEG Exif gone; WebP; remove and idempotent remove; a custom platform's logo goes with it; 415 plain text and SVG, 422 lying bytes, 413 too large, 404 unknown platform; 401 signed out, a phone reads but cannot delete; a 300 KB receipt still accepted; backup and restore on a fresh host carry the logo byte for byte; module off 404). `npm run verify` green.
- Not done: re-encoding images (no image library on the server; plan 25 section 7.2 says why); the web (PL4b).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): 256 KB per logo, no limit on how many (one per platform); not cached by the browser, as every API answer.
