## PR W5a — Web: shadcn Calendar date picker, on the service log

- Branch `pr-W5a-shadcn-calendar` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X2-all-merged`. Owner request 2026-11-07: the native date popup (white with blue highlights in dark mode) does not match the app; use shadcn's calendar.
- Done: shadcn **Calendar** and **Popover** added with the project's shadcn CLI (`npx shadcn add calendar popover`, style `radix-nova`, button kept as is), then Prettier. New **DateField** in `ui/form.tsx` beside `Field` and `SelectField`, built on shadcn's "Date picker with input": a text field showing the day for the driver (`Nov 7, 2026` in Canada and the US, `7 Nov 2026` in the UK: English with the driver's country, `Intl`) and a calendar button opening the Calendar in a Popover. The week starts on `settings.week_start`; "today" is today in the driver's time zone, not the browser's. Typing works: `2026-11-07`, numbers in the country's order with a four-digit year, or a month name (`Nov 7, 2026`, `7 November 2026`); anything else says "Not a date. Type it like Nov 7, 2026 or 2026-11-07." and gives the form no date. Alt+Down opens the calendar from the text field. The value stays `YYYY-MM-DD`; days become local dates at noon for the calendar and back by their parts (`src/dateText.ts`), never through `new Date('YYYY-MM-DD')`, which is UTC midnight. Optional `min` / `max` disable the days outside and refuse them when typed.
- Vehicle **service log**: Service date is a DateField. The form has two columns instead of five, because the five were too narrow inside the vehicles table for a date (or the Cost label) to show.
- Dependencies: `react-day-picker` 10.0.2 (MIT; what shadcn's registry installs today; peer `react >=16.8`, so React 19 is fine) and `date-fns` 4.4.0 (MIT; the shadcn calendar lists it, and react-day-picker uses it). About 20.6 kB gzip (68.7 kB minified) for DayPicker with what it uses of date-fns.
- Copied shadcn source (not counted): `apps/web/src/components/ui/calendar.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/ui/popover.tsx`.
- Standards checked: shadcn [Date Picker](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/date-picker) (Popover + Calendar; the "Input" variant so a date can still be typed; the "Time Picker" variant is a plain `Input type="time"`) and [Calendar](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/calendar); react-day-picker [upgrading to v10](https://daypicker.dev/upgrading) (`autoFocus` replaces `initialFocus`; `weekStartsOn`, `today`, `disabled` matchers) and [accessibility](https://daypicker.dev/guides/accessibility) (arrow keys, Page Up/Down, Home/End, Enter; `autoFocus` puts focus on the selected day or today when it opens); WAI-ARIA APG [date picker dialog](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/datepicker-dialog/) (a text field plus a button that opens a calendar dialog; Escape closes it and returns focus to the button); GOV.UK [date input](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/date-input/) (typing a known date is faster than picking it); MDN [`Intl.DateTimeFormat`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/DateTimeFormat) (`dateStyle: 'medium'` in UTC on both sides, so the shown day is the stored one).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/datefield.test.tsx` 8: typed days (ISO, the US and UK order, month names, impossible days refused) and formatting and daylight-saving days kept; on the service log: a text field showing today for the driver and no native date input; opens on click, picks a day, closes, sends `YYYY-MM-DD`; Monday or Sunday first by `week_start`; Escape closes and returns focus to the calendar button; a typed day is sent; text that is not a date is flagged. The 6 page tests fail without the page change. `maintenance.test.tsx` unchanged and passing. `npm run verify` exit 0 (vitest 124 files, 973 passed, 3 skipped; phone 271 passed, 4 skipped).
- Visual check: headless Chromium against a mocked API, the vehicle service log with the calendar open, light and dark, 390×900 and 1280×900: the popover uses the theme (dark popover, yellow selected day, muted outside days), Monday first. At 390 the date text is cut at the end, because on a phone the vehicles table squeezes its first column (as before this PR).
- Size: about 370 changed lines of source and tests (without the lockfile and the two copied files).
- Not done: Exports, Odometer, Expenses and the Schedule time fields (W5b).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the date is shown in English for the driver's country (`en-CA`, `en-US`, `en-GB`, …), because Settings has no language or date format; the rest of the app formats with `en-CA`.

## PR W5b — Web: every date field on the calendar; time fields follow the theme

- Branch `pr-W5b-date-fields` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W5a-shadcn-calendar`.
- Done: W5a's **DateField** (shadcn Calendar in a Popover, with typing) replaces the last native date inputs: Exports **From** and **To**, Odometer **Date**, Expenses **Date** and **Next due**. Each form keeps its checks and messages (Exports "Choose both dates." and the 366-day limit, Odometer's refusal of a future day). No `type="date"` is left in the web app.
- **Time fields** (Schedule: From, To, Template from, Template to) stay native `<input type="time">`, which is what shadcn's own "Time Picker" example uses (a plain `Input type="time"`; shadcn has no time picker component). They already follow the theme: `color-scheme: light` on `:root` and `dark` on `.dark` (W3a, tested in `layout.test.tsx`), so Chromium draws the field, its clock icon and its popup dark in dark mode; Firefox and Safari have no time popup. Checked in headless Chromium: the computed `color-scheme` of a time field is `dark` in dark mode and `light` in light mode. No code change was needed.
- Test helper: `pickDay(label, day)` in `test/support/kit.tsx` opens a date field's calendar and clicks a day.
- Standards checked: as W5a; shadcn [Date Picker, Time Picker example](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/date-picker); MDN [`color-scheme`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme) (form controls and their popups use the dark scheme) and [`<input type="time">`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/input/time) (12- or 24-hour display follows the browser's locale, not the page).
- Tests: `exports.test.tsx` (From and To picked on calendars, sent as `from=2026-02-20&to=2026-03-09`; no native date input), `odometer.test.tsx` (a day picked on the calendar is recorded at local midnight in the driver's time zone), `expenses.test.tsx` (Date and Next due picked on calendars, sent as `YYYY-MM-DD`). All 3 fail without the change; the earlier tests that type `YYYY-MM-DD` into these fields pass unchanged. `npm run verify` exit 0 (vitest 124 files, 976 passed, 3 skipped; phone 271 passed, 4 skipped).
- Visual check: headless Chromium against a mocked API, light and dark, 390×900 and 1280×900: Exports (From open), Odometer (Date open), Expenses (Date open, dark 1280), Schedule (time fields, no popup can be captured headless).
- Size: about 110 changed lines of source and tests.
- For the owner: a native time field shows 12- or 24-hour time by the browser's language, not by Settings' **Time format** (headless Chromium in English (US) showed "05:00 PM" with Time format 24h). A text time field honouring `time_format` would be its own change.

## PR W2f — Web: a shift with nothing recorded, and a running shift, explained in one card (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2f-shift-sparse-states` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X2-all-merged`.
- Why: the owner (2026-11-07, screenshot of the old page) found a shift with 0 km, no route, no trips and no stops unorganised: a "No route" line, empty cards, and the checks as codes far from the reason.
- Done: an ended shift with no trips, no stops and no route points (or the route module off) shows **one card in the map's place**: "No route for this shift" (with the route module off, "No trips for this shift"), what happened ("The phone sent no location points for this shift, so there is no map, and no trips or stops."), "It lasted under 3 minutes, so it may have been started by mistake. With no distance it adds nothing to your totals." when that is so, **What the checks found** in plain words (`no_stops_detected` is left out there: it adds nothing when nothing was recorded) and **What you can do**. The card holds the checks, so the separate Checks card is not shown and the "n to review" link points at it. The timeline says "Start and end only" and lists just those two rows. A **running shift** shows "Shift in progress" in the map's place: the phone sends its positions in batches, about once a minute, and the route, trips and stops are worked out when the shift ends and appear then; earnings once it has ended. Its route is not asked for (decision 1.10: no live map) and it shows no checks. The active and deadhead lines under Business (and the "estimated" note) are left out while the shift has no distance. The **Checks** card now ends with **What to do**, each piece of advice once: the phone's Permissions screen ("Allow all the time", battery "unrestricted": the phone's own words, `ladder.ts`) for few positions, interruptions and signal gaps; open MileWage with a connection when the phone's result never arrived; automatic date and time for clock skew; a clear view of the sky for imprecise positions; ending shifts on the phone for an auto-closed shift. The route is read by the page (`useShiftRoute` in `map/RouteView.tsx`) so the page knows when there is none; `RouteView` takes it as a prop. A shift with trips but no route keeps the W2e note.
- Standards checked: NN/g, empty states in complex applications (say what the system state is, teach, give a direct path to the task: [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/empty-state-interface-design/)); Carbon empty states (the empty state replaces the element it stands for, text-only when several can appear, a reason and a next step: [Carbon](https://carbondesignsystem.com/patterns/empty-states-pattern/)); Atlassian empty-state writing (say why it is empty and where to go next, no dead end: [Atlassian](https://atlassian.design/foundations/content/designing-messages/empty-state)); Material's original empty-states pattern ([Material 1](https://m1.material.io/patterns/empty-states.html)); background location needs "Allow all the time" and unrestricted battery use on Android ([Locato help](https://locatoweb.com/help/android), [Don't kill my app](https://dontkillmyapp.com/)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/shiftstates.test.tsx` 3 new (the owner's case: one card with what happened, the short-shift line, the checks without `no_stops_detected`, the two pieces of advice, no Route or Checks card, no zero split, timeline start and end only; route module off: "No trips for this shift" and no route request; a running shift: the in-progress card, start and "now", no checks, no route request). `shifts.test.tsx`: the checks test reads the Checks list and the "What to do" line. `trips.test.tsx`: no trips and no route now gives the card. All of them fail without the change. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 124 files, 968 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium on the Vite dev server with the API answered by fixtures (the owner's case: 01:35, 40 s, `low_fix_density`, `no_stops_detected`, phone missing; a running shift; the W2e Toronto shift), 1280 and 390 px dark. Screenshots in the session scratchpad (`shots/sparse/w2f`).
- Not done: the earnings, odometer, notes and export cards are unchanged here (W2g folds them for a shift with little in it); an empty "Label stops" card still shows (W2g).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): under 3 minutes with no distance is "maybe started by mistake"; deleting such a shift is not offered here (the server allows it only with a confirmation for shifts under 7 years old, so it stays a deliberate step).

## PR W2g — Web: odometer, notes and export folded into "More" on a shift with little in it (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2g-shift-more` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W2f-shift-sparse-states`.
- Done: on a shift with little in it (nothing recorded, still running, or no stops), Odometer, Notes and Export are **one "More for this shift" card** with a row each (name and one line: "Start and end readings, if you noted them", the note itself or "None yet", "Download this shift as a file"); a row opens in place (`<details>`/`<summary>`, so it works by keyboard and screen reader with no script). Notes starts open when the shift has a note. A running shift has no Export row (nothing to export yet). The Checks card (when the shift has one, W2f) sits above More, beside Earnings on wide screens. The **Label stops card is left out when there is no stop** (it said only "No stops were detected."; the timeline already says "0 stops"), but stays when a label lost its stop. A shift with stops keeps the W2e layout card for card. Notes moved into `ShiftNotes` (`ShiftStates.tsx`); `Section` takes `bare` (no card, no title) and `ShiftOdometer` passes it, for use inside More.
- Standards checked: NN/g, progressive disclosure (show the few things most people need, the rest one clear step away, no more than two levels: [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/)); Carbon empty states (consider how many empty states can appear in one space; do not show an empty structure: [Carbon](https://carbondesignsystem.com/patterns/empty-states-pattern/)); the disclosure widget is HTML's own ([MDN `<details>`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/details), [WAI-ARIA disclosure pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/disclosure/)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/shiftstates.test.tsx` 4 new (the owner's case: one More region with three closed rows, no Export or Stops card, notes saved from inside More; a running shift with a note: Notes open, no Export; trips but no stops: Checks kept, rest folded, no Label stops; a shift with stops keeps Odometer, Export and Label stops and has no More, a guard that passes before and after). `stops.test.tsx`: no stops gives no Label stops card. The first three and the stops test fail without the change. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 124 files, 972 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium, fixtures as W2f plus a 9-minute shift with one trip and no stops; 1280 and 390 px dark; the W2e Toronto shift is unchanged. Screenshots in the session scratchpad (`shots/sparse/w2g`).

## PR W2h — Web: a compact earnings block when nothing is entered yet (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2h-shift-earnings-compact` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W2g-shift-more`.

## PR W2i — Web: add a trip by hand on the shift page (shift detail rework)

- Branch `pr-W2i-shift-add-trip` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W2h-shift-earnings-compact`.
- Why: for a shift the phone did not record, the only fix the page could suggest was on the phone. The API already takes a trip typed by hand (`PUT /shifts/{ref}/trips/{client_id}`, source manual, 08 API spec; it counts in the totals and raises `manual_trip_present`), but the web had no form for it.
- Done: **Add a trip** opens a small form in the Timeline card: From and To (the shift's start and end by default, sent to the millisecond when left as they are; a To before From is the next day), Distance in the driver's unit, Kind (Business or Personal) and Purpose (the server requires one). It shows in the card that explains an empty shift ("If you know the distance (from the odometer or the platform), add it by hand.") and, once the shift has trips, at the top of the Timeline card; only on an ended shift (not running, not reviewed: the server refuses a finalized one). After saving, the page takes the recomputed shift, so the figures, the timeline (the trip marked "edited") and the layout follow. The trip id is a random version 4 UUID made with `crypto.getRandomValues` (`crypto.randomUUID` needs HTTPS, which a home server may not have). The test kit's fake server answers a key ending in `/*` for a made-up last path segment.
- Standards checked: Driversnote and MileIQ let a driver add a missed trip by hand with its distance and purpose ([Driversnote, add a trip manually](https://driversnote.helpscoutdocs.com/article/106-how-to-edit-a-trip), [MileIQ, how to add a drive](https://support.mileiq.com/hc/en-us/articles/204473535-How-to-add-a-drive)); a mileage log needs the date, distance and business purpose of each trip ([IRS Publication 463, section 5](https://www.irs.gov/publications/p463), [CRA, motor vehicle records](https://www.canada.ca/en/revenue-agency/services/tax/businesses/topics/sole-proprietorships-partnerships/business-expenses/motor-vehicle-expenses/motor-vehicle-records.html)); [RFC 9562](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9562) (UUID version 4 bits).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/addtrip.test.tsx` 4 new (from the empty shift's card: the PUT with the shift's own times, 12 500 m, kind and purpose, then the trip in the timeline, the card gone and the timeline offering the next trip; a distance that is not a number is refused without a request, offered once on an empty shift, Cancel closes; not offered on a running or reviewed shift, a guard; `uuid` gives version 4 ids). The first two fail without the change; the `uuid` test fails without the file. `npm run verify` exit 0: vitest 125 files, 977 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 271 passed, 4 skipped.
- Visual check: headless Chromium, the owner's case with the form open and closed, 1280 and 390 px dark; the W2e Toronto shift at 1280 shows the button at the top of its Timeline card and is otherwise unchanged. Screenshots in the session scratchpad (`shots/sparse/w2i`, `shots/sparse/after`).
- Not done: editing or deleting a hand-made trip on the web (the API has `PATCH` and `DELETE /trips/{id}`); a time typed across a daylight-saving change can be off by the hour shift.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): a trip added by hand is Business (active) or Personal; deadhead is not offered (the engine's estimate, not something a driver types).

No phone file changed, so no release build.

## PR T1 — Infra: Caddy keeps the https address that Tailscale sends (plan 19 T1)

- Branch `pr-T1-caddy-trusted-proxies` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-X3-web-merged`. Owner request 2026-11-07: "On the web it needs a prompt for me to use Tailscale. Is it Tailscaled by default?" (no: plan 19 sections 3.1 to 3.4; T1 to T3 and T5 build them).
- Done (plan 19 P1 and P5): Caddy's global block trusts exactly one sender, the Docker gateway of the stack's network: `trusted_proxies static {$MILEWAGE_PROXY_GATEWAY:172.29.88.1}/32`. `tailscale serve` on the host reaches Caddy through that gateway, so its `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, `X-Forwarded-Host: <host>.ts.net` and the phone's `X-Forwarded-For` now reach the API instead of being replaced by `http` and `127.0.0.1:8080`. New setting **`MILEWAGE_PROXY_GATEWAY`** (default `172.29.88.1`): the compose file passes it to Caddy and also sets it as the network's gateway, so the two cannot drift; `install.sh` writes it next to the subnet (the subnet's first address). Spec 10 section 8.1 and `install.md` section 3 no longer say "unverified": they name the gateway.
- Standards checked: Caddy [global option `trusted_proxies`](https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/options#trusted-proxies) and [reverse_proxy defaults](https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/reverse_proxy#defaults) (forwarded headers from an untrusted sender are replaced; from a trusted one they are kept and the sender is appended); [Caddyfile environment placeholders](https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/concepts#environment-variables) `{$VAR:default}`; Compose [network `ipam` gateway](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/networks/#ipam); plan 19 Q5 (gateway only, not every private range).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/caddy.test.ts` 2 new (the global block trusts exactly the gateway placeholder; compose passes the same gateway to Caddy and to the network, with the same default); `infra/install.test.mjs` 1 new and 1 extended (the `.env` holds `MILEWAGE_PROXY_GATEWAY=172.29.88.1`, and `10.77.5.1` for `--subnet 10.77.5.0/24`). All fail without the change.
- Behaviour check (by hand, `caddy:2.8.4-alpine`, the stack's image, two throwaway containers on their own network and port): a request to the published loopback port with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, `X-Forwarded-Host: mybox.tail1234.ts.net`, `X-Forwarded-For: 100.64.0.7` reached the upstream as `proto=https host=mybox.tail1234.ts.net for=100.64.0.7, 172.29.99.1`; with the old Caddyfile as `proto=http host=127.0.0.1:18091 for=172.29.99.1`. The API keeps trusting `X-Forwarded-For` only from the proxy subnet, which holds the gateway, so per-IP limits now see the tailnet address.
- Not done: the smoke regression "https QR through Caddy" (plan 19 section 6) needs a request that shows the address without signing in; it is added with A1's public `GET /api/v1/app` (its `page_url`).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): a process on the host itself can also send forwarded headers to `127.0.0.1:8080` and be believed. Same trust as `tailscale serve`; anyone with a shell on the host already controls the server.

## PR T2 — Server: one known server address for pairing codes (plan 19 T2)

- Branch `pr-T2-public-url` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-T1-caddy-trusted-proxies`.
- Done (plan 19 section 3.2, P2 and P3): new setting **`MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL`** (no default; the compose file passes it to the API, `install.md` section 3 step 5 says where to set it). It must be an absolute `https://` address with nothing after the host name (no path, query, fragment or user name); plain `http://` only when `NODE_ENV` is not `production`. A wrong value stops the server at start with a sentence naming the setting. `POST /pairing-tokens` takes an optional body `{ "base_url": "https://…" }` (the driver's correction from the web, same rules, 422 `validation_failed` with the reason otherwise) and answers two new fields: `base_url` (the address inside the code, without a trailing slash) and `base_url_source` (`driver`, `configured` or `request`). Both the QR (`pair_url`) and the `milewage://` link use that address. Without the setting and without a correction it is the browser's address from the forwarded headers, as before. OpenAPI and generated types updated; spec 08 section 2.4 amended.
- Order: **the driver's correction, then `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL`, then the request**. Plan 19 section 9 listed the configured address first; a driver who types an address and presses Make a new code would otherwise get the old address back with no explanation. Plan 19 section 9 updated. **Assumption (owner to confirm).**
- Standards checked: [WHATWG URL Standard, origin](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-origin) (the address is reduced to scheme, host and port; `https://Box.ts.net:443/` becomes `https://box.ts.net`); [RFC 3986 section 3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2.1) (user information in a URL is deprecated: refused); the [Twelve-Factor App, config](https://12factor.net/config) (deployment address in the environment, like `MILEWAGE_MODE`); self-hosted apps with the same setting: Nextcloud [`overwrite.cli.url`](https://docs.nextcloud.com/server/latest/admin_manual/configuration_server/config_sample_php_parameters.html#overwrite-cli-url), Immich [External domain](https://docs.immich.app/administration/system-settings) (used for shared links), Home Assistant [External URL](https://www.home-assistant.io/docs/configuration/basic/#external_url).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/devices.test.ts` 3 new and 1 extended (the request's address is reported as `request`; with `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL` the code carries it while the browser is on a LAN address, source `configured`; the driver's address is normalised and used, source `driver`, http accepted outside production, six bad values refused with 422 and no token stored; a production server refuses http); `apps/server/test/index.test.ts` 1 new (empty is unset, the trailing slash is dropped, http only in development, a path is refused). All fail without the change.
- Not done: the web side (T3), the install script writing the setting (T5).

## PR T3 — Web: "Your phone will connect to", Change address, and the Tailscale warning (plan 19 T3)

- Branch `pr-T3-web-address` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-T2-public-url`. Answers the owner's "On the web it needs a prompt for me to use Tailscale".
- Done (plan 19 section 3.3) in the shared `PairingCode` (`pages/Devices.tsx`), so the Devices page and the setup wizard's **Pair your phone** step both have it:
  - Under the QR: **"Your phone will connect to https://… (set on the server / the address this page is open at / you changed it)"**, from T2's `base_url` and `base_url_source`.
  - **Change address**: a field filled with the current address and **Make a new code**, which asks for a new code with `base_url`; the server's reason shows under the field when it refuses ("The address must start with https://"). Not a nested `<form>` (the wizard step is already one): Enter in the field does the same.
  - A **warning box before the QR** (`pairing/address.tsx`, `addressProblem`): `http://` "Phones only connect over https. Open MileWage at your Tailscale address (https://…ts.net) and pair from there."; `localhost`, `127.x`, `::1` "This address only works on this computer. Your phone cannot reach it. Set up Tailscale first…"; a bare IP over https "This address only works on this network… Set up Tailscale first". Each points to the install guide, section 3, named as a file (there is no public guide URL yet; to-do list). For a this-computer address the QR and the "tap Scan the code" line are not shown at all (a phone can never use it); Change address stays.
  - The `milewage://` text moved behind **Show code text** (`<details>`).
  - At 390 px the text column now wraps under the QR (it was squeezed beside it and the page scrolled sideways once the address line was added).
- Standards checked: GOV.UK [warning text](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/warning-text/) (a short bold title, then what to do) and WAI-ARIA [`note`](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#note) rather than `alert` (it is shown with the code, nothing changed under the driver); [RFC 6761 section 6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6761#section-6.3) (`localhost` and its subdomains are this machine) and [RFC 1122 section 3.2.1.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1122#section-3.2.1.3) (127.0.0.0/8 is loopback); Android [cleartext traffic](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-config#CleartextTrafficPermitted) (the release app refuses `http://`, P1); HTML [`details`/`summary`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/interactive-elements.html#the-details-element) for the code text; HTML forms cannot be nested ([content model of `form`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/forms.html#the-form-element)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/devices.test.tsx` 4 new (the address line and its source, no warning for a `.ts.net` address, Show code text; Change address: a refused address shows the server's reason, a good one makes a new code with `base_url` trimmed, shows "(you changed it)" and closes the field; a warning before the code for `http://192.168.1.20:8080`, `http://localhost:5173` (no QR) and `https://192.168.1.20`); `apps/web/test/pairing-address.test.ts` 11 new (which addresses are local, http, bare IP or fine, IPv6 included); the onboarding phone test's mock carries the new fields. All fail without the change.
- Visual check (headless Chromium, Vite dev server, mocked API; `…/scratchpad/getapp/devices-{ts,http,local}-{light,dark}-{390,1280}.png`): the Tailscale address with Change address open, the http warning above the QR, and the this-computer warning without a QR; no sideways scroll at 390 px.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): for a `localhost` or `127.x` address the QR is replaced by the warning, not shown under it (plan 19 section 3.3 says "before the QR"; the owner asked for no useless QR on the Get the app step, and the same holds here). For `http://` and bare IPs the QR stays, because a debug build on the home Wi-Fi can still use it.

## PR A1 — Server: what the app download page shows, the store link, and publishing the latest build

- Branch `pr-A1-app-info` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-T3-web-address`. Owner request 2026-11-07: "On the setup I need a page where either the server serves the latest APK from the local server, or later when I publish the app, opens the Play Store link. Scan to download it locally." Built as A1 (server, build script, guide), A2 (the public download page) and A3 (the wizard step), each under 400 lines.
- Done:
  - **`GET /api/v1/app`** (module `devices`, no sign-in: the phone has no account yet; `Cache-Control: no-store`): `{ apk: { size_bytes, updated_at, version } | null, store_url, page_url, base_url, base_url_source }`. `apk` is the file at `MILEWAGE_APK_PATH`, **looked at on every request**, so a new build put in place is offered at once, without a restart; `updated_at` is the file's time; `version` comes from `<file>.json` (a short version string, anything else is `null`). `page_url` is `<address>/app`, the download page A2 adds, with the same address rules as pairing codes (T2: `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL`, else the request's forwarded address). The APK route `GET`/`HEAD /api/v1/app/android.apk` is unchanged.
  - New setting **`MILEWAGE_APP_STORE_URL`** (no default; compose passes it): the app's store page once published, which must be an `https://` link (Google's format is `https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=<package>`; the package is `app.milewage.phone`). A wrong value stops the server at start with a sentence naming the setting.
  - **Latest build:** `scripts/phone-release-build.sh --publish <path>` (or `MILEWAGE_APK_PUBLISH=<path>`) copies the built app to `<path>` through the new `scripts/publish-apk.sh`, which also works alone on an app already built. It copies to `<path>.part` and renames (a phone downloading at that moment never gets half a file) and writes `<path>.json` with the version from `apps/phone/app.json` and the build time. Other arguments still go to Gradle. For `npm run dev` the path is `.dev/config/milewage.apk`.
  - `install.md` section 3a rewritten: the download page, the store setting, `--publish` for dev and for the Docker stack, "Install unknown apps".
  - Smoke (`infra/docker/smoke.sh`): plan 19's regression for T1. `GET /api/v1/app` through Caddy with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` and `X-Forwarded-Host: box.tail1234.ts.net` must answer `page_url` `https://box.tail1234.ts.net/app`.
- Standards checked: Google Play [linking to a store listing](https://developer.android.com/distribute/marketing-tools/linking-to-google-play) (`https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=<package_name>`); IANA media type `application/vnd.android.package-archive` (unchanged); write-then-rename for a file other processes read ([POSIX `rename`](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9699919799/functions/rename.html) replaces the target atomically on one file system); how self-hosted servers point to their own phone app: Home Assistant's [Companion app download](https://companion.home-assistant.io/docs/getting_started/), Nextcloud's [Android manual](https://github.com/nextcloud/android/blob/master/user_manual/android_app.rst) (the first-login screen and the web's personal page link the apps), Immich ([F-Droid listing](https://f-droid.org/packages/app.alextran.immich/), APKs on its GitHub releases): all link stores first and offer a direct APK as the other way; HTTP [`Cache-Control: no-store`](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111#section-5.2.2.5) so a new build shows at once.
- Tests: `apps/server/test/devices.test.ts` 2 new (no sign-in, `no-store`, `apk: null` and the request's address; the Tailscale address through forwarded headers; a file put in place while running is offered with its size and time, the version from the `.json`, an odd version is `null`; with the store setting and `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL` both are offered, source `configured`); `apps/server/test/index.test.ts` 1 new (the store setting is unset or an https link; `http://`, `market://` and a bare host stop the server); `scripts/publish-apk.test.mjs` 2 new (copy, version and build time, no `.part` left; a missing file exits 1 and writes nothing). All fail without the change. `infra/docker/smoke.sh` run: OK (the stack built and healthy, the https address kept); with T1's `trusted_proxies` line removed it fails with `page_url` `http://127.0.0.1:18080/app`, so it guards plan 19 P1.
- Not done: the download page itself (A2) and the wizard step (A3). The Play badge artwork is not added (A2 explains).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the download page's information (file size, version, the server's own address) is public to anyone who can reach the server, like the APK itself (PO4's assumption).

## PR A2 — Web: the public app download page (`/app`)

- Branch `pr-A2-download-page` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-A1-app-info`.
- Done: **`/app`**, a public web page (no sign-in, outside the session, like `/pair`; Caddy and `spaPaths` list it), which the setup wizard's QR (A3) opens on the phone. Title "Get the app — MileWage", heading **Get MileWage for Android**, one line on what the app does, then from A1's `GET /api/v1/app`:
  - store link set: **Get it on Google Play** first (a full-width button; "Get it from the app store" for a store that is not Google Play), then "Or download it directly:" and the server's file as the second button;
  - only the server's file: **Download MileWage** (`/api/v1/app/android.apk`, `download="milewage.apk"`), "Version 0.1.0 · 108 MB · Nov 7, 2026" (size in megabytes as Android shows them, date in the phone's language), and **Installing the download** in three steps: open the file; the first time, Android asks to let the browser install apps, "tap Settings, turn on Allow from this source, then go back"; tap Install;
  - neither: "This server has no app to download yet. Put one there first: see the install guide, section 3a."; a server error says to reload.
  - Ends with "Next: on your computer, open MileWage and scan the pairing code with the app."
- **Get the app** on the Devices page, the wizard's phone step and the `/pair` page now opens this page instead of downloading the file at once, so the driver also gets the store link and the install steps; it shows when the server offers the app either way (`GET /app` instead of `HEAD` on the file).
- **No Google Play badge artwork yet.** Google's [badge guidelines](https://partnermarketinghub.withgoogle.com/brands/google-play/google-play/lockups-icons-badges/#badges) require the current official artwork, unchanged, at least 28 px high with a quarter of its height as clear space, downloaded from Google's Partner Marketing Hub; loading it from Google's servers would tell Google about every visit to the driver's server (no third-party requests, plan 16). Until the app is published (no `MILEWAGE_APP_STORE_URL` yet), the page uses a plain button with the words "Get it on Google Play". When it is published: add the official badge file to the repository and replace the button. **Assumption (owner to confirm).**
- Standards checked: Android 8's per-app [Install unknown apps permission](https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2017/08/making-it-safer-to-get-apps-on-android-o.html) (granted to the app that opens the file, here the browser; Settings › Apps › Special app access › Install unknown apps › Allow from this source; the system's own prompt sends the user there); Google Play [store listing link](https://developer.android.com/distribute/marketing-tools/linking-to-google-play); self-hosted apps offer the store first and a direct APK second (Home Assistant [Companion app](https://companion.home-assistant.io/docs/getting_started/), Nextcloud [Android manual](https://github.com/nextcloud/android/blob/master/user_manual/android_app.rst), Immich on [F-Droid](https://f-droid.org/packages/app.alextran.immich/) and its GitHub releases); HTML [`download` attribute](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/links.html#attr-hyperlink-download); [`rel="noopener noreferrer"`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/links.html#link-type-noreferrer) on the store link (the server's address is not sent to the store); W3C WAI page titles ([WCAG 2.4.2](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/page-titled)).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/apppage.test.tsx` 4 new (the server's file with its link, `download` name, "Version 0.1.0 · 108 MB · Nov 7, 2026", the install steps, no Google Play, the page title and the `GET /api/v1/app` request; Google Play first and the file second; another store named plainly and no install steps without a file; nothing offered says what to do; Caddy serves `/app`); `pairpage.test.tsx`, `devices.test.tsx` and `onboarding.test.tsx`: Get the app opens `/app` and is offered from `GET /app`; `caddy.test.ts` expects `/app`. All fail without the change.
- Visual check (headless Chromium, Vite dev server, mocked API): `…/scratchpad/getapp/page-{apk,both,none}-{light,dark}-390.png`: the file only, Google Play plus the file, and nothing offered; no sideways scroll.

## PR T5 — Infra: the install script's Tailscale step (plan 19 T5)

- Branch `pr-T5-install-tailscale` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-A3-wizard-get-the-app` (needs T2's `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL`).
- Done (plan 19 section 3.4, with the recommended answer to Q1, "ask, then run it"): after the stack is healthy, `infra/install.sh`
  1. without the `tailscale` command: prints `https://tailscale.com/download` and the manual next steps, exits 0;
  2. reads `tailscale status --json --peers=false`; not `Running`: prints `sudo tailscale up`, exits 0;
  3. prints the **privacy note** with the exact name that becomes public (`Self.DNSName`); if the tailnet has no `CertDomains` (HTTPS certificates off), says to turn them on in the admin console (DNS) and exits 0 before running anything;
  4. asks `Turn on https for <name>? [y/N]` (`--yes-tailscale` answers yes, `--no-tailscale` skips the whole step; no answer is no);
  5. runs `tailscale serve --bg <port>`; on failure prints its output and `sudo tailscale serve --bg <port>`, exits 1;
  6. writes (or replaces) `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL=https://<name>` in `.env`, recreates the API with it (`docker compose up -d --wait api`), checks `https://<name>/healthz` for up to about 90 s, then prints "Open https://<name> in your browser and create the owner account…, then follow the setup steps: Get the app, then Pair your phone."
  It never installs Tailscale, never signs in for the driver and never runs `tailscale funnel`. `install.md` section 2 describes the step and the flags.
- Standards checked: Tailscale [`tailscale serve`](https://tailscale.com/kb/1312/serve) (`--bg` keeps serving after the command returns), [Enabling HTTPS](https://tailscale.com/kb/1153/enabling-https) (MagicDNS and HTTPS Certificates in the admin console; the machine name goes into public Certificate Transparency logs), [CLI `status --json`](https://tailscale.com/kb/1080/cli#status) (checked read-only on this host, 1.104.5: `--peers=false` leaves only `Self`, whose `DNSName` ends in a dot; `CertDomains` is listed once certificates are on); prompts default to No when the answer is empty or there is no terminal (as `apt`'s `[y/N]`).
- Tests: `infra/install.test.mjs` 4 new, run with fake `docker`, `curl` and `tailscale` on a PATH that holds only the tools the script needs (the host's real Tailscale is never called): Tailscale missing; yes after the privacy note serves the port, writes the address, recreates the API and checks the https address, never `funnel`; no, `--no-tailscale`, not signed in and certificates off serve and write nothing; `--yes-tailscale` without a terminal, and a failing `serve` exits 1 with the command to run and writes nothing. All fail without the change.
- Not done: running it on a real fresh host with a real tailnet (plan 19 T7, the owner's Spike D). On this machine Tailscale was not touched.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): plan 19 is still marked as awaiting sign-off; T1–T3 and T5 follow its recommended answers (Q1 ask then run, Q2 keep Change address, Q5 gateway only), as the owner asked for the Tailscale prompt on 2026-11-07.

## PR W6 — Web: the Add a goal form no longer overlaps

- Branch `pr-W6-goal-form-fit` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4c-setup-no-platform-step`.
- Owner report (2026-11-08, screenshot): on Goals, "Active distance (estimated)" in Measure ran over the Per dropdown. A grid item is at least as wide as its content by default (CSS Grid `min-width: auto`, [MDN: min-width auto on grid items](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/min-width)), and the select trigger never wraps, so the text spilled into the next column. `SelectField` and `Picker` now carry `min-w-0`, so a long choice is cut off with the trigger's existing one-line clamp; every select in a grid benefits. The goal form is two columns on small screens and, from `lg`, gives Measure a wider column with the button sized to its text.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/goals.test.tsx` 1 new (Measure and Per triggers and their fields can shrink); fails without the change.

## PR W8 — Server + Web: setup steps stay done; goal and backup can be skipped

- Branch `pr-W8-setup-steps-stick` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W6-goal-form-fit`.

The dashboard counts a step done when it is done now or listed, and lists any step it sees done, so it stays done afterwards. Goal and backup (optional, not needed to record shifts) get a **Skip** button; vehicle and phone do not.
- Standards checked: onboarding-checklist practice keeps completed tasks completed and lets optional ones be dismissed ([Appcues checklists](https://docs.appcues.com/en_US/checklists), [Userpilot checklist best practices](https://userpilot.com/blog/onboarding-checklist/), [NN/g on progress indicators and checklists](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progress-indicators/)).

## PR W7a — Web: expense form, five one-tap categories and a More list

- Branch `pr-W7a-expense-categories` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W6-goal-form-fit`.
- Owner request (2026-11-08, screenshot): "make the expenses adding more decluttered and less cognitive overload". The New expense card opened with a wall of 22 category chips, then date, amount and merchant, the deductible row, vehicle, shift, repeats and notes, all at once.
- Done (first half; W7b hides the rest behind More details):
  - **Amount first**, with the date beside it (today by default), and the cursor in Amount when the form opens, so a quick entry is: type the amount, tap a category, Save (Enter saves).
  - **Category: five one-tap choices**, the driver's most used (counted over the expenses listed), topped up with fuel, parking, tolls, phone and car wash, in the usual list order so they do not jump around. Everything else is in one **More…** dropdown (shadcn Select), grouped **Vehicle** and **Work and other**; a category chosen there shows in the dropdown, highlighted like a chip. Choosing a category still sets its default deductible flag and business share.
  - Form card limited to `max-w-2xl` on wide screens. Plan 18 section 5.3's web line updated.
- Standards checked: Nielsen Norman Group, [Progressive Disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) (show the few options most people need first, defer the rest to one clearly labelled place, at most two levels; choose them by frequency of use) and [Chunking](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/chunking/) (the More list in two named groups); Hick–Hyman law ([Laws of UX, Hick's law](https://lawsofux.com/hicks-law/): choice time grows with the number of options); "most used first" in expense tools: Visma Expense [remembers the last ten expense types](https://docs.vismasoftware.no/expense/release-notes/2026-02-01-expense-registration-remembers-the-last-ten-used-expense-types/), Findity lists [most-used categories first](https://www.findity.com/pre-release-notes/pre-release-notes-2023-09-25); gig-driver apps open one Add expense form from a single + button ([Stride](https://support.stridehealth.com/hc/en-us/articles/360023450253-How-do-I-start-tracking-my-expenses), [Everlance](https://help.everlance.com/hc/en-us/articles/45326143653915-Can-Everlance-Scan-Receipts), [Gridwise](https://help.gridwise.io/hc/en-us/articles/360061830913), [QuickBooks Self-Employed](https://quickbooks.intuit.com/learn-support/en-us/help-article/bank-transactions/manually-add-transactions-quickbooks-self-employed/L1obtgTBA_US_en_US)); only MileWage's own fields are kept, nothing added from them.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/expenses.test.tsx` 1 new (the cursor is in Amount; three meals and one accounting expense give Fuel, Parking, Tolls, Meals, Accounting and tax help as chips; Insurance is chosen from More and no chip is pressed; with no expenses the five common ones), and the add test now picks Meals from More. Both fail without the change.
- Visual check (headless Chromium, Vite dev server, mocked API, 390 and 1280 px): no sideways scroll; the More list fits a phone screen.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): five one-tap categories, and "most used" counted over the expenses currently listed (the list's filters change it), not stored.

## PR W7b — Web: expense form, the rest behind More details

- Branch `pr-W7b-expense-more-details` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W7a-expense-categories`.
- Done (second half of the owner's 2026-11-08 request, see W7a):
  - A new expense now shows only **Amount, Date, Category** (W7a's five chips and More list), one line saying what will be deducted ("Deductible in full.", "Deductible: 50 % business use." or "Not deductible.", followed by the standard-mileage note when it applies), then **Save expense**.
  - **More details** (a button with `aria-expanded`, the same disclosure as the dashboard's "n done"; while closed, a line names what is inside: "Merchant, deductible share, vehicle, shift, repeats, notes") opens Merchant (with its suggestions), Deductible with the Business use picks (picks only while deductible), Vehicle, Shift, Repeats with Next due, and Notes. Closed for a new expense, open when editing one.
  - **Shown only when relevant:** Vehicle when the driver has a vehicle; Shift when a shift is within 7 days of the date (or one is already linked).
  - **Default vehicle:** a vehicle category (fuel, parking, tolls, …) is put on the driver's default vehicle (Settings, the one preselected for a shift); other categories get none. A vehicle the driver chose is kept when the category changes. This also makes the standard-mileage check (`double_counts`) work without opening More details.
  - Every field the form had is still there; nothing is dropped from the request.
- Standards checked: Nielsen Norman Group, [Progressive Disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) (secondary options behind one clearly labelled control that says what it holds; one level only); GOV.UK Service Manual, [Structuring forms](https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/design/form-structure) and the Design System's [Question pages](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/question-pages/) (start from one thing per page; only closely related questions together; ask only what applies to the user); Baymard Institute, [Checkout form fields](https://baymard.com/blog/checkout-form-fields) (the number of fields shown weighs more on usability than the number of steps); WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices, [Disclosure pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/disclosure/) (a button with `aria-expanded`). A side sheet for the form was considered and not used: no other page uses one, and the inline card already appears only after Add expense.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/expenses.test.tsx` 3 new (a new expense shows Amount, focused, Date, the five chips and "Deductible in full."; Merchant, Vehicle, Shift, Repeats, Notes, Deductible and the 75% pick appear only after More details, which reports `aria-expanded`; no Vehicle or Shift field with neither; with default vehicle 5, fuel shows the standard-mileage note, Phone drops it, Parking is saved with `vehicle_id` 5 and the nearby shift 7; Edit opens with More details expanded and the notes filled, and saves with PATCH), and the add and date tests open More details. All fail without the change.
- Visual check (headless Chromium, Vite dev server, mocked API, 390 and 1280 px, light and dark): closed and open, no sideways scroll.
- Assumptions (owner to confirm): merchant is a detail, not part of the quick entry; the default vehicle is applied to vehicle categories; `tax_credit_cents` (HST/GST paid, plan 18 C1) is still not on the web form, as before this change; adding it under More details for a driver registered for sales tax is a separate decision.
## PR P2a — Phone: the odometer is asked once in a while, after a shift (Comma's cadence)

- Branch `pr-P2a-odometer-cadence` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W4c-setup-no-platform-step`. Owner request 2026-11-08: "the odometer doesnt go away / that odometer thing need to be like once in a while like how the comma app does rather than daily".
- Before: the Start card had an "Odometer at start" field on every shift, and the running-shift screen always showed an Odometer card (reading, Save start odometer, hint) for the whole shift.
- Comma's rule (commaApp `docs/features/mileage-tracking.md`, "Odometer reconciliation", and the end-of-shift handler in `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`): after a shift ends, a dialog asks for the dashboard reading when it is the 1st of the month or the oldest GPS-only shift since the last reading is 14 or more days old; "Remind me later" closes it. Comma's drift spreading across shifts is not copied (not in MileWage's plans; plan 07 section 2.2 keeps the GPS-against-odometer check a query).
- Done:
  - `shift/odometerDue.ts`: due when this vehicle was never checked (the baseline), when the last check is 14 days old, or when the last check was in an earlier calendar month in the driver's time zone. The last check is the newer of the vehicle's newest reading on any shift (the phone's or one the pull feed brought from the web) and the last time the driver answered or dismissed the prompt on this phone (`odometer.checked.<vehicle>` in the plain store). `ShiftStore.lastOdometerAt(vehicleId)` added.
  - Home: no odometer field on Start, no Odometer card while the shift runs. After **End shift**, when due, the **Odometer check** dialog (`screens/OdometerPrompt.tsx`, the MileWage dialog style): reading in the driver's unit, **Save reading** (saved as the `shift_end` reading of the shift just ended, through the same edit path as the shift page, so offline it waits in the outbox) or **Not now**. Either one marks the check, so it does not come back until the next one is due. A reading that is not a number keeps the dialog open with the existing error toast. Readings can still be typed any time on the shift page (End odometer) and on the web.
  - Server: unchanged. The server never decided when to ask; the phone decides offline from what it holds.
- Standards checked: [CRA, motor vehicle expenses](https://www.canada.ca/en/revenue-agency/services/tax/businesses/small-businesses-self-employed-income/business-income-tax-reporting/business-expenses/motor-vehicle-expenses.html) ("Record the odometer reading of each vehicle at the start and end of the fiscal period", and when the vehicle is bought, sold or traded; base year and 3-month sample): asking after the first shift of each month gives a reading in December and in January even when the driver does not work on the 1st; the exact year readings stay on the web Odometer page (`year_start`/`year_end`, plan 07 section 2.2). IRS [Pub. 463](https://www.irs.gov/publications/p463) does not require odometer readings but [Form 4562](https://www.irs.gov/forms-pubs/about-form-4562) Part V lines 30–33 and Schedule C line 44 ask the year's total, business, commuting and other miles, which readings back up. HMRC [BIM75005](https://www.gov.uk/hmrc-internal-manuals/business-income-manual/bim75005) asks for a contemporaneous mileage record and no odometer readings. Asking after the shift, not while driving: plan 05 section 2.1 (zero distraction while driving).
- Tests: `apps/phone/test/odometer.test.tsx` 6 new (first time due, not again within 14 days in the same month, due at 14 days; a new month in the driver's time zone, December to January; the last check is the newer of a reading and a dismissal; on the phone: no odometer on Start or while running, the dialog after the shift, Not now closes it and the next one is not due; a mistyped reading keeps it open and a typed one becomes the shift's end reading; no dialog after a recent check). `deadends.test.tsx` drops the old Start-field case (the field is gone). They fail without the change.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): Comma's "1st of the month" is read as "the first shift ended in a new month", and Comma's "Remind me later" (which asks again after the next shift) became "Not now", which waits until the next check is due, as asked ("stay gone until the next one is due").
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the first shift with a vehicle asks once, as the baseline reading (Comma takes it when the vehicle is added; MileWage vehicles are added on the web without one).

## PR P2b — Phone: the running-shift screen redesigned

- Branch `pr-P2b-running-shift` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-P2a-odometer-cadence`. Owner request 2026-11-08: "the shift running page on the phone app i dont like it", "make shift running page something better not the circle not what we have / make a good design over there on the phone app".
- Before (P1d): a bold clock with "Since", a Distance card with a server badge, the Odometer card (gone in P2a), notices, and a swipe-to-end slider; a pulsing dot in a Recording badge in the header.
- Done (`screens/RunningShift.tsx`, out of `HomeScreen.tsx`): an instrument-panel layout for a phone in a dash mount, no cards and no round shapes:
  - Header: **Shift running**, "Civic · since 19:00" (vehicle and start time), Shifts and Settings; under it a thin full-width bar with a yellow segment that keeps sliding (Material 3's indeterminate linear progress) as the "recording" sign, in place of the pulsing dot.
  - Three readings, largest first, each a small capital label over a large tabular figure with a hairline under it: **TIME** `1:04:09` (64), **DISTANCE** `38.4 mi` (48, unit muted, "Approximate; the server works out the final distance."), **DEDUCTION SO FAR** `$25.73` (36, earned green; "(your rate)" when the driver's own rate is used; hidden when no rate applies, never a guess). Then one line with a cloud icon: "On the server" or "Kept on this phone until the server is reachable". Notices and the sync banner follow, only when there is something to say.
  - Footer: one **End shift** button (56 dp, red, stop square), which asks "End this shift?" ("Recording stops at 38.4 mi. You cannot restart this shift.") with **End shift** and **Keep recording**. The swipe slider is no longer used here (`components/ui/swipe-button.tsx` stays, unused).
  - The deduction so far is the phone's estimate, as on Home: `store/summary.ts` gains `runningValue`: what the running distance adds to its month in `summarize`, so it is priced after the shifts already on the phone (Canada's 5,000 km tier counts them first) with the same rules as the Home figures.
- Comma's running console (commaApp `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, "Fullscreen Clock Overlay") was used only to check what a driver looks at: time, tracking status and start time, distance, write-off value, one end control. Not taken: pause, "Got first order", active/dead miles, platform logo (not in MileWage's plans; the phone has no order buttons, decision 1.5b). Nothing from the Comma web app.
- Standards checked: plan 05 section 2.1 (56 dp shift button in the bottom thumb zone, glanceable, high contrast); Material 3 [progress indicators](https://m3.material.io/components/progress-indicators/guidelines) (indeterminate linear for a process with no known end) and Compose [`LinearProgressIndicator`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/package-summary#LinearProgressIndicator(androidx.compose.ui.Modifier,androidx.compose.ui.graphics.Color,androidx.compose.ui.graphics.Color,androidx.compose.ui.graphics.StrokeCap,androidx.compose.ui.unit.Dp)); Material 3 [type scale](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Typography) (the largest styles "reserved for short, important text or numerals"); Material 3 [dialogs](https://m3.material.io/components/dialogs/guidelines) (confirm a step that cannot be undone, the action named on the button, a way to back out); WCAG 2.2 [target size](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html) for the swipe's replacement (a single tap works for everyone, no path-based gesture: [2.5.1 Pointer gestures](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/pointer-gestures.html)).
- Tests: `shift.screen.test.tsx` (Time and Distance readings, "Civic · since 19:00", End shift asks, Keep recording keeps the tracker running, End shift in the dialog ends it); `summary.test.tsx` 2 new (`runningValue` after a shared Canada tier, null without a rate; the running screen shows "Deduction so far (your rate)" $0.45 at the driver's own rate); `odometer.test.tsx` ends through the dialog. They fail without the change.
- Not done: a look on a real phone (no emulators; the owner tests the APK).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): "earnings so far" is the deduction value so far: the phone has no earnings (plan 22: the phone never writes earnings), and Comma's console showed the write-off value in the same place.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): ending is a button plus a confirm dialog instead of the swipe; the swipe was part of "what we have", and WCAG 2.5.1 prefers a single tap.
## PR P3 — Phone: the Permissions screen keeps what is allowed when you come back to it

- Branch `pr-P3-permissions-stable` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-W6-goal-form-fit`.
- Owner report (2026-11-08): "the permission test page is not stable if i go back and come back to that page it shows permission missing even tho its not".
- Root cause: the Permissions screen kept two facts Android cannot report in component state only, and started every visit from `initialLadder()`, where both are false: **the location disclosure was accepted** (never saved anywhere) and **the battery page was visited** (saved to the plain store for Home, but never read back here). Leaving the screen (Go back) unmounts it, so on the next visit "Location use" was Required again with the disclosure card and "I understand", and "Battery settings" lost its Done ("4 of 6 done" after finishing all six). On top of that every OS permission showed Required/Recommended until the first `snapshot()` resolved (a flash of "missing"), and a slow older read (Check again, coming back from settings) could land after a newer one and overwrite it.
- Done:
  - `permissions/ladder.ts`: `DISCLOSURE_ACCEPTED` (`perm.disclosureAccepted`) and `loadLadder(port, plain)`, which reads the OS snapshot together with both saved facts. A location answer already on record (granted, denied or blocked) also counts as the disclosure accepted, since the app shows the location prompt only after it; this covers installs from before the flag was saved.
  - `PermissionsScreen`: state is `null` until the first read lands; meanwhile every step says **Checking** and "Checking…" replaces "n of 6 done" (no Required, no warnings, no action button). Reads run on every focus (`useFocusEffect`) and when the app becomes `active` again (back from system settings); each read or action takes a ticket and a read that resolves after a newer one started (or after the screen lost focus) is dropped. "Check again" uses the same read. "I understand" saves `perm.disclosureAccepted`.
  - `SetupStepScreen`: "I understand" also saves `perm.disclosureAccepted`, so the Permissions screen does not ask again after setup. The setup flow itself is unchanged (it still shows the disclosure first).
- Standards checked: React Navigation [`useFocusEffect`](https://reactnavigation.org/docs/use-focus-effect/) and [navigation lifecycle](https://reactnavigation.org/docs/navigation-lifecycle/) (going back unmounts a stack screen; re-run work on focus and clean up on blur); React Native [`AppState`](https://reactnative.dev/docs/appstate) (`active` on return from Settings); React [fetching in effects](https://react.dev/learn/synchronizing-with-effects#fetching-data) (ignore a result that arrives after a newer request, the race condition it describes); Android [request runtime permissions](https://developer.android.com/training/permissions/requesting) (check `checkSelfPermission` every time; the user can change it in Settings at any moment), React Native [`PermissionsAndroid.check`](https://reactnative.dev/docs/permissionsandroid) (read-only, the call the adapter already uses; it is the counterpart of Expo's `getPermissionsAsync`, which the app does not use); Google Play [prominent disclosure for location](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9799150) (shown before the runtime prompt, so an answer on record means it was shown). The Android adapter's checks themselves (fine, background on API 29+, activity on 29+, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` on 33+) were reviewed and are correct; they were not the cause.
- Tests: `apps/phone/test/permissions.test.tsx` 4 new: finish all six steps, Go back, open Permissions again: still "6 of 6 done", no disclosure, no "I understand"; with every permission granted and the battery visited, a slow read on the second visit shows "Checking…" and no Required/Recommended/disclosure, then "6 of 6 done"; a slow older "Check again" read (location denied) resolving after a newer one (granted) does not overwrite it; `loadLadder` reads both facts back and derives the disclosure from a location answer. `connected.test.tsx`: setup saves the disclosure. All fail without the change.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): when location was already answered (for example granted in system settings before the disclosure was ever saved), the Permissions screen shows "Location use" as done rather than asking the driver to read the disclosure again.
## PR P4 — Phone: the running-shift notification as a metric card (owner request 2026-11-08)

- Branch `pr-P4-notification-design` (pushed, not merged), on `pr-W6-goal-form-fit` (which holds P1h).
- Owner request: "make the design of the notification really smooth and well arranged"; "the notification and metrics on the notification panel … make that visually appealing". The phone posts one notification, the foreground service's running-shift notification (`LocationTrackingService.kt`, P1h); no other code posts one (no expo-notifications, no other `NotificationCompat`), so there was nothing else to align.
- Done (09 §5.10 rewritten):
  - **Android 17+: `NotificationCompat.MetricStyle`**, Google's new template for live figures: **Shift** "12.3" km, **Time** "1:02:15" (a stopwatch from the shift start that the system ticks, so the app never re-posts for it) and, once a shift has already ended today, **Today** "36.9" km, side by side with equal width; units go on the label. Title "Shift in progress"; the start time and vehicle ("Started 09:02 · Civic") as the header's sub text, because this style shows no content text. Collapsed (when not promoted) Android puts the figures on one line without units. As a Live Update the figures are the card's headline and the shift's distance is the critical metric (chip).
  - **Android 8 to 16** (no metric template there): title "12.3 km this shift", the live clock in the header (as before); the text is today's total "Today 36.9 km · 3 shifts" or, for the first shift of the day, "Started 09:02 · Civic" instead of the old "Today 12.3 km · first shift", which only repeated the title. Expanded (BigTextStyle) only when there is a second line to show; "Tap to open MileWage" is gone (tapping a notification is how every notification opens its app).
  - Unchanged: promoted ongoing request with the distance as the status-bar chip (`setShortCriticalText`), the silent default-importance channel `milewage_shift`, brand-yellow accent and the route icon, one notification id, `setOnlyAlertOnce`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_IMMEDIATE`, public on the lock screen, tap opens the app.
  - **Smoother updates:** the notification is re-posted only when something on it changes (the whole content, not just the title, is compared), which is at most once per new fix and only when a figure moves by 0.1 km or mi (about every 4 s at 100 km/h); the clocks are system-driven.
  - `androidx.core:core-ktx` 1.17.0 to 1.19.1 (the first stable with `NotificationCompat.MetricStyle`; needs compileSdk 37 and AGP 9.1, which React Native's catalog already uses: compileSdk 37, AGP 9.2.1). MetricStyle is only built on API 37+.
- **No action buttons.** Considered "End shift": ending runs in the app (the hand-off, and the end odometer prompt now being built in P2), so the button could only open the running-shift screen, which is what tapping does; Android's guide says not to add actions that duplicate the tap. "Open" is rejected for the same reason.
- **Not used:** `ProgressStyle` (Android 16), meant for start-to-end journeys with a known end (rideshare, delivery, navigation); a shift has no target or end to measure against (no goal on the phone). A custom `RemoteViews` layout: Live Updates refuse custom views, and Google's Live Update FAQ says to avoid them because they behave differently across versions and makers.
- Standards checked: Android [Live Updates](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/live-update) (requirements: standard, BigText, Call, Progress or Metric style, ongoing, a content title, not colorized, no custom views, channel above `IMPORTANCE_MIN`; the chip shows `setShortCriticalText` or the time, at most 96 dp, whole text under 7 characters; appropriate uses include active navigation and rideshare); [Metric style](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/metric-style) (Android 17, up to 3 metrics, no content text, units on the label when expanded, dropped when collapsed, sub text in the header line when promoted) with [`NotificationCompat.MetricStyle`](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/core/app/NotificationCompat.MetricStyle), [`Metric`](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/core/app/NotificationCompat.Metric) (labels 10 characters or fewer), [`FixedFloat`](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/core/app/NotificationCompat.Metric.FixedFloat), [`TimeDifference.forStopwatch`](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/core/app/NotificationCompat.Metric.TimeDifference) and the [androidx.core release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/core) (MetricStyle in 1.19; AAR metadata: minCompileSdk 37, AGP 9.1.0); [Progress-centric notifications](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/progress-centric) (`ProgressStyle` for start-to-end journeys); Android design guide [Notifications](https://developer.android.com/design/ui/mobile/guides/home-screen/notifications) (title holds the most important information, under 30 characters, text under 40 and not repeating the title; no actions that duplicate the tap; foreground-service notifications; channel importance); [Create a notification](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/build-notification) (update with the same id, `setOnlyAlertOnce`, the system drops updates posted many times a second); how the first Live Updates look: Google Maps' chip shows one figure, the time to the next turn ([9to5Google, Android 16 Live Updates](https://9to5google.com/2025/07/03/google-details-android-16-live-updates/), [Android Authority, MetricStyle in Android 17](https://www.androidauthority.com/android-17-live-updates-metric-style-template-3669117/)); Uber's was shown only as a demo at Google I/O 2025.
- Tests: `ShiftNoticeTest.kt` (JUnit): first shift: title, text with start time and vehicle, no expanded text, chip, the Android 17 title, sub text and two metrics (Shift 12.3 km, a clock from the start); in miles with earlier shifts: "Today 11.0 mi · 3 shifts", both lines expanded, Today metric; labels within 10 characters, no change (no re-post) between 1,020 m and 1,040 m, a change at 1,060 m, and safe text without the app's details. They fail without the change.
- If Android 17 ever refuses the metric layout, the notification falls back to the Android 8–16 layout (logged), so a style can never stop the foreground service.
- `npm run verify` green (vitest 1057 passed, 3 skipped; phone jest 275 passed). Release build (`scripts/phone-release-build.sh`, no `--publish`) succeeds (15.5 minutes on a busy machine, APK 108,300,253 bytes) with JUnit 30 passed (comma-tracker 24 with ShiftNoticeTest 4, LiveDistanceTest 2; qr-scanner 3; net-check 3); after the fallback was added, `:comma-tracker:compileReleaseKotlin`, `:comma-tracker:testDebugUnitTest` and `assembleRelease` were run again in the same build tree and pass. Not seen on a device: the owner checks it on the phone (on Android 17 the metric card; on Android 16 the text layout with the chip; both expanded and collapsed).
- Size: about 130 added / 40 removed lines of Kotlin and tests, plus the plan and this entry.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the chip and the first figure stay the shift's **distance** (as P1h), not the elapsed time; no **End shift** button until ending can run from the notification with the end odometer prompt (P2), and then it should be a real one-tap end, not a shortcut to the app; earnings are not shown because the phone has no earnings during a shift (they are entered on the web afterwards).

## PR PW2 — Web: service worker, "new version" prompt and offline notice (plan 27)

- Branch `pr-PW2-service-worker` (pushed, not merged), stacked on `pr-PW1-manifest`.
- Done:
  - **Service worker** with [vite-plugin-pwa](https://vite-pwa-org.netlify.app/) `2.0.0` (2026-10-03) and `workbox-window` `7.4.1` (2026-05-04), exact versions, dev dependencies of `apps/web` (Workbox 7 `generateSW`, runtime inlined: one file, `/sw.js`; `mode: 'production'` whatever `NODE_ENV` says). It **precaches the app shell only** (index.html, hashed JS/CSS, fonts, icons, manifest: 26 files, 2.2 MB; not the MapLibre worker), has **no runtime caching** (API answers never enter a cache), and falls back to the precached index.html for navigations except `/api/*` (APK and export downloads) and `/healthz`. `cleanupOutdatedCaches` on. No service worker under `npm run dev`. Options live in `vite.config.ts` (`pwaOptions`).
  - **Update flow** (`src/pwa/update.ts`, `src/pwa/UpdatePrompt.tsx`, started in `main.tsx`): a new version installs and **waits**; a shadcn `Card` bottom right says "A new version of MileWage is ready." with **Later** and **Reload**. Reload sends skip-waiting and the page reloads once the new worker controls it, so one page never mixes two versions. An open page checks for a new version every hour (skipped offline). Shown over every screen, including `/app` and `/pair`.
  - **Offline / server down**: the auth shell's error state now says "You are offline" (when the browser knows) or "MileWage is not answering", with **Try again**, and tries again by itself on the `online` event, instead of a bare line of text.
  - Plain http on the LAN: there is no `navigator.serviceWorker`, so nothing registers and nothing breaks.
  - Caddy: `/sw.js` and **`/index.html`** with `no-cache`. Found by the new build test: the worker precaches `/index.html` by name and Caddy only served it under the screens' paths, so the worker's install would have failed with a 404. `smoke.sh` checks both.
- Standards checked: plan 27 section 2 — [prompt for update](https://vite-pwa-org.netlify.app/guide/prompt-for-update), Workbox [precaching](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/workbox/modules/workbox-precaching) and [navigation route](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/workbox/modules/workbox-routing#how-to-register-a-navigation-route), [service worker lifecycle](https://web.dev/articles/service-worker-lifecycle), [fresher service workers](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/fresher-sw), MDN [secure contexts](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Security/Secure_Contexts).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/build.test.ts` 6 new (Caddy routes every built file outside `assets/`, one `sw.js` with Workbox inside, the shell and every script precached and not the MapLibre worker, no runtime caching and `/api` and `/healthz` navigations go to the server, skip-waiting only on the page's message, `no-cache` for `sw.js` and `index.html`); `apps/web/test/pwa-update.test.tsx` 7 new (nothing registers without service workers, hourly check only online, the prompt appears on a waiting version, Reload calls the update with reload, Later hides it, offline notice and Try again, retry on `online`). All fail without the change. End to end in headless Chromium 1228 against Caddy 2.8.4 serving the build: worker active, Chrome's `getInstallabilityErrors` empty, 26 precached files and no `/api` entry, offline reload of `/shifts` shows "You are offline", a changed `sw.js` shows the prompt and Reload activates it (`…/scratchpad/e2e/{offline,update}.png`).
- `package-lock.json`: 196 packages added (Workbox's build tools: Babel preset-env, Rollup, Terser; build time only), 4 Babel patch bumps (7.29.12 → 7.29.13); the text diff looks large because of git's line alignment. The phone track also edits the lockfile: on a conflict, take theirs and run `npm install -w apps/web --save-dev --save-exact vite-plugin-pwa@2.0.0 workbox-window@7.4.1`.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): no "ready to work offline" message; Later hides the prompt until the next page load; no data is kept for offline use.
