## PR N1 — Phone: free-storage and OS-notification adapters wired

- Branch `pr-N1-phone-adapters`, on `main` (caeed56). Closes M5.8 "NOT done" item 3 and the JavaScript half of item 2.
- Done, free storage: `expo-file-system` ~58.0.13 is now a direct dependency (it was already in the app through `expo`; no new native code, its `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` were already stripped by the manifest policy). SDK 58's current API is the `Paths` class: `Paths.availableDiskSpace` (bytes, a synchronous getter); the old `getFreeDiskStorageAsync` export throws in SDK 58 and lives only under `expo-file-system/legacy`. `storage/freeSpace.ts` `createFreeBytes()` loads the module on first use and returns `null` on any error or on a value that is not a byte count, so the banner says "unknown", never "normal". Production wiring moved into `productionParts()` in `services.tsx` (`defaultServices()` builds from it), which now passes `freeBytes`, so the low (under 200 MB) and critical (under 50 MB) storage sentences of 15 section 3.4 can show on a device.
- Done, notifications (JavaScript side): `notifications/port.ts` defines `NotificationsPort` (`show(id, title, body)`, `schedule(id, atMs, title, body)`, `cancel(id)`, `granted()`), the `NotificationBackend` a library must provide (`enabled`, `present`, `schedule`, `cancel`) and `createNotifications`, the production adapter: every call first reads the ladder's own `notifications` status through `PermissionsPort.snapshot()` and does nothing unless it is `granted` and the OS switch is on; it never asks (the only prompt stays the ladder's `notifications` step); `cancel` works without the permission; backend errors are swallowed. The reminders channel is `milewage_reminders`, "Reminders", default importance (named like the tracker's `milewage_shift`). `notifications/unsynced.ts` `UnsyncedReminders.update(status)` keeps exactly one reminder with the id `unsynced` pending for the next due time with the banner's own rule and words (`reminderFor`: 3 days, then at most once a day, stronger from 7 days, the stronger words already in the scheduled text); a reminder counts as given when the banner showed it, when the pending one's time has passed, or when it shows one now; the same state twice schedules nothing new; nothing pending cancels the pending and the shown one. `SyncBanner` calls it on every refresh (mount and every sync event). `Services.notifications` and `Services.reminders` are new; `Parts.notifications` is injected. The banner key moved to `status.ts` (`REMINDER_SHOWN_KEY`, same value).
- **Not done, stopped as instructed: no notifications library is installed.** `expo-notifications` ~58.1.8 was installed with `npx expo install` and inspected, then removed again. Its Android library manifest declares `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED` (on the forbidden list; strippable, it only re-schedules after a reboot) and `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (allowed), plus a receiver for `BOOT_COMPLETED`, `REBOOT`, `QUICKBOOT_POWERON` and `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED`, and an `ExpoFirebaseMessagingService`. Its `build.gradle` always brings `com.google.firebase:firebase-messaging:25.0.1`, whose own manifest adds `WAKE_LOCK`, `com.google.android.c2dm.permission.RECEIVE`, `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`, the Firebase instance-id receiver and component discovery, and which depends on `firebase-installations`, `firebase-datatransport` and Google `datatransport` (`transport-runtime`, `transport-backend-cct`: Google usage logging). That is what owner decision Q1 removed with `expo-camera` (plan 24 section 6), it conflicts with 16 D2 and 18 section 11 ("no push service, no third-party endpoint"), and after a release build `test/manifest.test.ts` would fail on `datatransport` and on the unknown `WAKE_LOCK` and `c2dm` permissions. The policy was not changed. Production therefore runs `createNotifications({ backend: null })`: `granted()` is false, nothing fires, the banner remains the only channel, exactly as on Android 13+ without the permission. Adding a backend later is one object passed in `productionParts()`.
- Android 13 and later: without `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (refused, blocked or never asked on the ladder) nothing is shown or scheduled and nothing is stored; the banner still shows the reminder. On Android 12 and older the ladder reports it granted, and the backend's `enabled()` covers notifications turned off in system settings.
- The odometer prompt (P2a) has no "later" reminder in this tree (`OdometerPrompt.tsx`, `shift/odometerDue.ts`), so there was nothing to wire.
- Tests: `test/adapters.test.ts` 5 (the free bytes of the `expo-file-system` mock pass through; a throwing loader, a throwing getter, NaN, negative, infinite or missing values give `null`; `productionParts()` wires `freeBytes` and 100 MB free turns the status yellow with the low-storage sentence; production `notifications` is never granted and does nothing without throwing; `createServices` exposes `notifications` and `reminders`). `test/notifications.test.tsx` 15 (the adapter shows, schedules and cancels on the Reminders channel when granted; denied, blocked and undetermined do nothing and ask nothing while cancel still works; the OS switch off; no backend, a failing backend and a failing permission read never throw; the reminder is scheduled for the 3-day mark, deduped, shown once when due then scheduled a day later, not repeated after the banner showed it or after the pending one fired, carries the stronger words at 7 days, is cancelled once when nothing is pending, and does nothing without permission; the home banner schedules it while a shift waits and cancels it after Sync now). `expo-file-system` is mocked in `test/setup.ts`. Typecheck, lint and prettier green; phone 35 suites, 302 passed, 4 skipped. The new tests fail without the change.
- Decisions and limits: only inexact scheduling is in the port (no `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`). Without `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED` a reminder pending at a reboot is lost until the app next opens; when the app stays closed only the one pending reminder arrives, the next is scheduled when the app runs again. The `unsynced_reminders` toggle of 15 section 13 is not on the phone (always on). Nothing ran on a device; the owner tests on the phone.
- Owner decision needed for the backend: (a) accept `expo-notifications` with Firebase and Google datatransport (changes Q1 and 16 D2); (b) a small local Expo module like `native/qr-scanner`, using `NotificationManagerCompat` and an inexact `AlarmManager` alarm with a non-exported receiver (no new permission, no Firebase); or (c) `expo-notifications` with Firebase excluded in Gradle (unsupported by Expo; its push classes would be missing at run time).
## PR R1 — Server: milewage-cli restore

- Branch `pr-R1-cli-restore`, on `main` (caeed56). Closes the gap stated in `infra/docs/upgrade.md` and in M3.3 above: a rollback is now one command instead of "start the old version on an empty volume and use the setup screen".
- Done: `milewage-cli restore --file <path> [--key <key> | --key @<file>] [--yes]` (`apps/server/src/cli-restore.ts`), run with the API stopped in a one-off container: `docker compose stop api`, `docker compose run --rm --no-deps api milewage-cli restore --file /app/backups/pre-migration/<name>.db.gz.age`, `docker compose up -d`. Without `--key` it asks for the key; without `--yes` it says what it will do and asks for `yes`. Steps: refuse while any process holds the database; decrypt, check and forward-migrate the backup in `data/.restore-tmp/` (nothing live touched); **move** `milewage.db`, `-wal`, `-shm` and `receipts/` into `data/before-restore-<time>/`; create a fresh database, copy the restored rows in, write the receipt files back; on a failed check, delete the new files and move the old ones back. Prints schema version, integrity, shift count and date range, GPS points, stop labels, the new restore generation and where the old data went. Exit 1 for wrong key, damaged or truncated file, newer backup, missing file, unreadable key file, server running; exit 2 for usage.
- Refactor, no behaviour change for `POST /setup/restore`: the decrypt-check-migrate step and the row swap left the `createRestoreService` closure as `prepareRestore(file, key, work)` and `swapRestored(db, restored, { floorGeneration, verify })`, plus `recoveryRecipient(key)` and the `NEWER_BACKUP` message; the HTTP service and the CLI both call them. `swapRestored` sets `restore_generation = max(live, restored, floorGeneration) + 1`; the CLI passes the generation of the database it moved aside, so the result is the same as the HTTP path over that database (15 section 9.3).
- `runCli` is now async (it resolves to the exit code); `cli-main.ts` awaits it and supplies the terminal prompt (readline; a closed stdin answers empty instead of hanging).
- Docs: `infra/docs/upgrade.md` "Roll back" is the single command (with how to list `pre-migration/` and delete the moved-aside folder); the "Gap, stated plainly" paragraph is gone. `install.md` section 4 names the command. Plan 10 section 7 gains a status line with the differences from its steps; plan 03 section 5's example uses `run --rm --no-deps` with the API stopped.
- Tests: new `apps/server/test/restore-cli.test.ts` (6): a real encrypted backup (shift, 3 points, a receipt, generation 2) restored over a data folder at generation 7 gives generation 8, the counts, the receipt file byte-for-byte, the old database and receipt intact in `before-restore-<time>/`, `diagnostics.db` untouched, the key written to an empty `secrets.json`; into an empty folder with the key from a file (generation 3, no aside folder); refusal while the database is held by a connection in this process and by a child process ("stop the api service first", nothing moved); wrong key, damaged, truncated, newer-schema, missing file, malformed key, unreadable key file all exit 1 with nothing changed; a forced verification failure puts the old data back; the prompt (cancel, key typed at the prompt) and usage errors (exit 2). `apps/server/test/cli.test.ts` awaits the now-async `runCli` and checks the help lists `restore`. `scripts/build-server.test.mjs` builds the bundle (now under `node_modules/.cache` so it can load better-sqlite3 as `/app` does in the image) and runs the bundled `cli.mjs restore` on a real backup. All new tests fail without the change.
- Checks: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build:server` green; `npx vitest run apps/server scripts`: 66 files, 510 passed, 1 skipped.
- Decisions:
  - **Lock check:** there was no "server running" lock to reuse (`migrate.ts` only locks during a migration, `open.ts` none). The CLI opens the database with `locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE` and `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`: a WAL connection holds a shared lock on the file for its whole life, so this fails while the API runs, in this process, another process or another container on the same volume, and the OS drops the lock when a process dies, so a crashed server leaves nothing stale (a pid file would). It also refuses while `milewage.db.migrate.lock` exists. Checked twice: before the slow decrypt and again right before moving files.
  - **Where the old data goes:** `<data>/before-restore-<time>/` inside the `data` volume, not `<data>.before-restore-<time>` beside it as the brief proposed. In the container `/app/data` is the volume's mount point: a sibling folder would be a cross-device rename (fails) and would sit in the throwaway `run --rm` container. The time is ISO 8601 basic format, as backup file names use (`20261108T120000000Z`).
  - `diagnostics.db` is not moved: it is not backed up and not touched by restore (10 section 2).
  - No `pre-restore/` backup is written: the moved-aside data is the safety copy, and it is the original files rather than a re-encoded copy.
  - `docker compose exec` (plan 10's wording) cannot work: the API must be stopped, so the command runs with `run --rm --no-deps` (same image, volumes and `node` user as the api service).
- Limits: the restore must be run with the **previous** version's image (the CLI forward-migrates what it restores to its own schema), so a rollback to an image older than this PR still needs the old empty-volume route; `upgrade.md` says so. The key typed at the prompt is echoed on screen. Between the second lock check and opening the new database there is no lock held; starting the API in that moment is not prevented.
- Deviation: about 900 changed lines with tests and docs (the move of `prepare` and `swap` out of the closure is about 240 of them with unchanged logic; the new test file about 270).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the moved-aside folder is kept until the owner deletes it (no automatic cleanup), and the CLI never prunes it; the disk monitor counts it like any other data on the volume.
## PR C1 — Release engineering: signed phone builds, release CI, pinned images, Renovate

- Branch `pr-C1-release-engineering`, one commit per part.

### Part 1: phone signing and versioning

- Done:
  - `scripts/phone-keystore.sh` makes the upload keystore with `keytool` (PKCS12, RSA 2048, 10000 days, alias `milewage-upload`, a random 32-character password printed once) at `--out` (default `~/.milewage/upload.keystore`) and writes `~/.milewage/keystore.env` (or `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_ENV`) with `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_PATH`, `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`, `MILEWAGE_KEY_ALIAS`, `MILEWAGE_KEY_PASSWORD`, mode 600. It refuses to overwrite either file and refuses any path inside the repository, and ends with a loud "BACK THIS UP NOW" note (password manager; losing it means a new app identity on Google Play). `.gitignore` ignores `*.keystore` and `*.jks`. Not run for real: no keystore was created on this machine (the tests make throwaway ones in a temp HOME).
  - `scripts/phone-signing-env.sh`, sourced by `scripts/phone-release-build.sh` before Gradle: loads the env file when it exists (or uses the variables already in the environment, for CI), checks all four values and the keystore file, and says which key signs. Without them it says "Signing with the Expo DEBUG key ... Not for Google Play" and the build behaves as before.
  - Config plugin `apps/phone/plugins/withReleaseSigning.js` (`withAppBuildGradle`) with the text rewrite in `plugins/releaseSigning.js`: adds a `release` signing config inside `signingConfigs`, defined only when `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_PATH` is set and reading the four variables with `System.getenv`, and changes the release build type to `signingConfig System.getenv('MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_PATH') ? signingConfigs.release : signingConfigs.debug`. Idempotent; throws if the template lost its `signingConfigs` block or the release type's debug signing line, so a changed Expo template cannot silently ship the debug key. Refuses a Kotlin DSL build file.
  - `apps/phone/app.json` became `apps/phone/app.config.js` (CommonJS, already ignored by `apps/phone/*.config.js` in eslint). Every value is unchanged (checked: `npx expo config --type public --json` before and after differ only in the new `android.versionCode` and the new plugin). `version` is read from the root `VERSION`; `android.versionCode` is `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE` (default 1; anything but a whole number from 1 to 2100000000, Google Play's limit, throws). `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE=42 npx expo config --type public` shows `versionCode: 42`; `=4x` fails with the message.
  - `scripts/version.mjs check` also loads `apps/phone/app.config.js` in a separate `node` process with the current environment and reports it when its version is not `VERSION` or when it fails to load (so a bad `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE` fails `npm run version:check`).
  - Readers of `app.json` moved: the Settings footer shows the phone `package.json` version (kept equal to `VERSION` by `version:check`); `scripts/publish-apk.sh` takes the version from `VERSION`; comments in the module manifests, `android.ts`, `PairingLinks.tsx` and `branding/README.md`.
- Tests: jest `test/releaseSigning.test.ts` (7) runs the rewrite against the `android/app/build.gradle` inside the installed `expo/template.tgz` (the file prebuild starts from): the release config reads only the environment and sits behind the `if`; the release type falls back to debug; undoing the two insertions gives the template back byte for byte; idempotent; loud failure on a changed template; the plugin and Kotlin refusal; `app.config.js` lists the plugin. `test/manifest.test.ts` now reads the permissions from `app.config.js`. vitest `scripts/phone-keystore.test.mjs` (8): a real keystore in a temp HOME when `keytool` exists (alias, 2048-bit RSA, 10000 days, both files mode 600, password printed, backup note, a second run refuses and leaves the key), refusal of an existing `--out` and of a path in the repository, the ignore rules, and the signing env helper (debug fallback, loading, `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_ENV`, missing values). `scripts/version.test.mjs` 4 new: the real config resolves to the fixture's `VERSION`, a different version is reported, bad version codes (`12a`, `1.5`, `-3`, `0`, `2100000001`) are reported, the repository itself is in sync. `scripts/publish-apk.test.mjs` reads `VERSION`.
- Decisions/limits:
  - PKCS12 keystores have one password for the store and the key, so both variables hold the same value (keytool ignores a separate key password for PKCS12).
  - The upload key is for Google Play App Signing (Play re-signs with its own key); losing the upload key can be recovered through Play support, losing an app signing key cannot.
  - The signing config is read at Gradle time from the environment, never written into `android/` (which `prebuild --clean` regenerates anyway).
  - A real `expo prebuild` with the plugin was run once (`MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE=7`): `build.gradle` had `versionCode 7`, `versionName "0.1.0"` and the signing blocks as tested. The signed `assembleRelease` with a throwaway key was started and then **stopped on the coordinator's instruction** (machine memory: another session's APK build was being OOM-killed), so no APK was built or verified in this PR. The owner runs, once the key exists:
    `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE=<n> scripts/phone-release-build.sh` then `$ANDROID_HOME/build-tools/<version>/apksigner verify --print-certs apps/phone/android/app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk` and compares the SHA-256 with `keytool -list -v -keystore ~/.milewage/upload.keystore`.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the owner runs `scripts/phone-keystore.sh` once on the build machine and stores the file and password in a password manager; the dname is `CN=MileWage, O=MileWage` unless `--dname` is given.

### Part 2: CI and published images

- Done:
  - `.gitlab-ci.yml`: stages `test` and `build`. `verify` unchanged except that it also runs on `v*` tags (so a release is verified before images are built). `audit` (`npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`, `allow_failure: true`, with the comment that it becomes blocking once the baseline is clean; baseline on 2026-11-08: 11 issues, 2 low and 9 moderate, none high, so it passes today). `images` on `v*` tags, after `verify`: `docker:27` with the `docker:27-dind` service (TLS), QEMU through `tonistiigi/binfmt`, a buildx `docker-container` builder, then one `buildx build --platform linux/amd64,linux/arm64 --push` for the server (the Dockerfile's last stage, no `--target`) and one for `--target caddy`, tagged `$CI_REGISTRY_IMAGE/server:$CI_COMMIT_TAG`, `.../server:latest`, `.../caddy:$CI_COMMIT_TAG`, `.../caddy:latest`. `phone-apk`: manual on `v*` tags, runner tag `android`, runs `scripts/phone-release-build.sh` with `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE=$CI_PIPELINE_IID`, keeps the APK as an artifact (`expire_in: never`); `allow_failure: true` so the waiting manual job does not mark the tag pipeline as blocked. Its comment says the `android` runner does not exist yet and how signing is given to it (a protected File variable for `MILEWAGE_KEYSTORE_ENV`).
  - `infra/docker/docker-compose.release.yml`: the same services, settings, network and volumes as `docker-compose.yml`, with `image: ${MILEWAGE_IMAGE_REPO:?set to your registry path}/server:${MILEWAGE_VERSION:-latest}` (and `caddy`) instead of `build:`.
  - `infra/docs/install.md` section 2, "Run from published images": the two compose files, the two settings (`MILEWAGE_IMAGE_REPO`, `MILEWAGE_VERSION`), the pull and up commands, and that it is not usable until the GitLab project has a runner and a tag has been built.
- Tests: `infra/ci.test.mjs` (6) parses the file with `yaml`: stages and exactly four jobs, `verify` script and rules, `audit` command, `allow_failure` and rules, `images` image, service, rule, platforms, login, the two build commands and their four tags, `phone-apk` runner tag, rule (manual), artifact path and version code, and that the tag rule matches `v0.1.0` and `v1.0.0-rc.1` but not `0.1.0` or `main`. `infra/docker/release.test.mjs` (3): the two compose files are equal once `build` and `image` are removed; the release file builds nothing and pulls from `MILEWAGE_IMAGE_REPO`; `docker compose config` (when Docker is there) refuses it without the variable and resolves `registry.example.com/me/milewage/server:v1.2.3`.
- Decisions/limits: no pipeline has run (no runner). The `images` job needs a runner with `privileged = true` for Docker-in-Docker. `:latest` is pushed as asked, but the release compose file's comment and the guide tell the owner to pin `MILEWAGE_VERSION`.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): release tags are `v<VERSION>`; the phone `versionCode` in CI is the GitLab pipeline IID (grows with every pipeline of the project). If APKs are also built by hand on `coder`, the owner must pick a `MILEWAGE_VERSION_CODE` above the last one uploaded.

### Part 3: base images pinned by digest

- Done: `infra/docker/Dockerfile` pins `node:22.22.0-bookworm-slim@sha256:dd9d21971ec4395903fa6143c2b9267d048ae01ca6d3ea96f16cb30df6187d94` (three stages) and `caddy:2.8.4-alpine@sha256:af32e97399febea808609119bb21544d0265c58a02836576e32a2d082c262c17`. Both are the digests of the multi-arch index (the list that holds amd64 and arm64, both present), so the pin works on a Pi too. How they were read: `docker buildx` is not installed on this machine (`docker: unknown command: docker buildx`); the Docker Hub registry API answered an anonymous HEAD request for each tag (`Docker-Content-Digest`, content type OCI image index), and the same digests are the `RepoDigests` of the images already pulled here. A comment above the FROM lines gives the date and the command to check one by hand.
- Tests: `infra/docker/dockerfile.test.mjs` (3): every FROM of a registry image has a tag and a sha256 digest; one digest per tag, and the tags are exactly `node:22.22.0-bookworm-slim` and `caddy:2.8.4-alpine`; the server is still the last, unnamed stage (what the `images` job and a plain `docker build` produce).
- Not attempted, because of machine memory (coordinator instruction: another session's APK build was being OOM-killed): the ARM64 build and `npm run smoke:docker`. On this machine ARM64 also needs buildx, which is not installed. The owner runs, on a machine with buildx and QEMU:
  - `docker run --privileged --rm tonistiigi/binfmt --install arm64` (once, if `docker buildx ls` shows no linux/arm64)
  - `timeout 600 docker buildx build --platform linux/arm64 -f infra/docker/Dockerfile --target build -t milewage-arm64-test .`
  - `npm run smoke:docker` (builds the stack from the pinned images and checks it).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the digests are refreshed by Renovate (part 4) rather than by hand; until a Renovate bot runs, a new Node or Caddy patch needs a manual edit of both the tag and the digest.

### Part 4: Renovate

- Done: `renovate.json` extends `config:recommended`, `docker:pinDigests` and `schedule:weekly` (Renovate's preset: early Monday), with one package rule that groups every minor and patch update into one pull request ("minor and patch updates"); majors stay one pull request each. README notes that it runs only when a Renovate bot (self-hosted or the Mend app) is pointed at the project, plus one paragraph on releases (upload key, CI, release compose file).
- Tests: `infra/renovate.test.mjs` (3): valid JSON with exactly `$schema`, `extends`, `packageRules`; the presets; the minor and patch group, with no grouped majors; the README note.
- Not done: Renovate's own `renovate-config-validator` was not run (it needs the whole `renovate` package, a large download, on a machine short of memory). The presets are standard names from the Renovate docs.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): grouping includes Expo and React Native minors. Expo SDK packages move together through `npx expo install --fix`; if a grouped update breaks the phone build, add a rule that leaves `expo*` and `react-native*` to the SDK upgrade.

### Checks for the whole PR

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run typecheck:phone`, `npm run lint` (eslint and prettier), `npm run version:check` green. Only the vitest files this PR touched were run (coordinator instruction; the full suite is heavy for this machine): 7 files, 33 passed. Phone jest (`npm test -w apps/phone`, 2 workers): 34 suites, 289 passed, 4 skipped (the merged-manifest checks, which need a release build).
## PR F1 — Small fixes from the to-do list

- **Error `type` URIs.** Problems now carry `type: /api/v1/errors/<code>`, a relative URI reference as RFC 9457 allows, so no domain is needed. A public `GET /api/v1/errors/{code}` (module `core`, open before setup is confirmed) returns `{ code, title, status, description }` from the catalogue in `errors.ts`; an unknown code is a 404 problem. OpenAPI: Problem `type` description and example, new operation `getErrorType`; plan 08 section 7.1 updated. Tests: `apps/server/test/error-types.test.ts` (type shape, known code, unknown codes including `constructor`), `index.test.ts` changed.
- **Unused `ws` removed.** `ws` and `@types/ws` are gone from `apps/server/package.json` (nothing imported them since decision 1.10); the lockfile was updated by `npm uninstall`. Test: `apps/server/test/dependencies.test.ts`.
- **Money uses the currency's own places.** The server stores every amount as integer minor units of `settings.currency` (fields are named `*_cents`, the per-distance rate is described as minor units). Web `formatMoney` now divides by 10 to the power of the currency's places (from `Intl`: JPY 0, USD 2, KWD 3), and `parseMoney`, `moneyInput`, `parseRate` and `rateInput` are the inverses used by every form (earnings, ledger, expenses, stops, maintenance, tax, goals, own mileage rate). The phone already did this in `HomeSummary.money` and has no other money code. Tests: `apps/web/test/format.test.ts` (JPY, USD, KWD, negatives, round trip), stops, goals, earnings and expenses tests updated.
- **Vehicles tax-year error.** A failed tax-year load shows "The tax year could not be loaded." instead of the server's bare message. Test in `apps/web/test/vehicles.test.tsx`.
- **Schedule times follow Settings > Time format.** The browser time input ignores the setting, so Schedule now uses a `TimeField` (hour, minute and, for 12h, AM or PM dropdowns) that stores `HH:MM` as before; listed times use `formatClock`. `formatTime` and `formatDateTime` take an optional `timeFormat` (default 24h, as before); other pages keep their fixed 24h display. Tests: `apps/web/test/schedule.test.tsx` (12h, 24h, formatters).
## PR S1 — Caddy: Content-Security-Policy and HSTS; a public privacy page at /privacy

- Branch `pr-S1-csp-privacy`, on `main` (caeed56).
- Done, part 1 (`infra/docker/Caddyfile`): every answer gets `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob: https://tiles.openfreemap.org; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https://tiles.openfreemap.org; worker-src 'self' blob:; manifest-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (08 response headers, amended by decision 1.11). It is set with `?`, so a policy the API sets itself is kept (platform logos: `default-src 'none'; sandbox`). `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000` only on requests with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` (`@https` matcher; `tailscale serve` sends it and Caddy trusts that proxy since plan 19 T1), so plain http on the host or LAN gets none. What the client needs was read from the build: the OpenFreeMap styles (`positron`, `dark`, fetched with curl on 2026-11-08) name only `tiles.openfreemap.org` for tiles, sprites, glyphs and the Natural Earth raster (the other links in the JSON are attribution links, not fetched); `dist/index.html` has no inline script (vite-plugin-pwa has `injectRegister: false`; `registerSW` is bundled, workbox-window is a file); MapLibre starts its worker from `/assets/maplibre-gl-worker.mjs` on the same origin (`blob:` kept for its cross-origin path); its worker's `globalThis.eval` is only for the RTL text plugin, which MileWage does not load, so no `'unsafe-eval'`; fonts are fontsource files; Radix's scroll lock adds a `<style>` element, hence `'unsafe-inline'` for styles only. The stale "Tiles are added by a later PR" comment is gone. `infra/docs/install.md` section 8 says what a `VITE_MAP_STYLE_URL` build on another host must add to `img-src` and `connect-src`.
- Done, part 2: `apps/web/src/pages/PrivacyPage.tsx` at `/privacy`, public like `/pair` and `/app`. The three public pages now live in one table, `src/publicPages.tsx`, used by `main.tsx` (outside the router and the session) and by `spaPaths` (so Caddy's `@spa` list, checked against it, covers each public page). Sections: On the phone, On this server, Who else sees something and when, Diagnostics, Deleting your data, Questions. Each claim was checked in the code: GPS only between Start and End shift (tracker service; Activity Transition API pauses GPS while still), 10 s / 20 m defaults (`CadenceConfig.kt`), raw points evicted 14 days after upload (`LOCAL_RAW_DAYS`, never while pending), shift results kept on the phone with no automatic removal, device token in Keystore-backed `expo-secure-store` and only its SHA-256 on the server, `allowBackup=false`, the eight Android permissions and what each is for; server raw-point retention 90 days by default, at least 7 (`raw_retention_days`, archiver), no route deletes a shift; backups age-encrypted to the recovery key's public half, the live database not encrypted; the server makes no outbound request; the browser keeps no API data (no runtime caching) and three localStorage keys; MCP off by default, its read tools return trips with start and end coordinates (`get_shift`), no tool reads points or routes or deletes anything; capture sessions exist on the server (30 day purge) but the phone does not record them; Valhalla is not built ("not in this version"). The map line names the basemap host of the build (`basemapHost` in `map/style.ts`, from `VITE_MAP_STYLE_URL`), or says there is none. Links: under every sign-in, setup, recovery-key and restore card (`ui/form.tsx` `Card`, `PrivacyLink`), Settings › Tracking and privacy, the account menu at the foot of the sidebar, and the `/app` download page. All are plain `<a href="/privacy">` (a full page load; the page is outside the router). `infra/docs/install.md` section 9: the privacy page, and removing everything with `docker compose -f infra/docker/docker-compose.yml down -v`.
- Verified through the real `caddy:2.8.4-alpine` image: `caddy validate` passes; the built `dist` served by that image with this Caddyfile on a loopback port, proxying to the real server bundle (`dist/server.mjs`) on a fresh data directory seeded through the API (owner, vehicle, paired phone, one shift with 312 fixes). Headless Chromium (Playwright cache, SwiftShader WebGL): `/privacy` without a session, sign-in, `/dashboard`, `/shifts/1` with the route on the OpenFreeMap basemap (17 tile, style, sprite and glyph requests, all 200), service worker active: **no `securitypolicyviolation` event, no CSP console error** (only the expected 401 before sign-in). Negative control: the same run with `https://tiles.openfreemap.org` removed from the policy reports `connect-src https://tiles.openfreemap.org/styles/positron` and loads no tiles, so the check does detect a missing host. `curl`: the CSP on `/`, `/privacy`, `/healthz` and API answers; an uploaded platform logo keeps `default-src 'none'; sandbox`; HSTS present with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, absent without.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/caddy.test.ts` 7 new (the CSP line in the site header block; `default-src`, `script-src` `'self'` only, no `unsafe-eval` anywhere, `object-src 'none'`; `frame-ancestors 'none'`; every outside host in `style.ts` in both `connect-src` and `img-src`; worker, manifest, font and image sources; HSTS only behind the `X-Forwarded-Proto https` matcher, once, not in the unconditional block; every public page in `@spa`) and the spaPaths shape updated; `apps/web/test/privacy.test.tsx` 7 new (renders at `/privacy` without a session and with no fetch; the six sections and key facts, no em-dash; the map line for OpenFreeMap, another host and `none`; the link under the sign-in card, in the account menu, in Settings › Tracking and privacy and on `/app`); `apppage.test.tsx` waits for the store link before listing links. The new tests fail without the change.
- Assumptions (owner to confirm): the page is the Google Play privacy policy URL, at each owner's own address; no contact address for the developer is given. The phone keeps its shift list until the app's data is cleared (that is what the code does; plan 15's 90-day removal of the phone's route line is not built). The HSTS max-age stays one year with no `includeSubDomains` (a `ts.net` name is per machine). Tailscale and Google statements describe their role in general terms and point to their own terms; MileWage cannot check what they keep.
## PR Q1 — Web: TanStack Query for every read the pages make

- Branch `pr-Q1-web-tanstack-query`, from the worktree's HEAD `13a0143` (the W14b tip), not from `caeed56`: see the assumption below.
- Why: plan 17 decision T8 chose TanStack Query for the web and it was never added. The pages read with about 40 `useEffect` and 180 `useState`: no request was cancelled, so on fast navigation a slow answer could replace a newer one; nothing was cached; every page wrote its own loading and error state.
- Done (web only; no server or OpenAPI change):
  - `@tanstack/react-query` **5.107.1** (latest stable, pinned) in `apps/web`.
  - `apps/web/src/api/query.ts` (124 lines): `createQueryClient` (answers fresh for 30 s, read again when the tab comes back, one retry but never for a 4xx `ApiError`), `useApiQuery(path, init, options)` typed from the OpenAPI paths through the existing openapi-fetch client and `unwrap`, with the key `[path, params]` and the request's `AbortSignal`; `useApiMutation(fn, { invalidates })`; `useInvalidate()(...pathPrefixes)` (a prefix matches the path and every path under it, so `/shifts` also refreshes `/shifts/{ref}/trips`); `useSetApiData` for a save that answers with the new record; `apiKey`, `underPath`, `shouldRetry`.
  - `main.tsx`: one `QueryClientProvider` around the router. `session.tsx`: signing out empties the cache.
  - Pages now read through queries: Dashboard (hero, recent shifts, goal ring, setup checklist, notices), Shifts (week, shift detail, trips, stops, route, vehicle name, earnings, odometer), Vehicles (list, service log, tax year), Expenses, Platforms, Goals, Progress, Tax, Analytics, Odometer, Schedule, Notifications (inbox, alert settings, the header's unread count), Exports (vehicle list), Devices, Backups, AI assistants, and the shared `useCountries` and `usePlatformOf`. Saves invalidate what they change (for example a goal refreshes `/goals` and `/progress`, a platform every `/platforms` list, a backup `/backups` and `/system/status`; a save on a shift puts the new shift in the cache, marks the week and the totals stale, and reads the trips again after a recompute, as before). Lists that change with a filter or a period keep the old rows while the new ones load (`keepPreviousData`), as the pages did. Rendered text and roles are unchanged.
  - `apps/web/src/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx`: the shell wraps the page area in an error boundary. A page that fails to draw shows "This page could not be shown" with **Try again** (resets the queries and draws the page again); the sidebar stays; opening another page clears it.
  - The setup wizard keeps its own reads, but leaving it marks every cached answer stale, so the screens read what the wizard saved.
  - In pages, `useEffect` went from 41 to 7 (the rest are not reads: scroll, an observer, a form value) and `useState` from 181 to 126.
- Standards checked: TanStack Query v5 docs, [Query keys](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/query-keys) (an array with the path and its parameters), [Query cancellation](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/query-cancellation) (pass the `signal` to fetch), [Invalidation from mutations](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/invalidations-from-mutations), [Updates from mutation responses](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/updates-from-mutation-responses), [Paginated queries](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/paginated-queries) (`keepPreviousData`), [Testing](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/guides/testing) (a new client per test, `retry: false`, `gcTime` 0) and [`QueryErrorResetBoundary`](https://tanstack.com/query/v5/docs/framework/react/reference/QueryErrorResetBoundary); React [error boundaries](https://react.dev/reference/react/Component#catching-rendering-errors-with-an-error-boundary). openapi-react-query exists but adds a second client wrapper; the gap (keys, invalidation by path) is about 60 lines over the client the app already has.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/query.test.tsx`, 7 new: two `/summary` requests answered out of order (the newer shows, the slow one does not replace it, and the old request is aborted); retry rules; a mutation reads its list again and not an unrelated one; the error boundary keeps the shell, Try again resets the queries and draws the page, and another page clears it; signing out empties the cache; after the wizard the Vehicles page reads the vehicle it added instead of a cached empty list. The last two fail without their change. `test/support/kit.tsx`: `renderApp` gives each test a fresh cache (`testQueryClient`: retry off, `gcTime` 0) and can take one. No existing test was edited.
- Checks: `npm run typecheck:web`, `npm run lint` green; `npx vitest run apps/web` 52 files, 386 passed (379 before, plus 7). Before the change the same run had 6 failures under machine load (`findBy` waits of 1 s timing out; the files pass when run alone); after it, 386 of 386 passed.
- Not converted, with why: the setup wizard (`onboarding/Wizard.tsx`, its pairing step's poll in `GoalPhoneSteps.tsx`): every step awaits `ctx.reload()`, which reads five lists with a fallback each; moving that to queries changes the wizard's contract, so only the stale marking above was added. `pairing/GetTheApp.tsx` (`useAppInfo`): it also runs on the public `/app` and `/pair` pages, which have no session or query client. Setup, Restore, Login, Recovery key, Settings, Account, Import, Add trip and the earnings ledger read nothing on mount (they only send), so there was nothing to convert. The export download stays a plain request (a file the driver asked for, not data to cache).
- Decisions/limits: query keys hold the path template and its parameters (`['/shifts/{ref}', { path: { ref: '7' } }]`); a read that is more than one request (all pages of a week, the six analytics groups, four odometer kinds) has its own key or one key per request. `useApiQuery` options do not take `select` (keeps the types simple). The countries list is never stale (`staleTime: Infinity`; it changes only with a server upgrade).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the branch starts from the worktree HEAD `13a0143` (W14b) as asked; the brief named `caeed56` as the base, and the two differ in `Tax.tsx`, `tax.test.tsx`, `ShiftStates.tsx` and this file, so merging may need the Tax hunk redone on main. Answers stay fresh for 30 s: before, every page asked the server each time it opened; now a page opened again within 30 s shows what it read then, and a change made on the phone appears after that or when the driver comes back to the tab.
## PR T1 — Tests: verify reliably green, runner tuned, coverage

- Branch `pr-T1-test-stability`, on `main` (caeed56).
- Measured on an 8-core machine (`os.availableParallelism()` is 8) shared with other agents' test runs, so every timing below is noisy; the load average is given with each.
- Before, at caeed56: full `npx vitest run` at load 29 to 45: 143 files, 14 tests failed in 13 files, wall 182.9 s. `apps/web` alone at load 31: 35 tests failed in 19 files, 127.4 s. Every failure was a wait running out (findBy* after 1 s, a test after 5 s) or the engine timing test; each file passes when run alone on a quiet moment.
- Root causes and fixes:
  - `packages/track-engine/test/process.test.ts` and `stops.test.ts`, "5,000 fixes ... under 50 ms" (test was wrong): the budget was timed with `performance.now`, which also counts the time the process waits for a CPU, so a loaded machine read 53 ms with the engine unchanged. Now measured as the CPU time of the calling thread (`process.threadCpuUsage`, new `test/support/cpuTime.ts`); the budget (50 ms, median of 9 after a warm-up) is unchanged. The thread's time, not the process's: with `--pool=threads` `process.cpuUsage` counted the other test files' threads too (95 ms).
  - `apps/web/test/*` page tests (test setup was wrong): Testing Library's findBy* and waitFor give up after 1 s, and a page test mounts the whole app in jsdom, which took 1 to 8 s to render under load. New `apps/web/test/support/setup.ts` sets `asyncUtilTimeout` to 10 s for the web project; the route view (5 s) and onboarding pairing poll (4 s) tests no longer set their own shorter waits. A missing element still fails.
  - `apps/web/test/*` (runner was wrong for them): vitest's 5 s per test is too short for a page test that waits for several renders in turn (seven tests timed out at 5 s at load 31). The web project now has `testTimeout` and `hookTimeout` of 30 s; server, engine and scripts keep 5 s and 10 s so a hang there still fails fast.
- Not reproduced on this tree: `routeview.test.tsx` "draws on the OpenFreeMap basemap when no style URL is configured, and scrubs" (expected 1 map, got 0) passes alone three times and in every full run; its "got 0" is its own 5 s wait for the lazily loaded map running out under load, fixed by the wait above. `backups.test.tsx` "uploads the file and the key as multipart" (PR B1) also passes alone and in every full run; it timed out once (23 s, at load 44) under the old 5 s limit.
- Runner (`vitest.config.ts`): two projects, `web` (`apps/web/test`) and `node` (server, packages, scripts, infra), both `environment: 'node'`; pages keep their `@vitest-environment jsdom` comment, and server and engine tests already ran in node. Kept at vitest's defaults after measuring, with the reasons in a comment: the `forks` pool (threads: 101.3 s at load 18), all cores but one as workers (half the cores: 99.9 s and 110.0 s at load 20 to 25, against 93.5 s and 74.6 s with the default), `isolate: true` (without it the `vi.mock('maplibre-gl')` and module state would leak between files), and CSS, which vitest already replaces with empty modules.
- After: the full suite passed three times in a row, 81.6 s, 61.8 s and 62.5 s at load 14 to 19 (143 files, 1133 passed, 3 skipped). The old configuration at the same load took 64.0 s, so the change is in reliability, not speed. `npm run verify` green twice, 114.1 s and 114.5 s at load 12 to 18 (vitest 64.3 s and 81.9 s; phone 33 suites, 282 passed, 4 skipped, 29.6 s and 10.5 s).
- Coverage: `npm run test:coverage` (`@vitest/coverage-v8` 3.2, text and lcov into `coverage/`, already in `.gitignore`, now also ignored by ESLint) over `apps/*/src` and `packages/*/src`, without the phone (jest-expo), `apps/web/src/components/ui` (shadcn/ui) and generated code; all 243 source files are in the report. No thresholds yet. The coverage run sets `MILEWAGE_COVERAGE=1` and skips only the two engine speed budgets (V8's counters made one take 3.5 s; the budget is for plain Node and `npm test` checks it). Line coverage (V8): `packages/track-engine` 98.36 % (721 of 733), `apps/server` 98.32 % (12,315 of 12,525), `apps/web` 96.38 % (10,649 of 11,049), `packages/shared` 99.14 % (1,490 of 1,503); all files 97.53 % lines, 90.70 % branches, 93.53 % functions. Run time 75.4 s at load 16.
- Not done: no thresholds (a later PR can set them from these numbers). Under extreme load (load average near 100 on 8 cores, seen once) single page tests took up to 27 s; the 30 s limit covers that, but nothing can promise green at any load.
## PR H0 — Plan: home and away addresses, and linking a phone found on the Wi-Fi (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H0-home-away-plan`, on `main` (`d67522e`). Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `22dbbf9`); the branch was then deleted with the rest. Owner report (2026-11-08): "if its in the same network then it shouldnt be asking for tailscale" and "the link process needs to be smoother".
- Done: `plans/milewage/28-home-away-linking-plan.md`: the owner's two decisions (plain http only to private/home addresses; auto-find on the Wi-Fi + confirm code, QR as fallback), what is wrong today (release app http refusal, one stored address, the Tailscale screen before Connect, Caddy on loopback only, the always-`Secure` session cookie, the web's http warning, no discovery), research, design (shared home-address rule, home and away addresses in codes and redeem answers with QR `v=1` plus an optional `h`, the phone's probe-based pick, RFC 8628-shaped link requests, the LAN advertiser for dev and Docker, web changes), security notes for http on the LAN, PRs H1–H8, tests and remaining questions. Plan 19 Q3 and plan 24 screen 3 point to it.
- Standards checked: Home Assistant companion [networking](https://companion.home-assistant.io/docs/troubleshooting/networking/) (SSID-based internal URL, needs location all the time) and its [network security config](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/blob/main/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml) (cleartext allowed for all); Immich automatic URL switching ([v1.122](https://linuxiac.com/immich-1-122-brings-hdr-video-support-in-the-mobile-app/), [#21930](https://github.com/immich-app/immich/issues/21930)); Jellyfin [Quick Connect](https://jellyfin.org/docs/general/server/quick-connect/); [RFC 8628](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628) sections 3.3, 5.1, 5.2, 5.4; [RFC 6762](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6762) / [RFC 6763](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6763) / [RFC 6335 section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6335#section-5.1); Android [local network permission](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/local-network-permission) (enforced from target 37 only; do not request it before), [`NsdManager`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/net/nsd/NsdManager), [network security config](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-config); MDN [Set-Cookie `Secure`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Set-Cookie) (an http page cannot set a `Secure` cookie, localhost excepted); Docker and multicast ([HA Docker install](https://www.home-assistant.io/installation/linux#docker-compose): `network_mode: host`); [`@homebridge/ciao`](https://github.com/homebridge/ciao).
- Tests: none (plan only).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the phone shows the code and the driver types it on the web (RFC 8628 and Jellyfin Quick Connect), not a one-tap Approve with the code on both screens (plan 28 section 3.4, Q1); probing instead of the Wi-Fi name, so no location "all the time" is needed (section 3.1).

## PR H1 — Server: home and away addresses in pairing codes (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H1-home-away-server`, stacked on `pr-H0-home-away-plan`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `dedb290`); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Done (plan 28 sections 4.1 and 4.2):
  - **One rule for "home address"**, `packages/shared/src/home-address.ts` (server, web and phone use it): private IPv4 (`10/8`, `172.16/12`, `192.168/16`), IPv4 link-local `169.254/16`, IPv6 unique local `fc00::/7` and link-local `fe80::/10` (also IPv4-mapped and with a zone id), and the names `*.local`, `*.lan`, `*.home.arpa`. `localhost`, loopback, public addresses and Tailscale's `100.64/10` are not home addresses. `httpAllowed(url)`: https always, http only to a home address. Written without `URL`, because Hermes' `URL` has no `hostname`.
  - New setting **`MILEWAGE_HOME_URL`** (no default): the server on the home network, http or https, only a home address with nothing after the host; a wrong value stops the server with a sentence naming the setting.
  - `POST /pairing-tokens` answers **`home_url`** and **`away_url`**. Away: the driver's correction, `MILEWAGE_PUBLIC_URL`, or the request's address when it is https and not a home address. Home: the driver's correction when it is a home address, `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL`, or the request's address when it is one. `base_url` (the code's `u`) is away, else home, else the request's address, as before.
  - **The code stays `v=1`** and gains an optional `h=<home address>` (in `qr_payload` and in the `/pair#` link the QR shows) when the home address differs from `u`. The phone's parser already ignores unknown parameters, so an older app reads `u` as before.
  - `POST /pairing/redeem` also answers `home_url` and `away_url` for the address the phone redeemed at, so a phone linked at home learns the Tailscale address and the other way round.
  - **Behaviour change (owner decision 2026-11-08):** a production server now accepts a plain `http://` address from the driver when it is a home address (it becomes `home_url`); any other http address is still refused, now with "…or be on your home network (for example http://192.168.1.20:8080)".
- Standards checked: [RFC 1918](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1918) (private IPv4), [RFC 3927](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3927) (IPv4 link-local), [RFC 4193](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4193) (IPv6 unique local `fc00::/7`; `fd00::/8` is the locally assigned half), [RFC 4291 section 2.5.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4291#section-2.5.6) (`fe80::/10`), [RFC 6762](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6762) (`.local`), [RFC 8375](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8375) (`home.arpa`), [RFC 6598](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6598) (`100.64/10` shared address space, used by Tailscale, not a home range); the WHATWG URL origin as in T2.
- Tests: `packages/shared/test/home-address.test.ts` 3 new (every range and name, the range edges `172.15`/`172.32`, `fec0::`, loopback, `100.100.1.2`, `evil.local.example.com`, user information hiding the host, odd schemes); `apps/server/test/devices.test.ts` 1 new and 2 changed (the web on the home address with Tailscale configured: `u` away, `h` home in both forms, the redeem over Tailscale answers the away address and no home; `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL` with the web on `localhost`: `u` home, no `h`, redeem at home; a production server takes http only to a home address); `apps/server/test/index.test.ts` 1 new (`MILEWAGE_HOME_URL`: home http and https accepted, Tailscale, localhost, public and paths refused). All fail without the change.
- Not done: passing `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL` in the compose file comes with LAN access (H4); the web shows the two addresses in H5, the phone uses them in H6.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the driver's corrected address replaces the home address when it is a home address and the away address otherwise (one field on the web, as today).

## PR H2 — Server: signing in works on the home network over plain http (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H2-cookie-http-lan`, stacked on `pr-H1-home-away-server`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `0c43889`); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Problem (plan 28 H-P5): the session cookie was always `Secure`. A browser drops a `Secure` cookie that a plain `http://` page sets (only `localhost` is excepted), so once Caddy is reachable on the LAN (H4), signing in at `http://192.168.x.x:8080` would answer 204 and still leave the driver signed out. The dev server hid this by stripping `Secure` in the Vite proxy.
- Done: `Secure` is set when the page is https: the browser's `Origin` on the sign-in request starts with `https:`, or Fastify's `protocol` is https (it reads `X-Forwarded-Proto` only from the trusted proxy, `MILEWAGE_TRUST_PROXY`; through Caddy that is `tailscale serve`'s `https`). On an http page the cookie is sent without `Secure`; `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` and `Path=/` stay. Setup, sign-in and sign-out (the clearing cookie) follow the same rule. The Vite proxy workaround is removed. `08` section 2 notes it.
- Standards checked: MDN [Set-Cookie, `Secure`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Set-Cookie) ("Insecure sites (http:) cannot set cookies with the Secure attribute"; localhost excepted); [RFC 6265bis draft, "Secure" attribute and non-secure origins](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpbis-rfc6265bis/) (a user agent ignores a `Secure` cookie from a non-secure request); [Fastify `request.protocol` and `trustProxy`](https://fastify.dev/docs/latest/Reference/Server/#trustproxy); the `Origin` header is set by the browser and cannot be changed by page scripts ([Fetch, forbidden request headers](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#forbidden-request-header)).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/auth.test.ts` 2 new and 1 changed (an https page gets `Secure`, an http page signs in without it, sign-out clears with `Secure` on https; behind the trusted proxy `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` gives `Secure` and the same header from a LAN address is not believed; setup from an http page has no `Secure`). They fail without the change.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): on the home address the session cookie travels in clear on the home Wi-Fi, like the phone's token (plan 28 section 5); the web says so next to the home address (H5).

## PR H3 — Server: link a phone with a code (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H3-link-requests`, stacked on `pr-H2-cookie-http-lan`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `3e7fb95`, with the follow-up `017d2de` (H3b)); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Done (plan 28 section 4.4), module `devices`, `apps/server/src/routes/link-requests.ts`:
  - `POST /link-requests` (the phone, no sign-in): `{device_name, platform}` → `201 {device_code: "mwl_…" (256 random bits), user_code: 6 digits, expires_in: 600, interval: 2}`. At most 5 waiting phones per client address and 20 in all (`429 rate_limited`); the 6-digit code is unique among waiting phones.
  - `POST /link-requests/poll` (the phone): `202 {status: "pending"}` until the driver types the code; then **once** `201` with the redeem body (`device_id`, `device_token`, `settings`, `home_url`, `away_url`); `410 link_expired` after expiry, "Not my phone", use, or for an unknown code; a poll sooner than `interval` is `429 slow_down` with `Retry-After: 2` (250 ms slack for early timers).
  - `GET /link-requests` (web, session): the waiting phones with name, platform, the address they asked from and times; never the codes.
  - `POST /link-requests/approve` (web, session, Origin checked): `{user_code}`, spaces and dashes ignored; a wrong code is `422 invalid_link_code`; 10 wrong codes per 15 minutes per driver, then `429`. Approval makes an ordinary one-time pairing token for the driver; the phone's next poll redeems it through the existing `redeem`, so a linked phone is the same `devices` row as a scanned one.
  - `DELETE /link-requests/{id}` (web, session): Not my phone.
  - Requests live **in memory** for 10 minutes, no table: a restart drops them and the phone asks again (H7). New problem codes `link_expired` (410), `invalid_link_code` (422), `slow_down` (429). OpenAPI and generated types updated.
- Why this shape (plan 28 section 3.4): the short code is accepted only from a signed-in session, so nobody on the Wi-Fi can guess it; the phone's secret is the long `device_code`; typing the code picks the right phone even when two ask at once.
- Standards checked: [RFC 8628](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628) section 3.2 (`interval`, default 5 s), 3.5 (`authorization_pending`, `slow_down`, `expired_token`), 5.1 (rate-limit user-code attempts), 5.2 (high-entropy device code), 5.4 (show what is asking: the web shows the phone's name and address); Jellyfin [Quick Connect](https://jellyfin.org/docs/general/server/quick-connect/) (6-character code shown on the new device, typed on a signed-in one); Node [`crypto.randomInt`](https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html#cryptorandomintmin-max-callback) (uniform, no modulo bias).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/link-requests.test.ts` 7 new: the whole flow (pending, the web list without codes, a code typed with a space, the credential works, the phone is on the Devices page with both addresses in the answer, a second poll is gone); `slow_down`, unknown and expired; wrong codes and the 10-per-15-minutes limit; Not my phone; the web routes need a session; 5 per address, 20 in all, unique codes, room again after expiry; bad bodies. They fail without the change.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): 6 digits and 10 minutes (Jellyfin uses 6 characters; RFC 8628's example is 8 characters with a few guesses, but here only the signed-in driver can guess at all).

## PR H4 — Server: announce the server on the home Wi-Fi (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H4-lan-discovery-server`, stacked on `pr-H3-link-requests`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `aa0e105`); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Done (plan 28 section 4.6, the dev half; Docker follows in H4b):
  - `apps/server/src/lan/advertise.ts`: announces **`_milewage._tcp`** over mDNS / DNS-SD while the server answers `/healthz`, withdraws the announcement (goodbye packets) when it stops answering, and follows the instance id. The TXT record holds only `v=1`, `id=<instance id>` (absent before the owner account exists) and `path=/`. The name is "MileWage (<host name>)", one DNS label of at most 63 bytes, else "MileWage"; a clash on the network is renamed by the library ("MileWage (box) (2)").
  - `advertise.mjs` (new bundle entry, `apps/server/src/advertise-main.ts`): `MILEWAGE_ADVERTISE_PORT` (the port phones connect to), `MILEWAGE_HEALTH_URL` (default `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/healthz`), `MILEWAGE_ADVERTISE_NAME`; looks every 15 s; stops cleanly on SIGTERM. It runs **beside** the API, not in it, because in Docker the API's bridge network does not carry multicast.
  - `npm run dev -- --host` (Vite on the LAN): `scripts/dev.mjs` gives the API `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL=http://<first private IPv4>:<Vite port>` and runs the advertiser against the API's `/healthz`; prints the home address. Without `--host`, or with `MILEWAGE_LAN_DISCOVERY=0`, nothing changes. The Vite port is read from `--port` (default 5173); if Vite moves to another port because 5173 is taken, pass `--port` with `--strictPort`.
  - New dependency **`@homebridge/ciao` 1.3.12** (MIT; Homebridge's mDNS responder, passes Apple's Bonjour Conformance Test; 4 small dependencies). 1.4.0 is a week old, so the previous release is pinned (plan 28 corrected).
- Standards checked: [RFC 6762](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6762) (probing and conflict renaming section 9, goodbye packets section 10.1), [RFC 6763](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6763) (instance names section 4.1.1, TXT `key=value` and a version key section 6), [RFC 6335 section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6335#section-5.1) (service names); [`@homebridge/ciao`](https://github.com/homebridge/ciao) README (responder only, conformance results); Vite [`server.host`](https://vite.dev/config/server-options#server-host) (localhost only unless `--host`).
- Behaviour check (by hand, this machine, a throwaway health server on port 18099): the bundled `advertise.mjs` announced "MileWage (coder)"; a raw mDNS PTR query for `_milewage._tcp.local` from another socket was answered from <lan-address> with the instance id in the TXT record.
- Tests: `apps/server/test/lan-advertise.test.ts` 3 new (nothing before the server answers; announced without an id before setup, the id added with one TXT update, nothing sent when unchanged, withdrawn when down, announced again, shut down on stop; the record and the name rules; health from the header, a 503 and a network error); `scripts/lan.test.mjs` 2 new (the first private IPv4, never loopback or Tailscale's range; only with `--host`, `--port` in both forms, off with `MILEWAGE_LAN_DISCOVERY=0`). They fail without the change.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the dev server is announced whenever it is opened to the LAN with `--host`.

## PR H4b — Infra: LAN access in the Docker stack (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H4b-lan-docker`, stacked on `pr-H4-lan-discovery-server`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `c5b952e`, with the follow-up `08de9c7` (H4c)); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Done (plan 28 section 4.6, the Docker half):
  - Compose: Caddy is published on **`${MILEWAGE_BIND_ADDRESS:-127.0.0.1}`** (loopback by default, as before); the API gets **`MILEWAGE_HOME_URL`**; a new service **`lan-discovery`** in the profile `lan` runs `node advertise.mjs` from the API's image with `network_mode: host` (multicast does not cross the bridge), `MILEWAGE_ADVERTISE_PORT` = the published port, no health check (the image's check is the API's), 64 MB, a quarter CPU, its own build section so the image exists before it starts. The Dockerfile copies `advertise.mjs`.
  - `infra/install.sh`: **`--lan`** writes `MILEWAGE_BIND_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0`, `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL=http://<host LAN address>:<port>` and `COMPOSE_PROFILES=lan` (Compose reads the profile from `.env`), also into a kept `.env`, replacing lines rather than adding; the address is the source of the default route (`ip -4 route get`), or `MILEWAGE_LAN_IP`, and only a private IPv4 is used, else it says LAN access stays off. **`--no-lan`** writes loopback and empties the other two. In a terminal, with neither flag and nothing in `.env` yet, it asks `Let phones on your home Wi-Fi connect without Tailscale (plain http, this network only)? [Y/n]` (plan 28 Q2: ask, default yes). Without a terminal and without a flag nothing changes. The end message names the home address.
  - `install.md` section 2a **Home network access**: what it does, the three `.env` lines, the announcer and the Avahi service-file alternative, a fixed address on the router, and what to know: http is not encrypted on the home Wi-Fi (token, shifts and sign-in cookie), Docker's published ports bypass ufw/firewalld (bind to the LAN address to limit it), rootless Docker or `"iptables": false` is not supported (the userland proxy can make every client look like the trusted gateway).
- Standards checked: Compose [profiles](https://docs.docker.com/compose/how-tos/profiles/) and [`COMPOSE_PROFILES` from `.env`](https://docs.docker.com/compose/how-tos/environment-variables/envvars/#compose_profiles), [`network_mode: host`](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/#network_mode), [port publishing and firewalls](https://docs.docker.com/engine/network/packet-filtering-firewalls/) (published ports bypass ufw), [`healthcheck.disable`](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/#healthcheck); Home Assistant's container install with host networking for discovery ([install, Docker Compose](https://www.home-assistant.io/installation/linux#docker-compose)); [`avahi.service(5)`](https://linux.die.net/man/5/avahi.service).
- Checked by hand: `docker compose config` with `COMPOSE_PROFILES=lan` and `MILEWAGE_BIND_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0` resolves Caddy's port on `0.0.0.0` and the `lan-discovery` service with `network_mode: host`. The stack was **not** started on this machine (no containers built; the owner's server is where it runs).
- Tests: `apps/server/test/status.test.ts` 1 new and 1 extended (loopback by default, `MILEWAGE_HOME_URL` passed, the announcer only in the `lan` profile with the host network and the API's image, no ports, the Dockerfile copies `advertise.mjs`; log rotation also for `lan-discovery`); `infra/install.test.mjs` 3 new (`--lan` writes the three lines and the address, `--no-lan` on the kept `.env` replaces them; a public address leaves it off; no flag and no terminal writes nothing). They fail without the change.
- Not done: `infra/docker/smoke.sh` still checks the default (loopback); a LAN-mode smoke run needs a host with a LAN.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the install script asks with "yes" as the default answer in a terminal (plan 28 Q2).

## PR H6 — Phone: home and away addresses, http only on the home network (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H6-phone-home-away`, stacked on `pr-H5-web-link-a-phone`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `39019f6`); the branch was then deleted with the rest.
- Done (plan 28 sections 4.1 to 4.3):
  - **http only to home addresses, enforced in code.** The release build now allows cleartext at the platform (`usesCleartextTraffic: true`), because Android's network security config can name domains but not IP ranges. Every request goes through `ApiClient`, which refuses plain http to anything that is not a home address (shared `httpAllowed`) with `insecure_address` **before anything is sent**; debug builds (`__DEV__`) still take any http, as before. The Connect screen refuses such a code with "This code uses an unsecured address (http) outside your home network…"; a home http address is accepted in the release build.
  - **Two addresses per phone.** The credential gains `homeUrl` and `awayUrl` (an older credential reads its one address as home or away). Home: the address used when it is a home address, else the code's `h`, else the server's `home_url`; away: the address used when it is not a home address, else the server's `away_url`. The server's values are checked (home must be a home address, away https outside it), so an answer cannot lower the rules. A code's `h` is read only when it is a home address.
  - **The pick** (`connect/route.ts`): one address → that one; on mobile data alone → away without trying home; otherwise `GET <home>/healthz` within 1.5 s, which must be a MileWage answer from the same instance → home, else away. Remembered for 60 s; a request with no answer forgets it, so the next one looks again (walking out of the house switches to away). No Wi-Fi name and no location permission.
  - **Redeeming a code at home**: a code with `h` is redeemed at the home address when the phone is on Wi-Fi and home answers, so at home the phone never needs Tailscale to connect.
  - A network failure to a home address says "Can't reach your server at http://…. Check that this phone is on the same Wi-Fi as the server and that the server is on." (no Tailscale).
  - `native/net-check`: `localNetwork()`, true on Wi-Fi or Ethernet (also under a VPN, whose network carries the transports under it), false on mobile data, null with no network.
- Standards checked: Android [network security configuration](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-config) (`domain-config` names domains only) and [`usesCleartextTraffic`](https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/manifest/application-element#usesCleartextTraffic); Home Assistant's app allows cleartext for everything in its [network security config](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/blob/main/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml) (MileWage is stricter: the code gate); [`NetworkCapabilities.hasTransport`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/net/NetworkCapabilities#hasTransport(int)) (`TRANSPORT_WIFI`, `TRANSPORT_ETHERNET`); Android [local network permission](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/local-network-permission) (target 36: implicit with INTERNET; `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` must not be declared before target 37); Immich and Plex (plan 28 section 3.1) for reachability instead of the Wi-Fi name.
- Tests: `test/homeaway.test.ts` 12 new (http to public hosts refused with nothing sent, home http and `.local` sent, debug build takes any; `h` read, a non-home `h` ignored, the `/pair#` form; the home failure message; the picker: one address, an old credential, Wi-Fi with home answering, mobile data without a probe, unknown network tries, home silent → away, a minute of memory and forget; the probe: same instance, any instance, another instance, not MileWage, 503, timeout; a code redeemed at home with both addresses kept; on mobile data redeemed away with home from the code; requests go home, then away after the house is left without asking; server values that would lower the rules are dropped); `test/connect.test.tsx` updated (http outside the home network refused in release, home http and `.local` accepted); `test/manifest.test.ts` 1 new and 1 extended (cleartext on in `app.json`, no `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` or `NEARBY_WIFI_DEVICES`; the merged release manifest after a build); `test/secureStore.test.ts` now redeems at an https address (plain `http://x` is refused now). JUnit `LocalNetworkTest` 4 new (Wi-Fi, Ethernet, Tailscale over Wi-Fi local; Tailscale over mobile data not; no network unknown). They fail without the change.
- Size: 291 source + 272 test lines (over the usual 400 in all; the tests are half).
- Not done: discovery and linking with a code (H7); Settings and the Home notice showing both addresses, Tailscale hints only when away (H8).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): 1.5 s for the home check and a minute of memory; on mobile data the home address is never tried (a Tailscale subnet router that reaches the home address from outside is not used).

## PR H7a — Phone: find the server on the Wi-Fi, and link with a code (the logic) (plan 28)

- Branch `pr-H7a-phone-discovery`, stacked on `pr-H6-phone-home-away`. Merged 2026-11-08 (squash, `db25bdb`); the branch was then deleted with the rest. The screens follow in H7b.
- Done (plan 28 sections 4.4 and 4.5):
  - `native/net-check`: **`discover(type, browseMs)`** browses for `_milewage._tcp` with Android's `NsdManager` (`discoverServices`, stopped after `browseMs`, 1–15 s), then resolves each found service **one at a time** (before Android 14 NsdManager resolves only one at a time; a resolve that never answers is passed after 3 s) and returns name, one address (IPv4 first; IPv6 without its zone), port and the TXT record as text. NsdManager runs in the system: no multicast lock. With target SDK 36 nothing beyond INTERNET is needed (Android 17's `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` applies from target 37; plan 28 Q4).
  - `connect/discover.ts`: `serversFrom` keeps only announcements whose address is a **home address** (an announcement cannot send the phone to the internet), with record version 1 (or none), a valid port, each address once, and the instance id from TXT `id`. `NetPort.discover()` browses for 4 s.
  - `Pairing.startLink(url)`: `POST /link-requests` with the phone's name; checks the answer (a 6-digit code, the device code) and returns the code, the expiry and the poll interval (at least 1 s). `Pairing.pollLink(url, deviceCode)`: null while pending (202); once linked it stores the connection exactly like a scanned code (home = the address used, away = the server's checked `away_url`).
- Standards checked: Android [`NsdManager`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/net/nsd/NsdManager) (`discoverServices`, `resolveService`, `NsdServiceInfo.getHostAddresses` from API 34, `getAttributes` for TXT) and the [NSD guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/connectivity/wifi/use-nsd); [local network permission](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/local-network-permission); [RFC 6763 section 6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6763#section-6) (TXT `key=value`, a key without value); [RFC 8628](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628) section 3.5 for the poll answers.
- Tests: `test/discover.test.ts` 3 new (home addresses only, IPv6 in brackets, a later record version and a bad port skipped, duplicates once; the link asked with the phone's name, pending, then linked with both addresses and paired; `link_expired` and a strange answer named); JUnit `ServiceEntryTest` 3 new (IPv4 first and TXT as text, IPv6 without zone, no address or port is nothing). They fail without the change.
- Not checked on a phone: NsdManager on the owner's S24 (Android 16); the release build compiles the module (see the APK build in H7b).
