## PR Z1 — Loose ends: committed dev data out of git, backups default beside the data folder, merged-manifest checks in the phone-apk job

- Done:
  - The folder `.dev.before-integrate-2026-11-08/` (a dev server's `milewage.db`, `diagnostics.db`, `secrets.json` and three `.age` backups, committed by mistake in cada039) is taken out of the git index with `git rm -r --cached`; the files stay on disk. `.gitignore` ignores `.dev.before-integrate-*/` next to `.dev/`.
  - Stray `backups/.tmp` at the repo root: the cause was the `startServer` test in `apps/server/test/db.test.ts`, which sets `MILEWAGE_DATA_DIR` and `MILEWAGE_CONFIG_DIR` but not `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DIR`. The server starts the nightly backup job, whose start empties and recreates `<backups>/.tmp`, and the default `backups` was relative to the folder vitest runs in (the repo root). Fixed at the default instead of the test: `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DIR` now defaults to `backups` next to the data folder (`join(dirname(dataDir), 'backups')`). With no settings this is still `backups`; in the image it is `/app/backups`, the same as the Dockerfile sets. The empty `backups/.tmp` and `backups/` at the repo root are removed (nothing under `backups` is tracked).
  - The phone-apk CI job runs `MILEWAGE_REQUIRE_MERGED_MANIFEST=1 npm test -w apps/phone -- test/manifest.test.ts` after `scripts/phone-release-build.sh`, so the four merged-manifest checks (no forbidden permission, no ML Kit, can see Tailscale, only known permissions) run on the APK that was just built. `verify` runs before any build, where they skip. With the new switch, a missing or stale merged manifest fails the test instead of skipping it, so a build that leaves no manifest cannot pass with everything skipped. The job is still manual with `allow_failure: true`, and its artifacts are unchanged.
  - The freshness check in `manifest.test.ts` no longer counts Gradle's cache folders (`.gradle/`) or files directly inside a `build/` folder as sources (before, only files deeper than `build/` were left out).
- Standards checked: GitLab CI `script` lines run in order and the job fails on the first non-zero exit, so a second script line is enough and no `after_script` is needed (`after_script` cannot fail a job). Jest's `it.skip` cannot fail, so a separate test switched on by an environment variable is the usual way to make an optional check required in one place. Node `path.dirname` gives `.` for a bare `data`, so the old default is kept.
- Tests: `apps/server/test/config.test.ts` (new, 3 tests: default beside the data folder, `/app/data` → `/app/backups`, `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DIR` wins); `npx vitest run apps/server/test/db.test.ts` no longer leaves `backups/` in the repo root (it did before the fix); `apps/server/test/index.test.ts` 16 passed; `infra/ci.test.mjs` 7 passed (new test: the phone-apk script lines, and the manifest test reads the switch); `npm test -w apps/phone -- test/manifest.test.ts` 8 passed, 5 skipped; with `MILEWAGE_REQUIRE_MERGED_MANIFEST=1` it fails because the merged manifest on `coder` is from 2026-11-07 13:48, older than its sources. `tsc --noEmit` for the root and phone projects is clean; prettier and eslint are clean on the changed files.
- Not done: no release build was run (no Gradle here), so the merged-manifest checks have not yet run on a fresh manifest. A read-only check of the old 2026-11-07 manifest shows it would fail three of them (no cleartext attribute, ML Kit present, no Tailscale query). It was built before ZXing, net-check and plan 28, so it says nothing about the current app; the next release build will tell. `buildBackupService` in `apps/server/src/modules.ts` still falls back to `'backups'` when a caller passes no `backupDir`; that file is outside this PR, and no current test runs a backup that way.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): a server given only `MILEWAGE_DATA_DIR=/some/where/data` should keep its backups in `/some/where/backups` (beside the data folder, like the image's `/app/data` and `/app/backups`), not inside the data folder. `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DIR` still overrides it.

## PR Z2 — A new recovery key after the old one is lost (Server, Web, Docs)

- Done, server: `POST /backups/recovery-key` (`createRecoveryKey`) and `POST /backups/recovery-key/confirm` (`confirmNewRecoveryKey`), in the `backup` module (`apps/server/src/routes/backup.ts`, `recoveryKeyRoutes`). The first takes `{password}` and checks it again even with a valid session (401 `invalid_credentials` when wrong; 5 wrong passwords per 15 minutes per owner, then 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`). It makes an age key pair and returns `{recovery_key, state: "needs_confirmation"}` once. Only the public key and a hash of the last 6 characters are kept, in memory, until the confirmation; `config/secrets.json` is not touched, so backups keep using the old key. A second request replaces a key that was not confirmed; a restart drops it. The confirmation takes `{last6}` (any case; 422 `validation_failed` on `/last6` when it does not match, 422 when no key is waiting), writes the new public key to `secrets.json` and makes one `manual` backup with it at once. It answers `{state: "ready", backup}`; `backup` is null when that backup failed (the key change stands; the driver uses Back up now). The backup service has a new `runAfterCurrent`: a backup already running with the old key does not stand in for the new one. Old backups are not re-encrypted. `modules.ts` passes the shared secrets store to `backupRoutes` (one line). The immediate backup shows up in diagnostics as the usual `backup.run` event; no new event kind was added. The server log gets one line "recovery key replaced" (no key in it).
- Done, contract: the two paths, `NewRecoveryKeyRequest`, `NewRecoveryKey` and `NewRecoveryKeyConfirmed` in `openapi.yaml`, both operations in `x-modules.backup`; generated files regenerated.
- Done, web: Backups page, a separate section "Lost your recovery key?" (`apps/web/src/pages/NewRecoveryKey.tsx`) with "Make a new recovery key". The dialog asks for the password and says that backups made before today still need the old key; then it shows the key once with a Copy button and the same "Last 6 characters of the key" / "I saved it" step as setup; then it says new backups use the new key and that a backup was made (or that it failed). A click outside does not close the dialog while the key is on screen. The setup screen (`RecoveryKey.tsx`) is unchanged: it is tied to `/setup/confirm` and the full-page card, so it was not reused. Added the standard shadcn Dialog (`components/ui/dialog.tsx`, radix-nova style from the shadcn registry; `radix-ui` was already a dependency).
- Done, docs: new driver guide `docs/if-you-lose-your-recovery-key.md` (what the key does, what cannot be recovered, the steps, older backups and retention, a leaked key), linked from `docs/README.md`; the FAQ answer "What if I forget the recovery key?" now points to it.
- Standards checked: 10 section 9 "Rotation" already says a lost or leaked age key is handled by generating a new key and old backups stay readable only with the old card; that is what is built. Same answer as Bitwarden, Vaultwarden and Nextcloud end-to-end encryption (a lost key or recovery code cannot be recovered) and restic `key add`/`key remove` (the new key only protects what it can reach; here old files stay encrypted to the old key, since the server never has a private key to re-encrypt them). Password again before a sensitive change: GitHub's sudo mode, OWASP re-authentication advice. Route name: under `/backups` because the key exists only for backups (08 section 8 lists the backup routes there, and `/setup/*` is open before setup is confirmed); the noun `recovery-key` and the `/confirm` step mirror `/setup` and `/setup/confirm`. Same problem details as elsewhere.

Ran `npx vitest run apps/server/test/recovery-key.test.ts` (8 passed, 3 runs), `npx vitest run apps/server/test/backup.test.ts apps/server/test/setup.test.ts apps/server/test/matrix.test.ts apps/server/test/restore.test.ts apps/server/test/backup-offsite.test.ts apps/server/test/error-types.test.ts` (79 passed, 1 skipped), `npx vitest run apps/web/test/backups.test.tsx` (13 passed), `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`, `npx tsc --noEmit -p apps/web/tsconfig.json`, eslint and prettier on the touched files, `npm run openapi:check`. The full suite was not run.
- Not done: no new diagnostics event kind for "key replaced" (needs a new schema in `packages/shared` diagnostics kinds, a bus event and a recorder entry; the immediate backup's `backup.run` event is recorded). 08 section 8's route table and 10 section 9 do not yet name the two routes (plans not in this PR's scope). No notification to other signed-in browsers. The phone app has no part in this.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): the waiting key is kept in memory only, so a server restart between the two steps means starting again. The immediate backup is recorded as `manual` (no new trigger value, so no migration) and so counts toward the 15-minute limit of Back up now. Retention keeps only the newest backup of a day, so an old-key backup made earlier the same day is replaced by the new-key one; the dialog says "backups made before today".

## PR Z3 — MCP audit rows pruned after a year; server settings reference

- Done:
  - `mcp_audit` retention (plan 26 section 3.7 and Q6): `AUDIT_KEPT_MS` (365 days) and `createAuditPrune` in `apps/server/src/mcp/audit.ts`, a `mcp-audit-prune` job of the `mcp` module (`apps/server/src/modules.ts`). Like the route archive, a first pass runs a minute after start, then one a day, so a server restarted every day still prunes. The statement is prepared on each pass, so a server in read-only mode on an old schema (no `mcp_audit` yet) logs the failure and keeps running. A pass that drops rows logs `mcp audit pruned`. Test seam `mcpHousekeepingMs` in `apps/server/test/support.ts`. The earlier attempt ran the pass synchronously inside `start()` and prepared the statement when the modules were built (that would stop a read-only server from starting); both changed.
  - `infra/docs/configuration.md`: every `MILEWAGE_*` setting in eight groups (server runtime, backups, diagnostics, network and addresses, modules, tax and country options, phone app download, release and build), with what it does, the default in the code, the image or compose default where it differs, and where it is set. Also: the order default, then `settings.json`, then the environment; which settings the compose file passes on (and that a module switch other than `MCP` in `.env` never reaches the API); the `settings.json` keys; names that are not settings (`MILEWAGE_MODE`, `MILEWAGE_AUTH`). Links from `infra/docs/install.md` section 5 and the README "Running it:" sentence.
- Standards checked: the neighbouring jobs (`change-log-prune` 30 days and `capture-purge` daily `setInterval`, `route-archive` first pass after `min(60 s, interval)`, the diagnostics keeper); no new scheduling mechanism. No index: the table grows by at most 30 writes a minute per token and is scanned once a day, so the existing `(user_id, at)` index is enough. The doc follows [Twelve-Factor config](https://12factor.net/config) as the code does (environment over file over default); every default read from the code, not the plans.
- Tests: `npx vitest run apps/server/test/mcp-audit.test.ts` (3 passed: a row older than a year goes, newer rows stay; the job runs inside the server with the module on; a missing table is logged, not thrown); `npx vitest run apps/server/test/mcp-write-tools.test.ts apps/server/test/mcp-write-more.test.ts apps/server/test/mcp-tokens.test.ts apps/server/test/mcp-endpoint.test.ts` (15 passed); `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean; eslint and prettier clean on the touched files.
- Not done: no test runs a server with real data across a year (the clock is the harness's). `MILEWAGE_COVERAGE` (a track-engine test switch) is left out of the doc on purpose.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): plan 26 Q6, one year (365 days) for assistant change rows. Export format `pdf` is in the server's switch list but no exporter is registered, so the doc lists only the five real formats.

## PR Z4 — Phone: the Location switch is read, and `shift.summary` is sent at shift end

- Done: the tracker module (`native/comma-tracker`) gains two read-only functions: `locationEnabled()` (`LocationManagerCompat.isLocationEnabled`) and `battery()` (`BatteryManager` capacity in per cent and `isCharging`). Both return null when the phone cannot say. `src/tracker/native.ts` reads them lazily (`nativeLocationSwitch`, `nativeBattery`) and answers null on an APK built before they existed, so nothing changes on an old build.
- Done: `productionParts` passes `locationEnabled: nativeLocationSwitch` to `AndroidPermissions`. With the switch off the ladder's existing warning ("Location is switched off on this phone. Turn it on or no miles are recorded.") shows on the Permissions screen, the mode is `none` and a shift cannot start. Null keeps today's "assumed on".
- Done: `shift.summary` (16 section 3.2). The shift controller reads the battery at "Start shift" and at "End shift", keeps both in the persisted active shift, and when `shift.end` is queued hands a summary to diagnostics (`PhoneDiagnostics.recordShiftSummary`, tagged with the shift id, then persisted). Fields: `duration_s`, `battery_start`, `battery_end`, `charging_seen` (charging at start or end), `fixes` (stored rows), `gap_count` (`gap_counted` plus `gap_dropped`; `gap_large` only qualifies a counted gap), `service_restarts` 0, `engine_ms` (time of the phone engine run), `capture` false. No coordinates, no ids in the data. If either battery level is unknown the event is not sent: the allowlist requires both and a guessed level would mislead H7. A failing battery read never stops a start or an end.
- Done: the earlier draft `src/diagnostics/shiftSummary.ts` was rewritten: it read a `LocalRun.ms` that does not exist, counted a large gap twice and was built on `expo-battery`, which is not installed.
- Standards checked: `expo-location` (`hasServicesEnabledAsync`) and `expo-battery` (`getBatteryLevelAsync`) are the Expo wrappers, but neither is a dependency, and `expo-location` would merge its own location permissions into the manifest. Both wrap the same platform calls used here (`LocationManagerCompat.isLocationEnabled` from androidx.core, already on the classpath; `BatteryManager.BATTERY_PROPERTY_CAPACITY`, API 21, and `isCharging`, API 23; minSdk is 24). No new dependency and no new permission.
- Tests: `test/permissions.android.test.ts` (switch off gives the warning, mode `none`, no start; on or unknown gives neither; the native reader and an old build without it). New `test/shiftsummary.test.ts` (one valid event with the shift id and exactly the allowlisted fields, kept on disk; charging seen; not sent when either level is unknown or the reader throws, and the shift still ends; gap counting; the native battery reader). `npm test -w apps/phone -- <file>` for those two and the neighbouring controller, diagnostics, permissions and tracker suites: all pass. `npm run typecheck:phone` passes. eslint and prettier clean on the changed files.
- Not done: the Kotlin is not compiled (no Gradle run here) and nothing ran on a phone. Home checklist and the Start-shift toast do not name the switch: with the switch off the Home item "Allow location" shows as not done and Start shows "Location permission is needed to record a shift.", which is the wrong cause; copy for those two places was not changed. `service_restarts` stays 0 until the service reports its restarts (09 section 5.4). A charge between start and end is not seen. A shift continued from the server ("adopt") sends no summary (its start battery is unknown).
- Assumption (owner to confirm): blocking "Start shift" while the Location switch is off is right (the ladder already said so; it could not fire before). `service_restarts: 0` is acceptable until the native count exists.
- Follow-up by the coordinating session: the Start-shift check on Home now says "Location is switched off on this phone. Turn it on to record a shift." when the switch is off and the permission is granted (it blamed the permission before); test in `shift.screen.test.tsx`.

## PR Z5 — Phone: shifts sent again by themselves after the server was restored (15 section 9.3)

- Worked in the tree on `main` beside other agents; not committed.
- Done (15 section 9.3, 3.2, 6.1, 6.2, 8.4):
  - **Automatic, no new button.** 15 section 9.3 says the phone "resends" when it sees a higher restore generation, and words the notice "The server was restored from a backup. MileWage is re-sending the last 14 days from your phone." So it happens on the next sync, and **Sync now** starts it at once. The banner now says that sentence (it said shifts stay "on phone only" until sent again).
  - **What is sent again.** When the restore is seen, the monitor writes one `resend:<client_id>` row in `sync_state` for every shift whose create is not still queued: the shifts that were on the server, the ones half sent, and the running one. The rows live in `app.db`, so eviction stays blocked across an app restart until each one is accepted again (before, the block was a flag in memory). The restore generation is now stored after the rows, so an app killed halfway sees the restore again.
  - **How** (`src/sync/resend.ts`, before the backlog in the drain): per shift, oldest first, the running shift first: `POST /shifts` (201 created again, 200 kept), then every acknowledged point still on the phone in batches of 500 from the last seq accepted, then the odometer readings, `end` (manifest and phone result from the points, or from the stored manifest and result when the points are gone) and trip edits through the outbox. The shift turns "on server" when `end` is accepted (as on the first upload), and its row goes. When none is left the notice goes and eviction may run again.
  - **Nothing goes out early.** The outbox runner holds every item of a shift not yet created again, so an odometer or `end` queued before the restore is not refused (404) and parked.
  - **Server ids.** The server's shift ids are `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, so after a restore it hands out ids again from where the backup stopped. The phone clears the server ids of those shifts and their trips when it sees the restore; the answers set them anew. Without this the second shift sent again failed on `UNIQUE(server_id)` (found by the tests).
  - **Cases.** A shift the server kept: 200 unchanged, points come back as duplicates, `end` answers the stored result; nothing doubled. Partial success: each accepted batch is stored, the next sync goes on from there. Offline: everything stays; sent when the server answers. A second restore while sending: each row is written only if unchanged since read, so the newer restore wins and the run starts over (at most 3 times per run). A running shift: sent again first; ended shifts wait until it ends (the server allows one active shift, and an old shift is active there from create to end). A shift deleted on the web meanwhile: dropped (8.4). A shift known only from the server feed: not sent, back to "on server".
  - `docs/if-your-phone-is-lost.md`: the restore section now describes what is built.
- Standards checked: 15 sections 9.3 (the procedure and the wording), 3.2 (eviction blocked until acknowledged again), 6.1 and 6.2 (create, points in seq order, `end` with manifest and result behind the barrier, oldest first, the running shift first), 4.1 ("on server" only after `end` is acknowledged), 7 and 8.4 (server wins on deletes); 08 section 2.2 (the 14-day device window) and the server's `POST /shifts` (200 unchanged for a known `client_id`, 409 `shift_already_active`), `POST /telemetry/batch` (duplicates by `(device, seq)`, inserted 0 means no recompute), `POST /shifts/{ref}/end` (an ended shift answers its stored result); optimistic concurrency by compare-and-set on the stored value (no locks across awaits).
- Tests: `test/resend.test.ts` 8 new, against a fake server that can be backed up and restored and carries `X-MileWage-Instance` / `X-MileWage-Restore-Gen` on every answer: a restore from before two shifts (both back, all points, odometer, `end` with manifest and result, "on server", notice gone, eviction free); a kept shift not doubled (400 duplicates, 2 shifts); a 503 on the second batch (offline, row at seq 500, `end` not sent, next sync sends only the other 700); offline with an app restart (rows kept, eviction blocked before they are read, notice back); a second restore mid-shift (one drain, both back); the running shift first and the ended one after it ends (no 409); a feed-only shift not sent; queued odometer and `end` held, never parked (this one fails with the hold removed). Neighbours run: status, drain, awayhints, summary, upload, pull, outbox, edits, shift, storage — 118 passed. `npm run typecheck:phone` clean; eslint and prettier clean on the changed files.
- Size: 476 source lines (new module) + about 90 changed in 4 files; 428 test lines.
- Not done:
  - Not checked on a phone: a real restore against the dev server with the S24 (the owner tests). Native buffer behaviour is unchanged: points are re-read with `readByShift` and stay as they are; no Kotlin change.
  - Shift notes edited after the backup are sent only in the create of a shift the server lost; a kept shift keeps the server's notes (the phone row's `updated_at` is overwritten at end, so a notes patch could not be ordered fairly).
  - Points older than 14 days cannot be sent (15 section 9.3 says so). Their shifts still go; the server then computes from fewer or no points.
  - The general backlog has the same one-active-shift clash (an offline-ended shift drained while a new one runs is parked); not changed here.
- Assumption (owner to confirm):
  - Shifts known only from the server feed (another phone, or before a reinstall: no manifest, no own result, no points, nothing queued) are put back to "on server" and not sent. 15 section 9.3 says "every `on_server` row"; sending a bare `end` would make the restored server count them from no points and the phone would then show that. Otherwise they would stay "on phone only" for good, with reminders.
  - A shift the restored server kept and that started more than 13 days ago is taken as on the server and not touched: the device API cannot reach it any more (08 section 2.2).
  - A shift whose points are gone is sent with the manifest and result stored at its end, so the server reports `differs` and the phone keeps its numbers in the diff log. The spec does not say what the totals should be then.

## PR Z6 — Web: Show more on Expenses and Notifications; Import lists every problem

- Done:
  - Expenses and Notifications show 50 rows, then a "Show more" button (shadcn outline button) at the end of the list. It adds the next 50, says "Loading" while it reads, and goes away when the server has no more. A filter on Expenses starts again at 50 rows, and Show more keeps the filter.
  - The server already had keyset paging on `GET /expenses` (cursor = base64url `[spent_on, id]`) and `GET /notifications` (cursor = base64url id), with tests. No server change was needed.
  - New `apps/web/src/api/paged.ts` (`usePagedList`): one query that follows `cursor` / `next_cursor` itself, the way the Shifts week does. Its key is `[path, params, { pages }]`, so `invalidate('/expenses')` or `invalidate('/notifications')` still reads every shown row again after a change. It asks for up to 200 rows per request (the routes' `limit` maximum), so N pages take ceil(N×50/200) requests. New `apps/web/src/ui/ShowMore.tsx` is the button.
  - Expenses: the year tiles come from `GET /expenses/totals` on the server, so paging does not change them. The "most used" category chips (`quickCategories`) are counted over the rows loaded so far, which is now every page shown, not just the first 50.
  - Import: the preview listed problems only from its first 200 rows (`rows`, server), and the web showed only the first 10 of those. The preview now also returns `problems`: every problem row in file order, up to 1000 (`summary.error` is still the full count). The web shows 10 of them and a "Show all problems" / "Show fewer" toggle. When the server stops at 1000, a line says "And N more rows with problems, not listed. Fix these first, then check again." `openapi.yaml` `ImportPreview.problems` was added and the types were regenerated.
- Standards checked: keyset (cursor) paging with a "load more" button (the route contract was already in place); the TanStack Query cache key and invalidation that the app already uses (no `useInfiniteQuery` helper exists, so I followed the Shifts loop); `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls` on the toggle that shows the rest of the list.
- Tests: `npx vitest run apps/web/test/expenses.test.tsx` (17 passed), `apps/web/test/notifications.test.tsx` (5), `apps/web/test/import.test.tsx` (6), `apps/server/test/imports.test.ts` (11), plus `apps/server/test/matrix.test.ts`, `expenses.test.ts`, `notifications.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/dashboard-extras.test.tsx`, `shell.test.tsx` and `layout.test.tsx` (all passed). `tsc --noEmit` passed for the web and the root (server) projects. eslint and prettier are clean on the changed files. `openapi:generate` and `openapi:check` passed.
- Not done: "Show more" asks for the shown rows again from the start (one request per 200 rows) instead of only the next page, because no infinite-query helper exists. The commit result's `error_rows` stays capped at 50; the web does not show it.
- Assumption (owner to confirm): a preview lists at most 1000 problem rows. A 5 MB file can hold about 100 000 rows, and that many problems means the column mapping is wrong. On Expenses, "most used" is counted over the rows on screen, so a category filter changes it. That was already true before this change.

## PR Z7 — Web: edit a goal; shift start times by hour on Analytics

- **Done.**
  - Goals: the row menu has **Edit**. It fills the add form (heading "Edit goal", button "Save changes", Cancel) and saves with `PATCH /goals/{id}` sending `label`, `metric`, `period`, `target`. The target goes back into the field as typed (`targetInput`, the inverse of `targetOf`: money through `moneyInput` in the settings currency, minutes as hours, metres as km or mi). Saving uses the same checks and minor-unit conversion as adding. The name is trimmed. Cancel, or a finished save, puts the form back on the add defaults. Deleting the goal being edited ends the edit.
  - Server: `PATCH /goals/{id}` trims the name the way `POST` does. The API already accepted `target`, `metric` and `period`, so `openapi.yaml` is unchanged.
  - Analytics: a **Shift start times** section under Work rhythm draws `rhythm.by_start_hour[].shifts` as 24 CSS columns (`apps/web/src/ui/HourBars.tsx`, no chart library). Bars use the `chart-1` token, are at most 24px wide, have a 4px rounded top and a square base, with 2px gaps and a hairline baseline. Only the busiest hour has a number on it. Ticks are every six hours and follow the 12h/24h setting. Hovering a column shows "3 shifts at 17:00" above the chart. The numbers are in a table with a caption: a screen reader always gets it (the columns are `aria-hidden`), and "Show as a table" shows it to everyone. With no shifts there is an empty state instead of the chart.
- **Standards checked.** The goals route and its OpenAPI `GoalPatch` already take every field. Edit works the way Expenses edits (same form, PATCH, Cancel). Rules from the dataviz skill: one series so no legend, labels only where they matter, text in text tokens, a table view, hover never the only way to read a value. Validator result: the brand yellow on a white card is 1.62:1, below 3:1, so the visible table and the number on the busiest bar are what make the chart readable. On the dark card it passes.
- **Tests.** `apps/web/test/goals.test.tsx` (9): edit pre-fills the form, sends the right PATCH body (target 600 to 60000 minor units, trimmed name, new period) and shows the new target. Cancel and delete-while-editing are covered, and so is `targetInput`. `apps/web/test/analytics.test.tsx` (9): 24 columns, heights, labels, ticks, the hover readout, the table (25 rows), the table toggle, the 12h labels and both empty states. `apps/server/test/goals.test.ts` (8): a PATCH that changes label, metric, period and target together, with the name trimmed. Web and root `tsc --noEmit`, eslint and prettier pass on the changed files.
- **Not done.**
  - After choosing Edit, focus goes back to the "..." button, not the form. Expenses works the same way. A fix belongs in the shared `RowMenu` (`onCloseAutoFocus`) and is outside this PR.
  - There are no y-axis ticks. The busiest bar is labelled and every value is in the hover readout and the table.
  - The server still accepts a name made only of spaces, which then saves as an empty name. The web form blocks it.
- **Assumption (owner to confirm).** Chart bars use `--chart-1` (the same yellow as the brand). On light it is 1.62:1 against the card. A darker light-theme step for `--chart-1` in `index.css` would bring it above 3:1. That is the owner's palette choice.
- Follow-up by the coordinating session: the light theme's `--chart-1` is `#b88600` (3.3:1 on a white card; the brand yellow was 1.6:1, below the 3:1 a chart mark needs). Dark mode keeps `#ffc21f`. Only `HourBars` uses the token. Assumption (owner to confirm): a darker amber for chart marks in light mode is acceptable within the brand palette.
